SK Telecom is an SK Group affiliate, so a 2GW build specified on Vera Rubin + SK hynix HBM is a captive-ish HBM sink that does not depend on winning allocation against [[SAMSUNG]] or [[MU]]. ⚠️ Bound it honestly, though: "up to 2GW" with NO timeline, against a first stage of only 100MW for a third-party tenant (AWS) and a site cap of 1GW — the 2GW figure and the 1GW site cap are not obviously the same scope, and the only dated, tenant-committed number in the whole item is 100MW in late 2027. Treat 2GW as an ambition, 100MW as the fact. Cross-ref [[NVDA]], [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md).**
- ⚠️ **The same note's Korea cluster mention — QUALITATIVE CONTENT ADOPTED, KRW MAGNITUDES QUARANTINED (MS "AI Sovereignty", 2026-08-19).** Korea's **"three mega investment projects"** — described as the largest domestic investment programme by any Korean administration to date — include **semiconductor clusters from both [[SAMSUNG]] and SK hynix**, and the note states **SK hynix plans to build an AI memory production network across the Yongin Cluster, Cheongju, and a NEW CLUSTER IN THE SOUTHWESTERN REGION, aiming to complete a fourth [fab/line]. That much is carried as fact and it corroborates the cluster geography already on this page.** 🔴 **THE KRW FIGURES ARE NOT. The text layer of that two-column exhibit extracted as "SK Hynix plans to invest KRW 1,100 trillion" and "Samsung … approximately KRW 2,450 trillion by December 2040" — roughly an order of magnitude above these companies' actual capex run-rates, and pdfplumber demonstrably interleaved columns elsewhere in the same note. Marked EXTRACTION-UNCERTAIN, NEEDS VERIFICATION AGAINST THE ORIGINAL EXHIBIT; not asserted as fact.** ✅ **Partial corroboration worth recording rather than hiding: the KRW1,100tn figure MATCHES the number this page already carries from the same house — MS · Kathleen Oh, "South Korea: A Landmark Industrial Strategy", 2026-07-01 — which decomposes it as KRW600tn Yongin + KRW100tn Chungju + KRW400tn southwestern cluster (summing exactly to 1,100) and dates the 4th Yongin fab to 2033, accelerated 12 years. So SK hynix's own figure survives a same-house cross-check on a cleanly-extracted note; the Samsung KRW2,450tn / Dec-2040 figure has no such corroboration here and is NOT carried.** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/USELECTION_20260819_1049.html)

- **ADR-premium / flow-donor read + 4Q DRAM ASP check (Jefferies (sales) · Rankie Wong, 2026-07-09).** Jefferies sales say investors are comfortable with a **15-20% SKHY ADR premium** (the TSM ADR comp now ~13%, 10-yr avg 10-15% — above MS S&T's ~5-10% estimate on the page); post-correction **MU trades <5x P/E on buyside $200 EPS — roughly on par with Hynix**, so **MU is seen as the likely flow donor once SKHY lists**, with Samsung (MSD P/E) a catch-up candidate. Latest checks (Sherman/Edison) point to **4Q DRAM ASP +30-40% q/q, higher than most expect**; memory called "the most crowded trade globally." (mixed — bullish ASP/flow vs crowded-positioning caveat) (Jefferies · Rankie Wong, 2026-07-09).
- **Bear counterpoint on output growth + leverage (Tim Culpan / Culpium, 2026-07-08).** Culpan flags SK Hynix's **bit-shipment CAGR at only ~11% since 2020** — lagging Samsung on output growth over the past two AI years — and a **highly levered balance sheet** (contrast the page's net-cash W35tn Q1-2026 framing); a bear read on whether the HBM-mix lead has come at the expense of overall bit-output competitiveness. (bear) (Tim Culpan / Culpium, 2026-07-08).
- **ADS roadshow — LTA strategy is margin-not-floor, and the structural case for a higher P/E (SK Hynix ADS roadshow, 2026-07-06).** On the ADS roadshow management drew an explicit contrast with Micron: asked whether it would adopt Micron's floor-price LTAs, mgmt said "my main focus is margin… rather than the pricing arrangement," structuring each customer's LTA differently. The re-rating logic laid out directly: the new LTAs run **3-5 years with penalty provisions** (vs the old ~1-yr "gentleman's agreements" that were unenforceable — "you cannot sue basically") → lower demand/supply-shock volatility → a higher P/E deserved; a higher HBM mix (annual contracts vs quarterly/monthly commodity pricing) reinforces the lower-volatility case. **>50% of the 2028-30 capacity expansion will be LTA-covered** (its major LTA customers are >50% of revenue), with **LTA signing expected before year-end 2026**. On capacity, total wafer capacity is guided to **~2x by 2030, but customer demand for 2030 is "a lot bigger" than that potential supply**. Management **pushed back on any committed HBM-to-DDR margin parity** ("to my best knowledge, we haven't committed anything") — the current strong market is only a positive backdrop for next year's HBM price negotiation, framed around preserving core-customer relationships. On China, **CXMT has ~20% of DRAM wafer capacity but only ~6-7% of bits** (low yield → worse quality, usable only for low-end consumer), and China memory impact has been "minimal"; YMTC's expansion has repeatedly under-executed on Western-tool access. **ASIC share of HBM is "going up a little" but the majority of HBM still goes into GPU.** Bull on the durability/re-rating leg (SK Hynix ADS roadshow, 2026-07-06). [../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_06_sk_hynix_roadshow.html](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_06_sk_hynix_roadshow.html)
- **Chairman Chey Tae-won — the strategic case: capacity-doubling, partnership moat, and "memory-as-a-service" (Bloomberg TV — Chairman Chey Tae-won interview, 2026-06-10).** Around the ADR debut (priced **$149/ADR** vs an indicated **$177**, raising **$26.5bn**), SK Group Chairman Chey framed the AI memory cycle as a structural break from the old boom-bust: the LTAs were **proposed by customers, not SK Hynix** ("not SK… they actually require that"), so "it's not cyclical business anymore." SK Hynix has announced it will **double total capacity within five years**, but customers "said that's not enough… they want us to quadruple, five times, six times." Supply tightness lasts **until AI "settles" with human society** (no fixed date); the demand cycle is now driven by agentic-AI KV-cache, not device-unit counts. On the "AI bubble": **the technology is real (no bubble); only the stock market may overshoot**. Two durable moats: SK Hynix **works with TSMC on the HBM base die "which the others do not do,"** and **"doesn't compete with any customers"** — both enabling deeper partnerships. Also: a new **US fab in Indiana**; **SK Group ~$1T AI-datacenter investment over 10 years (~15GW in Korea + ~5GW outside Korea)**, with **~$35B already invested in the US** (bio, battery, Hynix, SKT AI startups); and a longer-term **"memory-as-a-service"** concept (memory delivered as a managed solution rather than just product). Bull/strategic framing (Bloomberg TV — Chairman Chey Tae-won interview, 2026-06-10). [../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_10_ceo_hynix_interview_10jun26.html](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_10_ceo_hynix_interview_10jun26.html)
- **JPM server-CPU model — rising DIMM/SOCAMM demand a fresh read-through for SK Hynix (JPMorgan · Albert Hung / Gokul Hariharan, 2026-07-12).** JPM's new proprietary server-CPU shipment model forecasts total server CPUs from **26mn units 2025 → 68mn 2028 (38% CAGR)**, with overall server shipments sustaining **20%+ growth**, driven by AI head-node CPUs (AI-accelerator shipments ~50% CAGR FY25-28) plus a **155%-CAGR "agentic-AI" server-CPU** vector (inference orchestration / test-time scaling). JPM frames the agentic-AI server "super-cycle" as **positive for memory vendors SK Hynix and Samsung Electronics on rising DIMM/SOCAMM demand** (SK hynix OW, W2,221,000 as of 10-Jul; no new PT in this note). Corroborates the server-DRAM/SOCAMM demand leg already on the page (NVDA Vera CPU, LPDDR5/SOCAMM pull). Bull read-through (JPMorgan · Albert Hung / Gokul Hariharan, "server CPU shipment model", 2026-07-12). [../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/JPM_Server_JPM_proprieta_2026-07-12_5362742.html](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/JPM_Server_JPM_proprieta_2026-07-12_5362742.html)
- **Mirae Asset maintains Buy, TP W4,200,000 — "take a broader view," pullback = attractive entry (Mirae Asset · Young-gun Kim "SK Hynix — Take a broader view", 2026-07-14).** Mirae **maintains Buy and TP W4,200,000** (unchanged, last raised 06-25; current W1,845,000 on 7/13 → **127.6% upside**) — ties Jefferies (07-06) as the **street-high TP on the page** — arguing the recent ~14% 1-month pullback reflects a "sharp unwinding" of 2Q26/ADR-listing optimism that is "already priced in." **It CUT earnings forecasts ~12%:** 2Q26F OP to W62.3tr (from W70.7tr); **2026F OP W266,817bn (from Mirae's prior W299.1tr) vs consensus W275,793bn**; **2027F OP W388,855bn (+46% YoY)**; 2028F OP W400,069bn; OP margin **76.8%/76.6%/73.5%** (26/27/28F); revenue W347.5tr/W507.9tr/W544.1tr; **EPS 26F W295,026 (+400.4% growth), P/E 6.3x**; capex **W56.9tr/W79.7tr** (26/27F, 2026 unchanged). LTAs cover **~50% of revenue** (earnings-stability + less speculative-demand exposure). Industry backdrop "exceptionally tight": **DRAM 16Gb DDR5 & DDR4 spot up 40+ consecutive trading days**, near prior highs even with the CXMT-listing overhang; 2027 growth driven by HBM lifting overall pricing as LTA/HBM-committed capacity crowds out conventional DRAM. TP = **6.5x target P/B** (10% discount to Micron/Kioxia pure-play avg) on 12MF BPS W583,213. Bull signal (Mirae Asset · Young-gun Kim, 2026-07-14). _Divergence: the W4.2m Mirae/Jefferies street-high sits far above JPM OW **W2,221,000** (07-10), Bernstein W3,300,000 (07-06), UBS W3,200,000 (07-03), MS W2,600,000 (07-02), BofA W3,590,000 (06-22) — an unusually wide ~W2m PT dispersion across the desk._ [../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/20260714_SK_Hynix_000660_KS_Buy.html](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/20260714_SK_Hynix_000660_KS_Buy.html)
- **BofA rebuts the 2Q ASP-miss fear — "DRAM ASPs look better than concerns" (BofA · Simon Woo, 2026-07-13).** Into the sharp mid-July memory selloff (triggered by the KIS/local 2Q26 OP-preview ~8% below the W65tn consensus), BofA argues the correction is **overdone**: conventional DRAM ASP is **+50-60% QoQ**, Korea semiconductor exports **+193% YoY in the first 10 days of July**, and DRAM spot has rallied since June. BofA assumes global DRAM ASP **+53%/+21%/+7% QoQ for 2Q/3Q/4Q26**, and frames **Korea's lower 2Q ASP (~low-to-mid 40% QoQ) as 2Q-specific** — a product of stable HBM contract prices plus an exceptionally high 1Q ASP base — **not** the start of a 3Q deceleration. A direct counter to the SK Hynix ASP-miss narrative (higher HBM mix → lower blended ASP hike) behind the plunge. Bull signal (BofA · Simon Woo, 2026-07-13).
- **Record Seoul plunge then a same-week rebound to near all-time highs — a flow/ETF whipsaw, not a fundamentals re-rate (Bloomberg / @citrini / Barron's / @firstadopter, 2026-07-13→14).** Days after the record-setting Nasdaq debut, SK Hynix posted its **biggest drop on record in Seoul** on the KIS/local 2Q26 OP-preview miss ("SK Hynix Shares Plunge Most on Record in Deepening Korea Selloff," Bloomberg via Outlook 2026-07-13; @zerohedge -4.5% 07-14; @citrini flagged **W1,850 as the technical line** below which leveraged Korean retail gets "wiped out"). It then **rebounded ~+27% the next session to approach all-time highs as the US ADR premium kicked in** (The_AI_Investor / @firstadopter / Barron's, 07-14). @JulianKlymochko: "$SKHY is a $1 trillion company trading like a meme stonk" — **3.8x next year's earnings, +493% over 12 months**, "the most contrarian long in the market" (07-13/14). @jimcramer: "How did all of tech become hostage to SK Hynix!" — leverage to "ridiculous leveraged ETFs" (07-14). (valuation, bear→mixed — sentiment/technical washout on the ASP-miss headline, reversed as ADR-premium/flow reasserted.)
- **Price up, content down — the 2027 HBM-pricing call and the offsetting de-spec, sourced from the buyer's own BOM (SemiAnalysis "Can AMD break the CUDA Moat? AMD Advancing AI 2026", 2026-07-24).** The bull leg is unusually well-placed because it comes from the *accelerator vendor's* bill of materials rather than a memory maker: SemiAnalysis explicitly expects "**a significant increase in HBM pricing in 2027 as memory suppliers move to match the margins they now earn on commodity DRAM**" — commodity-DRAM margin is now the *anchor* HBM is being repriced toward, which is the same inversion this page tracks from the other side (Redburn: HBM 2027 revised down 60→50bn Gb-eq because conventional DRAM is more profitable; roadshow 07-06: management explicitly **refused to commit** to HBM-to-DDR margin parity). And it is modelled as strong enough to **compress the accelerator vendors themselves**: AMD's ASPs must rise but it cannot pass the full memory increase through, because "most of AMD's volume goes to large buyers with real negotiating leverage, and those buyers have more alternatives than they used to," so HBM cost passes through "**at a lower incremental margin than their corporate margins, dragging down gross margins on the MI455X package.**" That is a third-party estimate of HBM pricing power strong enough to move a customer's gross margin — the most direct external support yet for the page's "next year's HBM price negotiation" framing. Baseline: "**HBM, DRAM, and NAND prices alike have increased dramatically since our baseline in mid-2025**" (SemiAnalysis Memory Model / Accelerator and HBM Model). **The offsetting bear leg is in the same note and is the reason price × prior content is the wrong revenue model.** Tight supply is already destroying attach: the original 2025 MI455X BOM estimate carried "a staggering **138TB in DRAM across LPDDR5X and DDR5 MRDIMM**"; the current estimate has **LPDDR5X entirely removed** plus a **significant DDR5 de-spec to a far lower DDR5 RDIMM content** — specifically the roadmapped **up to 1TB of direct-attached LPDDR per MI455X accelerator on the EAM module has disappeared**, attributed directly to tight memory supply. SemiAnalysis's summary is the line to carry: "**much of the DRAM price increases are offset by aggressive de-specs.**" Net for SK hynix: **price-led revenue with content as a live offset** — bullish on ASP durability into 2027, but the bits-per-accelerator assumption underneath consensus volume is now the exposed variable. (SemiAnalysis "Can AMD break the CUDA Moat? AMD Advancing AI 2026", 2026-07-24) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_24_ENC_Can_AMD_break_the_CUDA_Moat_AMD_Advancing_AI_2026_email.html)
- **Jefferies reiterates BUY on both Korean memory names (Jefferies (Silk Road) · Cristina Titu, 2026-07-15).** Samsung and SK Hynix both rated **BUY** in the Silk Road note — consistent with the standing Jefferies bull stance (W4.2m TP, 07-06). Bull signal (Jefferies · Cristina Titu, 2026-07-15).
- **🆕 ARCHIVE BACKFILL — DAMNANG (independent Korean-semis Substack, launched 2026-03-15, ~9k subscribers, PAID). ⚠️ NOT A BROKER: no rating, no price target, no house model. These pieces are 2-5 MONTHS OLD, are recorded as HISTORICAL FRAMEWORK ONLY, and NONE supersedes any current number, rating or thesis above.** ⚠️ **DELIBERATELY EXCLUDED: the same author's “HBM Density Peak” (2026-08-20) — ALREADY FULLY INGESTED on this page on 2026-08-21 (`## Current state` CPO block, the HBM4 Sinal cell and the 08-20 Full-log row). It is NOT re-logged here.**
  - **HBF, dismantled from the SK hynix researchers' own paper — the most rigorous HBF scepticism on this page, and it comes from a source that is otherwise structurally bullish memory** (**DAMNANG (Substack)** — “HBF's Limits and Reality: Between Promise and Structural Obstacles (H³ Paper Review)”, **2026-03-18**). The promise being tested: **stack NAND with TSVs like HBM for ~16x HBM capacity (~3TB) at equivalent ~8TB/s bandwidth, at ~1/5 the cost of HBM**, SK hynix and SanDisk targeting **sampling in late 2026**. The H³ architecture in the paper is explicitly HYBRID (HBM + HBF), because the author's core objection is physical and unarguable: **NAND carries a 1-2 ORDER-OF-MAGNITUDE latency disadvantage that cannot be architected away**, and the paper's mitigation — **a 40MB SRAM buffer plus tens of GB of DRAM plus a complex FTL controller** — *“makes the total system anything but cheap.”* Second objection: **the paper assumes pure READ-ONLY workloads and deterministic access patterns**, which he calls *“extremely limited in actual production.”* Open items he lists: production yield, thermal management in a GPU environment, NAND read-disturb/endurance under those conditions. **His conclusion is a COMPLEMENT, not a substitute: “a niche solution effective in highly specialized CAG workloads… a complement targeting ‘the gap between HBM and SSD,’ NOT an HBM replacement.”** ➤ **Independently corroborated five months later from the commercial side by Micron's own management (Mizuho, 2026-08-09): HBF substituting HBM “unlikely… but will see adoption in the memory stack.” Two sources, one physical and one commercial, same destination — which is the reason to price HBF as a tier, not as an option on HBM's TAM.** The strategic framing is worth keeping regardless of the product: *“SK hynix dominates DRAM and HBM, while SanDisk dominates NAND. Their collaboration is… the memory industry's effort to transition from COMMODITY business to PLATFORM business.”* [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026-03-19_-_HBFs_Limits_and_Reality-_Between_Promise_and_Structural_Obstacles_H%C2%B3_Paper_Review.html)
  - **“Memory is no longer a cyclical industry” — the framing this page's structural leg rests on, sourced to an SK hynix executive** (**DAMNANG (Substack)** — “Memory is no longer a cyclical industry”, **2026-03-25**, written up from a ~20-minute seminar by **SK hynix's North America regional president** at the Silicon Valley K-ASIC Seminar on 2026-03-24, plus a 1-on-1 afterwards). The argument: the classic glut→price-collapse→investment-cut→shortage loop no longer runs because **all three players “paid for it” in the last cycle and have shifted priority from volume share to “profitability, capital efficiency, and customer mix”**; with HBM, allocation decisions are margin decisions, so **LTAs are “a signal that memory companies are managing supply far more STRATEGICALLY than before”** rather than merely locking volume. ⚠️ **THE AUTHOR'S OWN DISCLOSURE, CARRIED: “given that the underlying perspective comes from someone inside the memory industry, the content may lean BULLISH on memory.”** ⚠️ **AND ONE PASSAGE IS EXPLICITLY LABELLED SPECULATION BY THE AUTHOR AND MUST NOT BE READ AS COMPANY GUIDANCE: asked about an ADR, the executive deflected in public and, privately, answered with a joke — “just buy Micron.” The author then builds an unsupported thesis that [[MU]] has carried a structural premium as the only large-cap memory name accessible to US investors and that an SK hynix ADR would erode it. He calls this “entertainment, not signal.” RECORDED AS SUCH.** ➤ Cross-read to the 08-25 UBS line above, which is the company's first on-the-record ADR framing: regulatory approval to work through, liquidity to build over time — consistent with “not near-term.” [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026-03-25_-_Memory_is_no_longer_a_cyclical_industry.html)
  - **The captive-ratio framework — the cleanest statement of WHY this cycle's margins might hold, and of what would break them** (**DAMNANG (Substack)** — “Will the Memory Stock Rally Keep Going?”, **2026-05-11**). Opening marks (historical): **SK hynix posted a 72% operating margin in a single quarter; [[MU]] was locking 3-to-5-year supply agreements; [[SAMSUNG]] had a quarter where conventional-DRAM profitability EXCEEDED HBM.** Demand backdrop cited: **Big Tech 4 (Google/Microsoft/Meta/Amazon) 2026 capex ~$725bn, +~77% y/y**; **[[NVDA]] ~$68bn quarterly revenue guiding ~$78bn**. The argument: *“what matters in this memory cycle is not peak margin itself. It is HOW MANY QUARTERS this margin can hold. In the past, supply gluts answered that question. This time, THE CAPTIVE RATIO answers it — and the tools to lower the captive ratio are still unproven.”* His three-item quarterly dashboard is a genuinely usable checklist for this page: **(1) the pace at which platform owners deploy ‘bypass’ tools in production; (2) memory-vendor progress converting cHBM / HBF / PIM into revenue; (3) whether the MEMORY SHARE WITHIN hyperscaler capex keeps rising.** ➤ Read against 2026-08: item (1) is exactly what the de-spec debate is measuring, and item (2) is what the CMM-Ax/custom-base-die work above is. [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026-05-12_-_Will_the_Memory_Stock_Rally_Keep_Going-.html)
  - **CXMT — the SK-hynix-specific conclusion, and the double-edged sword inside it** (**DAMNANG (Substack)** — “Is CXMT a Threat or an Illusion?”, **2026-04-11**; the author cites separate conversations with **SK hynix's Americas head** and an **ASML employee**). *“SK hynix has the STRONGEST DEFENSIVE POSITION for now, thanks to HBM”* — even if CXMT produces HBM3, SK hynix competes at HBM4 with TSMC base-die collaboration locked in, and its commodity book has been restructured toward higher-value product, so CXMT's commodity expansion hits [[SAMSUNG]] harder. 🔴 **THE WARNING IS THE PART TO KEEP: “the variable is the 3D DRAM transition… THE STRONGER THE HBM MOAT, THE LOWER THE PERCEIVED URGENCY TO INVEST IN THE 3D DRAM TRANSITION, and that could become a double-edged sword over the long run” — with the Americas head himself described as taking the speed of China's 3D DRAM pursuit seriously.** Scale context: **CXMT went from ~40kwpm in 2020 to ~720,000 wafers per QUARTER by end-2025 across three 12-inch fabs, reaching a ~16nm G4 node (TechInsights) on DUV multi-patterning with NO EUV** — a level the Big 3 reached in 2018-19. ➤ **This is a DIFFERENT piece from the 2026-07-27 “After CXMT's Listing” note already on this page and it argues a different thing: that one tests the price-war claims, this one identifies the technology transition where the rules could change. Both retained.** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026-04-11_-_Is_CXMT_a_Threat_or_an_Illusion-.html)
  - **Hybrid bonding — SK hynix is the CONSERVATIVE one, and that is a position with a shelf life** (**DAMNANG (Substack)** — “Hybrid Bonding, Fully Dissected”, **2026-03-28**, based on the author's interviews with engineers at SK hynix, Samsung and Micron). **“SK Hynix appears to be the MOST CONSERVATIVE of the three”: 12-layer HBM4 development and MP-readiness completed on its existing MR-MUF approach, 16-Hi HBM4 showcased, hybrid bonding targeted at HBM4E.** Against that, **“Samsung is the most aggressive,” signalling adoption at 16-layer HBM4E with SEMES having produced working 16-layer hybrid-bonded HBM samples — the author's read being that hybrid bonding is “a RESET OF THE GAME BOARD” for a company that could not catch SK hynix on TC/NCF bonding. [[MU]] has disclosed no timeline and is “most likely to be last.”** Timing mechanics: **JEDEC relaxing HBM4 package thickness from 720μm to 775μm let 16-layer HBM4 be built with conventional TC bonding and pushed hybrid bonding out one generation; from HBM4E at 20 layers, TC bonding's “economics and technical feasibility deteriorate sharply.”** ⚠️ **The engineers' own caveat, carried: they target HBM4E for initial adoption but “given the cost challenges, it may realistically not happen at scale until HBM5” — while also saying “technically, they can no longer afford to delay.”** ➤ **The relevance in August 2026 is that Mizuho/SemiAnalysis now call 16-20Hi HBM AND hybrid bonding “uncertain for future gens” — which, if right, EXTENDS the life of SK hynix's MR-MUF advantage rather than shortening it. That is the under-discussed second-order read of the de-spec debate for this name.** (From the same author's “Why HBM Is So Hard to Make”, **2026-03-20**: the current split is **SK hynix on MR-MUF — best thermal performance, most production-proven — vs Samsung on NCF + thermocompression, better precision at fine pitch**; HBM4 at 16 layers pushes bump pitch to ~16-18μm from ~25μm, with **no bump-to-bump redundancy in the HBM PHY signal path**.) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026-03-29_-_Hybrid_Bonding_Fully_Dissected.html)
  - **Two smaller archive marks.** (i) **The base-die asymmetry, stated plainly from the Samsung side** (**DAMNANG (Substack)** — “How Samsung Overtakes SK hynix in HBM4 — An ISSCC 2026 Paper Review”, **2026-03-20**): Samsung's HBM4 moves the base die to its own **4nm FinFET logic** process while jumping the core die 1a→1c, and the author's structural point is that this *“only makes sense if you have memory and foundry under the same roof — SK Hynix would need to SOURCE THAT LOGIC PROCESS EXTERNALLY (e.g. from TSMC)”*, with the offsetting note that **“SK Hynix will almost certainly pursue its own logic base die strategy through its partnership with TSMC.”** ⚠️ Historical; the TSMC base-die collaboration is already established on this page from better sources. (ii) **SOCAMM2 and the NAND leg** (**DAMNANG (Substack)** — “Vera Rubin's DRAM Cut in Half”, **2026-06-08** — ⚠️ **the ingest router mislabelled this piece “SemiAnalysis”; it is DAMNANG, discussing a SemiAnalysis report**): **SK hynix announced 192GB SOCAMM2 mass production** (as did Micron), which is the author's reason for treating the reported 96GB Vera Rubin standard build as *“a supply chain account, not an official confirmation”*; and **SK hynix's own 1Q results said AI is growing NAND demand ALONGSIDE DRAM, not just DRAM.** Framework worth carrying: he splits inference state into **CAPTIVE (must stay in HBM), ABSORBED (LPDDR/SOCAMM), and LEAKED (storage)** — the same taxonomy the 2026-08 de-spec debate is implicitly arguing over. [Sources](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026-03-20_-_How_Samsung_Overtakes_SK_hynix_in_HBM4-_An_ISSCC_2026_Paper_Review.html) · [Vera Rubin](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026-06-08_-_Vera_Rubins_DRAM_Cut_in_Half-_Is_the_Memory_Cycle_Really_Over-.html)
  - **NAND layer-count standing, as of March 2026 — historical, kept only as the baseline the Dalian 240L upgrade is measured against** (**DAMNANG (Substack)** — “Is SanDisk Done?”, **2026-03-26**): **SK hynix in mass production at 321-layer QLC — the highest layer count of the group** (Samsung V9 ~290L-class TLC; [[MU]] 276L G9 QLC; [[SNDK]]/[[KIOXIA]] BiCS8 at 218-232L). ⚠️ **The author's caveat cuts against SK hynix and is carried: “SK hynix at 321 layers is APPROACHING THE LIMITS of how much additional efficiency it can extract, while SanDisk/Kioxia still have steps left to climb.”** Also in that piece and relevant here: **Solidigm (SK hynix subsidiary) took a $500M / 2.0% stake in Nanya Technology at NT$223.9 alongside SanDisk ($1B / 3.9%), Kioxia ($500M / 2%) and Cisco ($500M / 2%) in a single ~$2.5bn placement, each SSD maker signing a separate multi-year DRAM supply agreement** — three SSD makers locking DRAM sourcing simultaneously, which the author reads as the industry conceding a **DRAM (FTL cache) bottleneck** in enterprise SSD. [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026-03-26_-_Is_SanDisk_Done-.html)


## Catalysts / what to watch
- **🆕🔴🔴 1Q27 (BofA/DB) — YONGIN FAB 1's FIRST CLEANROOM ENTERS HVM, AND THE DATE IS NOW TRIPLE-SOURCED.** Deutsche Bank: *"six cleanrooms; first cleanroom to start HVM in 1Q27; expected to add 360k WSPM by 1H30"* ("Global Memory: Chipping away at industry supply imbalance", 2026-09-16), matching the 09-15 DAMNANG mark (Y1 first cleanroom **February 2027**) and the semicap tool-in read already on this page. ➤ **This is the single largest capacity item in the company's plan and it is no longer a 2029-2030 story — the first bits are inside five months. The falsifiable test is whether the 1Q27 cleanroom actually produces, and at what node.**
- **🆕🔴 2H26 (vs the normal late-January cadence) — AN EARLIER OR LARGER SPECIAL DIVIDEND.** BofA names this explicitly as an upside risk in its SK Square valuation: *"Hynix's earlier/stronger-than-expected special dividend announcements in 2H26 vs normally late-Jan"* ("Korea super-cycle continues into 2030", 2026-09-18). ➤ **A dated, falsifiable capital-return item that sits alongside — and could pre-empt — the 3Q26 shareholder-return update already on this page.**
- **🆕 JULY 2027 — YONGIN FAB 2 CONSTRUCTION START, CLEANROOM MID-2029, ~200k WSPM TOTAL (DBe).** The second-largest item in the build, and the one where slippage would show up first in the 2029-30 supply curve that DB says determines whether oversupply arrives at all (Deutsche Bank, 2026-09-16).
- **🆕⚠️ THE 2030 OVERSUPPLY DATE IS NOW A NAMED, MODELLED CALL — AND THE SWING FACTOR IS CHINA, NOT KOREA.** DB has DRAM demand/supply at **108% / 112% / 110% / 102% / 96%** for 2026-30 and raises **CXMT to 300k (2025) → 775k WSPM (2030), +475k — the largest single-vendor addition in its model, overtaking [[MU]]'s 760k by 2030** on accelerated Shanghai expansion plus a new Beijing fab. ➤ **Track CXMT's Shanghai and Beijing tool-ins as the leading indicator of whether the 2030 date pulls forward. DB's own hedge: "risk of oversupply can be mitigated by DRAM makers slowing the pace of tooling projects if needed."**
- **🆕🔴 DATED FLOW CATALYST — 2026-09-18 (ON THE CLOSE, FRIDAY): 000660 KS IS AN UPWEIGHT IN THE FTSE GEIS SEPTEMBER GLOBAL REBALANCE *AND* AN ADDITION TO THE ICE SEMICONDUCTOR INDEX (ICESEMI) IN ITS ANNUAL RECONSTITUTION.** Per **UBS Index Analytics · Yujue Wang — "UBS Index – September 2026 Global Quarterly Rebalance", 2026-09-06** ⚠️⚠️ **SALES DESK COMMENTARY, EXPLICITLY "NOT A PRODUCT OF UBS RESEARCH" — no rating, no PT, no house model, and this is a mechanical-flow item, NOT a fundamental view**: the September 2026 global rebalance totals **~US$228bn gross notional, effective on the close of Friday 2026-09-18**, and includes an **UPWEIGHT of 000660 KS in FTSE GEIS**. Separately, the **ICE semiconductor index (ICESEMI) annual reconstitution — ~US$12bn gross turnover — ADDS SKHY**, alongside **CBRS** and **TSEM**, and **DELETES RMBS, SWKS and NVMI**. ➤ **A dated, one-off, non-fundamental bid. Size it against the float before treating it as a driver; UBS gives the index-level notional, not the stock-level ticket, and this wiki has NOT computed one.** ⚠️ **CALENDAR COLLISION WORTH NOTING RATHER THAN CONFLATING: this page already carries "the September pricing-negotiation finalisation (UBS's own named catalyst, ~Sep 18)" in the `HBM4` Sinal cell. **Two completely different events land in the same window — a mechanical index flow and a fundamental HBM contract-price print. Do not read one as evidence of the other.**
- **🆕🔴🔴 THE RUBIN-GENERATION STACK-HEIGHT LADDER — A SPEC, NOT A FORECAST, AND THEREFORE SETTLEABLE.** As of 2026-09-06 this page carries **Rubin at 12-Hi HBM4** (Jefferies Asia TMT specialist SALES · Conor O'Mara, 09-06 — ⚠️ SALES DESK COMMENTARY, NOT RESEARCH, relaying TrendForce / ET News) **against UBS's own house view that NVIDIA already moved VR/R300 to HBM4 8-Hi** (UBS Korea Summit first read, 2026-08-25) — and **Rubin Ultra at 8-Hi HBM4, "around one-third fewer than 12-Hi in Rubin"** (Morgan Stanley · Charlie Chan et al., 09-06; ⚠️ MS's own wording is "Rubin Ultra **CPU**", ambiguous, not corrected here). **WATCH, IN PRIORITY ORDER: (1) an NVIDIA primary platform spec or a memory-maker qualification disclosure fixing the Rubin and Rubin Ultra stack heights; (2) the END-SEPTEMBER 2026 HBM contract-price round — the direct test of whether ASP HOLDS while stack height FALLS, which is what separates the bear (content down = revenue down) from @jukan05's bull inversion (scarcity relocates to PIN SPEED, per-layer value rises); (3) whether the HBM4 base-die unit count and HBM4e migration offset the step, and whether that value lands at the DRAM maker or one layer down at the base-die foundry — directly relevant to the [[INTC]]/[[TSM]] base-die item above; (4) whether Morgan Stanley restates the 48,618mn Gb 2027 AI HBM demand figure its 08-24 model built on a 12-Hi Rubin Ultra.** ⚠️ **Explicitly NOT asserted: @jukan05's UNCONFIRMED question (09-06) that **Feynman** was "also lowered to 8-High" — it contradicts Mizuho's 768GB/GPU HBM4e 16-Hi Feynman mark. Both stand.**
- **🆕🔴🔴 DECEMBER 2026 — SAMR'S FIVE-YEAR CONDITIONS ON THE 2021 [[INTC]] NAND ACQUISITION BECOME ELIGIBLE FOR REMOVAL. ⚠️ CONDITIONAL CATALYST: THE EXPIRY OF THE COMPLIANCE PERIOD IS NOT THE LIFTING OF THE CAP.** The condition: SK hynix may **not raise PCIe/SATA eSSD prices in China above the average of the 24 months preceding the 2021 approval** (plus obligations to keep expanding output for five years and to support a third competitor's entry). The five-year period **expires this December**; the restrictions **do NOT automatically lapse**, and SK hynix is *expected* — **Yonhap's inference, not a company statement** — to petition for removal once eligible. (TrendForce News, "SK hynix's China eSSD Business Hits Turning Point as Intel NAND Deal Restrictions Near Expiry by End-2026", 2026-09-02, citing Yonhap News / DealSite on SK hynix's semiannual report.) **WHAT TO WATCH, IN ORDER: (1) any SK hynix filing or IR comment confirming it has APPLIED — nothing is on the record today; (2) an SAMR ruling, which has no published deadline and which Yonhap frames as a policy trade-off between AI-data-centre supply cost and fostering YMTC; (3) China eSSD contract prices breaking above the capped level — the observable falsifier. THE PRIZE: repricing a book fed by 35%–40% of group NAND capacity (TrendForce) into a rising NAND tape, plus freedom to allocate volumes by profitability. THE OFFSET ALREADY VISIBLE: group eSSD share fell to 21.1% in 2Q26 from 23.1% (TrendForce), so the pricing lever would arrive on a shrinking position.** (mixed — bull only if actually lifted)
- **🆕🔴🔴 THE "SHORTAGE THROUGH END-2030" CLAIM IS NOW THE CENTRAL FALSIFIABLE THESIS ON THIS PAGE, AND JPM NAMES THE TEST: LTA PRICING DISCLOSURES.** The CEO's position is that oversupply risk is low *"since memory chips are no longer 'just commodities' in the AI era"* (@dnystedt, 2026-08-27). JPM's four catalysts for the sector are **(a) shareholder-return policy update at the 3Q26 earnings conference, Oct-end; (b) CSP capex update; (c) LTA pricing disclosures; (d) China competition update.** ➜ **(c) is the one that adjudicates the commoditisation argument: if LTA pricing holds as supply loosens in 2028, the CEO is right and TrendForce's late-2027 oversupply call is a commodity-framework error. If LTAs re-price down, he is not.**
- **🆕 3Q26 EARNINGS CONFERENCE, OCT-END — shareholder-return policy update expected, and JPM flags it as the sector's first named catalyst.** *"We expect to hear more on a transparent shareholder return policy update from both US/Asian memory makers within the next 3-4M."*
- **🆕🔴 THE INTEL HBM4E BASE-DIE EVALUATION — the highest-consequence unconfirmed item on this page.** *"SK hynix Reportedly Weighs Intel for HBM4E Base Dies amid TSMC Cost Pressure and Supply Diversification"* (UBS TMI, 2026-08-31). **Watch for: any confirmation, the generation it would apply to, and whether [[TSM]] responds — TSMC publicly claimed HBM base-die silicon from N12 (HBM4) to N3 (HBM5) at SEMICON Taiwan the same week. Cross-filed [[INTC]], [[TSM]].**
- **🆕 THE JAPAN GREENFIELD JV — a chairman's stated EVALUATION, nothing more.** *"SK Group's chairman Mr. Chey mentioned evaluation of a JV in Japan for a new greenfield fab where power and water supply are sufficient"* (JPM, 2026-08-31). **JPM's base case is that the majority of production stays in Korea (Yongin cluster 1 at 390-420K wfpm by 2030, three further Yongin clusters after it) and that any ex-Korea capacity is non-leading-edge — packaging or NAND, on the Solidigm/Dalian template. Watch for a named partner or site before treating it as capacity.**
- **🆕⚠️ THE NAND NODE-TRANSITION LAG IS A CAPEX OBLIGATION, NOT JUST A MARGIN GAP.** *"176-layer NAND still accounted for more than half of SK hynix's production [in 2Q26], while Samsung had already completed the transition… to 236-layer"* (@jukan05, 2026-08-27). ➜ **Read against the BofA 08-27 tour line that SK hynix has *"no interest in conventional DRAM/NAND capacity expansion"* and against the M17 Cheongju NAND fab base logged 08-28: migration capex is required regardless of appetite for net-new bits. Watch the 3Q26 capex split between migration and greenfield.**

- **🆕🔴 3Q26 EARNINGS — management "suggested further update" on the shareholder-return pool THEN.** The specific commitment to be tested: lifting the pool **from <50% to over 50% of accumulated FCF** (JPM · Kwon, 2026-08-23). Note [[SAMSUNG]] deferred its own equivalent decision to **January 2027**, so Hynix's 3Q26 print is the earlier of the two Korean capital-return events.
- **🆕 BY END-SEPTEMBER 2026 — HBM contract price update** (the industry-wide catalyst JPM names first for the complex); **2027E HBM ASP negotiations are already "at the later part of the process."** (JPM · Kwon, 2026-08-23)
- **🆕 2026-08-26, 09:00 HKT — Nanya Tech President call (BofA-hosted).** Nanya is the cleanest independent read on the CXMT question — at the JPM tour it argued **CXMT "has now been present in the market for well over a decade without materially disrupting global S/D, in large part because AI has been absorbing the very supply that would otherwise serve consumer end-markets."** (BofA · Woo, 2026-08-23; JPM · Kwon, 2026-08-23)


- **🆕🔴 By 2026-09-30 — HBM CONTRACT PRICE UPDATE.** Named by JPM (Kwon, 2026-08-19) as the #2 company-specific catalyst. **This is the one that moves earnings rather than the share count**, and it is the live test of the "HBM repricing in 2027" narrative the 2Q26 call half-confirmed.
- **🆕 Next month (~Sep 2026) — US SUBSIDIARY LISTING PLAN UPDATE** (JPM · Kwon, 2026-08-19).
- **🆕 By end-Oct 2026 — 3Q26 earnings call: the SECOND tranche of shareholder return.** The company said explicitly that it intends to pursue **additional** returns **within the current program period**, with scale/timing announced after board approval at the 3Q26 release, and that it is reviewing **both regular and special dividend** increases. MS: "this is not the end of the program."
- **🆕 2026-08-20 → 2026-11-19 — the buyback executes on-market** (SK Securities). Daily execution is observable; **the falsifier for the "most aggressive among peers" claim is whether the full W40trn actually gets bought and retired inside the window, and whether any of it is diverted to ADR float rather than cancelled** (management said float could expand without new-share issuance).
- **Q2 2026 earnings:** late July 2026 (English call; prior Q2 was 2025-07-24) — HBM4 ramp progress, DRAM ASP, capex number.
- **HBM4 / NVDA Rubin ramp** through 2026; HBM4 blended ≤50% of mix in 2026, 2H26 crossover (Jefferies Techknowledge, 2025-12-17); whether SK hynix holds ~56% HBM share (UBS 2026 overview) vs Samsung's pursued NVDA slot.
- **Samsung/Micron HBM4 qualification at NVDA** — Samsung targeting NVDA HBM4 qual by 1Q26 (UBS 2026 overview); the single biggest share-erosion risk, with NVDA itself nudging Samsung to add capacity (Jefferies Techknowledge, 2025-12-17).
- **Rubin (R200-class) HBM supply-share split** — whether SK hynix holds SemiAnalysis's modeled ~60% of first-12-months Rubin HBM supply (vs Samsung ~30%, Micron ~0%); confirmation would validate the current HBM3/HBM3E-execution lead carrying into HBM4 (SemiAnalysis "Memory Mania", 2026-02-06).
- **2026 capex disclosure & funding** — M15X (Cheongju) ramp delayed by Korea labour-law/industrial conflicts (fab structure targeted mid-2026, fab start ~later; Daifuku via Jefferies Techknowledge, 2025-12-17); first Yongin cluster fab delayed to next year; Indiana advanced-packaging expansion; dollar HBM capex rising sharply (greenfield + hybrid bonding) — SK hynix is "open to customer deposits" to help fund it. **Roadshow use-of-proceeds detail (SK Hynix ADS roadshow, 2026-07-06):** the ~$26.5bn ADR proceeds fund **Cheongju (₩19tn, plant PNT7) + Yongin (₩21tn) + EUV scanners**; net cash since Q3'25, management **targets ₩100tn cash by year-end 2026** ("easily") and, having hit that position earlier than planned, will **announce a shareholder-return measure on the Q3 conference call** (executable this year, vs the original 3-yr plan). New **M17 (Cheongju) NAND capacity contributes from 2029** (the reason NAND expansion in the roadshow deck footnotes read as ~2030 — decided/disclosed after the deck was produced). **Yongin Y1 acceleration (@jukan05, 2026-07-14):** SK hynix has begun placing equipment orders for Yongin Y1 and **pulled the Phase-1 pilot ("one-pass") line forward to Feb-2027 (from May-2027)**, with full install toward ~20K wpm following in Mar/Apr on **1c DRAM** (the most advanced node in commercial production, also feeding HBM4E, which could commercialize as early as next year); it also **moved equipment price-negotiations forward a quarter (to 3Q)** to speed installs. Y1 Phases 2&3 cleanroom construction is set to start 2H-2026; the Yongin cluster (KRW600tn / 4 fabs) has its 4th fab pulled forward 12yrs to 2033 — concrete execution on the capacity pull-forward.
- **ADR/local arbitrage "malfunctioning" until July 29 (@jukan05 / Korea Securities Depository, 2026-07-14):** two-way conversion between SK Hynix's Korean-listed common shares and the Nasdaq-listed SKHY line **only becomes possible after July 29**, when the newly issued Korean common shares backing the ADR offering list domestically. Until then the fungibility mechanism is inoperative — a technical explanation for the ADR-vs-local premium dislocation in the interim window.
- **Korea national AI mega-plan follow-through** — Samsung + SK Group's **KRW4,755tn (~US$3.1tn)** long-term capex plan through 2040 (JPM · Jay Kwon, 2026-06-30, revised up from the $880B/$1.3T framing reported just 48hrs earlier); watch for confirmation of the implied doubling of installed Korean DRAM capacity within 5 years and the 7-12yr Yongin pull-forward, plus whether the 60-70% front-end-wafer / 20-30% infra-cleanroom capex split (JPM, 2026-06-30) holds as disclosures firm up. Near-term signal to watch: whether the "hyperscaler curse" disconnect (capex-beneficiary basket +5% while memory names sell off — Rothschild, 2026-07-01) resolves toward the memory names as NPS rebalancing/DRAM-export-data noise fades.
- **US Nasdaq listing — expected Friday 2026-07-10** (fresh capital raise on top of the KRX:000660 line); investor reception is a read on both the IPO-market strength and the US-investor bid for the name (only MU among the big-3 is US-listed today). Firms up the "US listing/ADR" re-rating catalyst previously flagged by JPM (05-28) / Barclays (05-03) with a concrete date. On the valuation the listing highlights: 3.6x fwd sales vs MU 4.6x / NVDA 10.8x, P/B discount to MU (The Information, 2026-07-05). **Ticker confirmed SKHY** and the listing **raises capital** as part of the offering; rationale is to make the stock easier for US investors to buy; context cited: Hynix revenue **+200% between 2023 and 2025, then +200% again in Q1 2026** (The Information, 2026-07-05; multiple bookrunner IPO notices, 2026-07-06). **Size now put at ~US$29bn — potentially the largest-ever US IPO by a foreign issuer**, with proceeds "largely earmarked for expanding manufacturing capacity in Korea and securing additional EUV lithography machines from ASML" — "not financial engineering… a direct bet that the AI memory shortage is far from over" (Jefferies · Cristina Titu, 2026-07-07). Trading setup: several desks recommend **buy the ADR / sell the Seoul-listed shares** (ADR expected to command a premium + a cleaner route for global HFs), and Korean authorities are reportedly preparing for significant FX flows ahead of settlement. Valuation framing: Hynix trades ~**7x this year's / 5x next year's earnings** vs AI stories at 30-80x sales — "extraordinarily difficult to describe as anything other than cheap" as long as AI capex is alive; the biggest AI winners so far have been the "picks-and-shovels" suppliers, "and among those nobody is better positioned than SK Hynix" (Jefferies · Cristina Titu, 2026-07-07). **Deal size marked at ~US$28.2bn** (~17.79M new shares / ~2.5% of TSO; 1 ADR = 0.1 common share) by Morgan Stanley (S&T) on the 07-08 marketing detail — consistent with Jefferies' ~US$29bn (rounding/pricing-window difference, not a supersession); see the dedicated ADR-mechanics bullet below for premium/flow/index detail.
- **US Nasdaq ADR listing — mechanics & premium/flow view (Morgan Stanley (S&T) "SK Hynix ADR Listing", 2026-07-08).** MS Equity S&T (Pankaj Mataney, Asia Platform Sales & QDS — *note: S&T commentary, not MS Research, no rating*) details the deal: SK Hynix began marketing the proposed **Nasdaq ADR (ticker SKHY)** on **2026-07-06**; offering ~**US$28.2bn** via ADRs representing ~**17.79M new common shares (~2.5% of TSO)**; **1 ADR = 0.1 common share (10 ADRs = 1 ordinary)**; bookbuild Jul 6–9, pricing Jul 9, **ADRs begin trading 2026-07-10**, subscription-proceeds payment ~Jul 14, new underlying shares list on KRX Jul 29. Proceeds fund domestic expansion (Yongin, Cheongju packaging, advanced equipment). **Liquidity:** MS estimates the ADR line at **~10-20% of aggregate turnover** near-term (foreigners ~30% of local turnover × ~50% US-based); plus leveraged ETFs **SKHU/SKUU (2x long), SKDD (-2x)** expected ~Jul 13 adding dealer-hedging/rebalancing flow (Asia-listed SK Hynix levered ETFs, ~$18.7bn AUM, already run ~18% of cash turnover). Most Korean ADRs stay <10% of turnover (ex-KT/SK Telecom at 31-61% on prior FOL breaches). **Premium:** MS expects the SKHY ADR to trade at a **~5-10% premium** to the local line (higher in event-driven windows like index/ETF inclusion) — between typical Korean ADRs (parity to low-single-digit) and TSMC ADR (double-digit; ~18% as of Jul 6 vs 13.3% 5-yr avg). Driver = limited ~2.5% issuance headroom + supportive narrative; caveat: headroom alone doesn't guarantee a premium (TSMC traded near parity in stretches), and weaker memory fundamentals/sentiment could compress it toward parity. **Fungibility is one-way:** ADR→local seamless (from Jul 29); local→ADR constrained by the ~2.5% headroom (expected fully used at launch), so new ADRs need existing holders to cancel/convert back. Further primary issuance is limited near-term — **SK Square must hold ≥20% of SK Hynix (currently ~20.5%)**, capping dilution; any expansion likely via secondary/buyback structures or onshore approval. MS also flags **KSD headroom (not SEC Form F-6, which shows max program size of 1.78bn ADS / 25% TSO) as the right benchmark** — some investors are mis-sizing US index-inclusion market cap off the F-6 figure. **Index events:** MSCI ~**$1.9bn** inflow (Nov-2026 review, local line); FTSE ~**$1.28bn** (Sep-2026, local line); **KOSPI 200 ~$955mn** (ad-hoc, effective ~Jul 29); **VanEck SMH ~$3.8bn** (earliest Sep-2026 if treated as an index IPO, else Sep-2027); **SOX ~$114mn** (earliest Sep-2027); **Nasdaq-100 near-term inclusion unlikely** (eligibility on full ADR market cap ~$30bn vs the ~$200bn fast-entry / ~$70bn top-75 thresholds). Options expected ~2 trading days after listing; SEC Rule 105 / Reg M short-sale constraints may reach the local line given fungibility (Morgan Stanley (S&T) "SK Hynix ADR Listing: Key Investor Questions and Our Views", Pankaj Mataney, 2026-07-08). - **SKHY book heavily oversubscribed** — demand for the ~$28bn US share sale was **more than 7x available shares** (Reuters, 2026-07-09), a strong read on the US-investor bid ahead of the 2026-07-10 trading debut. (bull)
- **✅ RESOLVED — Nasdaq ADR debut (2026-07-10): priced at $149, raised ~$26.5bn.** SK Hynix priced its ADRs at **$149**, raising **~US$26.5bn** (regulatory filing) — the largest-ever first-time share sale by a foreign company on US markets, **pushing aside Alibaba** for the record (Bloomberg @business; Reuters, 2026-07-10). The book was **7x oversubscribed with 500+ investment firms**, and even key/anchor investors (incl. Leo/Philippe allocations) had **final allocations cut on the excess demand** (FT via @firstadopter, 2026-07-10). ADRs priced at a **~3% premium** to the local line (below the ~5-10% MS S&T range and the 15-20% some desks were comfortable with — @zerohedge, 2026-07-10; Barron's "set to trade at a premium"). Realized raise (~$26.5bn) came in a touch below the ~$28.2-29bn marketing size (MS S&T 07-08 / Jefferies 07-07). Korean-listed line still trades **~4.5x forward earnings** (FT via @firstadopter). ETF issuers have **≥6 products ready to launch next week** (incl. leveraged SKHU/SKUU/SKDD) (Bloomberg @business, 2026-07-10). Cramer: SK Hynix analysts likely to raise numbers Monday to "keep this tight" (@jimcramer, 2026-07-10). (bull — the US-listing/ADR re-rating catalyst is now realized with record-setting demand)
- **Intel Ohio fab — US-manufacturing option (@jukan05 / Korean media, 2026-07-21; SK Hynix official, 2026-07-22):** Korean media reported SK Hynix in talks / a "bold move" to acquire Intel's Ohio fab for US memory production. Both parties denied a sale/acquisition (07-21), without denying a possible JV; SK Hynix's official statement (07-22) says it "has neither pursued nor decided" on the Ohio plant but "continuously reviews various investment/acquisition opportunities." Watch item on US-footprint pressure — likely rumor for now, but a live channel for the US-localization push (cross-ref the Indiana packaging build).
- **HBF (High Bandwidth Flash)** — SK hynix signed an MOU with SanDisk (Aug-2025) to standardize HBF for AI inference (NAND augmenting HBM); first SanDisk HBF samples 2H26 (MS AI NAND overview).
- 🆕 **HBF + TWO MORE TIERS — SK HYNIX PUT A THREE-TIER PROPOSAL ON A SLIDE AT FMS, AND HBF IS ONLY ONE OF THEM (FUNDA, "Takeaways from the Future of Memory and Storage Conference 2026", 2026-08-11).** *"SK Hynix proposed **3D stacked DRAM, HBF, and CXL Pooled Memory** as three new tiers of memory."* ➜ **This is the first time this page can say that SK hynix is attacking the hierarchy at three levels rather than defending HBM plus one flash option. Two of the three are NOT HBM: HBF is NAND-based and CXL Pooled Memory is commodity-DRAM-based — i.e. the company is positioning to monetise the tiers that exist BECAUSE HBM is too expensive, which is the tiering logic already on this page from the former-SK-Hynix-executive expert call ("because of high cost, so they rather use TIERED MEMORY"). Watch which of the three gets a product date first; only HBF has one (2027 samples). Cross-ref [themes/cxl-memory-fabric](themes/cxl-memory-fabric.md), [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md).**
- 🆕 **HBF customer confirmation and its conspicuous omission, same source:** *"The Sandisk / SK Hynix announcement **confirmed [[GOOG]]'s interest in HBF**. Investors noted **[[NVDA]]'s absence from the HBF presentations and panels**, which suggests **Nvidia prefers an architecture built on high-IOPS SSDs**."* ➜ **The Google half corroborates the 08-04 consortium row below (Google + Tenstorrent as key ecosystem partners). The Nvidia half is the harder datapoint and it belongs with @bubbleboi's "who is the actual customer" objection already logged here: NVDA is not a consortium member, its own named storage paths are SCADA (GPU-direct-to-eSSD) and CMX (GPU→DPU→eSSD), and it sent nobody to the HBF panels. So the HBF demand anchor is the ASIC/TPU camp, not the largest accelerator buyer on the tape.**
- 🆕 **And the supply mechanic, which is the leg that pays SK hynix even if it never sells an HBF stack:** *"If HBF ramps, it may **consume NAND wafers at premium pricing the same way HBM did inside DRAM**. Every wafer diverted to HBF then **tightens commodity flash supply**"* (FUNDA, 2026-08-11). ➜ **Stated three days BEFORE Bernstein quantified the same mechanic at 3-4x wafer capacity per exabyte (08-14 row below). For a supplier that is simultaneously expanding China NAND output ~50%, an industry-wide NAND wafer sink is a tailwind to the part of the book this page has been most worried about.**
- **MU print** as the cleanest near-term memory demand/pricing tell; UBS sees MU DDR GM surpassing HBM GM for the first time in early CY26 on commodity-DRAM tightness (UBS 2026 overview).
- **Consumer demand elasticity** — PC/mobile cuts (NVDA SOCAMM2 cut, AAPL prepayment/balance-sheet talk; briefings 2026-06-18/19).

## Risks
- **Commodity cyclicality / ASP reversal** — peak ~72% OP margin is not a through-cycle level; capacity additions (own + China CXMT/YMTC) normalize pricing. CXMT aggressively expanding HBM (HBM2 8-high mass production from 1H25, TSV capacity nearing Micron's by YE); Huawei building its own HBM supply chain (SemiAnalysis HBM).
  - ⚠️ **COUNTER-EVIDENCE ON THE CXMT LEG OF THIS RISK, logged 2026-08-21 from an independent source that TESTS the claims rather than asserting them** (DAMNANG (Substack), "After CXMT's Listing, Do the Memory Three Have Further to Fall?", **2026-07-27** — ⚠️ **NOT a broker; ~25 days stale, its price marks are historical, the framework is the value**). The three load-bearing planks of the CXMT price-war bear all weaken on inspection: **(i) the "sells 30% cheaper" figure is a company-average artefact — two thirds of CXMT revenue is low-price mobile; like-for-like the prospectus gap is 5-10%, and in SERVER product the sign FLIPS (a 64GB DDR5 module on CXMT chips at RMB18,999 vs RMB18,595 for the same spec on Samsung/SK hynix chips, July 2026 = CXMT 2.2% ABOVE)**; **(ii) it cannot undercut — >30% higher cost for the same bits, ~40% larger die, yield below the 85-90% industry standard**; **(iii) the "8% share" is REVENUE (7.7% of 1Q26 world DRAM revenue on US$7.31bn) while 13% of wafer starts convert to just 6% of capacity, and its 1Q26 +71% y/y was ASP +57% on volume +11%.** On HBM the direction actually INVERTS: **<2% of wafer starts, ~25% 8-high yield vs 70-90% incumbent, and stacking consumes ~3x the wafer area per GB — so CXMT moving into HBM pushes ordinary DRAM prices UP, not down.** IPO-funded supply lands **2028 at the earliest**. ➡ **The SK-hynix-specific conclusion is the one to hold: earnings concentrated in HBM, a segment "CXMT cannot enter," make this the MOST INSULATED of the three — and "what moves SK hynix shares is [the capex plans of a handful of AI-accelerator customers], not CXMT."** ⚠️ **This does NOT retire the risk line above: the 2028+ capacity build, the CXMT HBM-localisation vector, the DRAM price-fixing litigation and the China-substitution legs already logged on this page are untouched. It re-weights the NEAR-TERM CXMT price-war fear specifically — and note it agrees with the two sell-side CXMT initiations already on the page (UBS 08-07: "LIMITED price discounts for CXMT's DRAM vs global peers"; Nomura 07-26 bullish on CXMT while still forecasting demand outrunning all-maker supply). Full detail in the archived 07-27 row: [## Changelog](#changelog).**
- **HBM share loss / customer concentration** — UBS 2026 HBM share of 56% (vs ~90% historically); NVDA encouraging Samsung to ramp; Samsung's HBM4 self-described as best-in-class (Jefferies Techknowledge, 2025-12-17; UBS 2026 overview).
- **HBM tool dependency** — concentrated TC-bonding reliance on Hanmi for HBM3E 12-high; ASMPT/Besi/Hanwha not yet fully qualified (SemiAnalysis HBM). Concrete case study: Hanmi held ~100% share of SK hynix's TC bonders until fall-2024 diversification into Hanwha; Hanmi then pulled its field-service teams from SK hynix's fabs (early April 2025), the Hanwha tools went undelivered, and the ASMPT bonders ordered fall-2024 didn't work for HBM3E 12-high — forcing SK hynix to place a placating order back with Hanmi to keep packaging output flowing (SemiAnalysis "Scaling the Memory Wall: HBM", 2025-08-11).
- **Demand destruction** — elevated DRAM/NAND prices crimp PC/mobile bit demand.
- **De-spec / content destruction in the accelerator platforms themselves — a distinct risk from consumer elasticity (SemiAnalysis, 2026-07-24 and 2026-07-21).** The platforms are cutting DRAM and HBM attach *because* memory is scarce and expensive, so bits-per-accelerator (which most models hold flat or growing) is the exposed variable rather than price. On AMD's MI455X the **up-to-1TB per-accelerator direct-attached LPDDR tier has been removed outright** and DDR5 de-spec'd to a far lower RDIMM content vs an original 2025 BOM of **138TB of DRAM per platform across LPDDR5X + DDR5 MRDIMM** — "much of the DRAM price increases are offset by aggressive de-specs" (SemiAnalysis "Can AMD break the CUDA Moat?", 2026-07-24). In the custom parts it is a direct HBM-content cut: **Meta's custom MI450/MI455 halves the compute silicon (8 dies → 4), cuts HBM from 12 stacks to 6 per package, and downgrades the HBM4 from 12-Hi to 8-Hi** — at one of AMD's two largest GPU customers, on a program SemiAnalysis argues "is going to nuke AMD's volume at Meta because TBD will vastly prefer Rubin," making the read **doubly negative: fewer stacks per package on a program that may itself shrink** (SemiAnalysis "Meta's Infrastructure Team Needs A Culture Reset", 2026-07-21; restated 2026-07-24). Read against the page's LTA thesis this cuts both ways — take-or-pay locks volume for the covered book, but the *un*covered ~33-50% of the market is exactly where de-spec bites. [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_21_ENC_Metas_Infrastructure_Team_Needs_A_Culture_Reset_email.html)
- **HBM4 top-bin qualification, not just capacity** — Nvidia's above-JEDEC **10.7 Gbps** HBM4 pin-speed target (vs **7.6 Gbps** on AMD's MI455X) forced suppliers to rework HBM4 and means Nvidia "must use a much higher-quality bin than AMD"; that concentrates the risk in **yield on the premium bin** rather than in wafer output, and widens the quality/price spread between the three suppliers (SemiAnalysis "Can AMD break the CUDA Moat?", 2026-07-24). Relevant given this page's core swing factor is HBM4 allocation at NVIDIA; SemiAnalysis names no bin winner.
- **Capex intensity / funding** — large greenfield fab + packaging build-out (Yongin, M15X, Indiana) into a possible cycle peak; rising hybrid-bonding/R&D cost; Korea construction delays (Daifuku via Jefferies Techknowledge, 2025-12-17). Funding mechanism corroborated tool-side: ASML's DRAM customers only placed (prepaid) EUV orders **after receiving hyperscaler prepayments** — i.e. the LTA/deposit chain is what de-risked the capex (BofA ASML callback, 2026-04-15).
- **Litho/tool intensity & EUV bottleneck risk** — Hynix's DRAM expansion is increasingly EUV-layer-intensive (1C ~6-7 EUV layers, a prospective 1D ~10 layers ~2028), but legacy capacity adds still pull **immersion**: e.g. **SK Hynix's Wuxi (China) fab runs no EUV**, so capacity growth there is immersion-driven — a China-exposure/tool-mix nuance (BofA ASML callback, 2026-04-15).
- **Korea/macro & FX** — KRW reporting, geopolitical/export-control exposure, China memory entrants.
- **Antitrust / price-fixing litigation** — a 17-plaintiff DRAM price-fixing class action filed in N.D. Cal. (2026-06-30) names Samsung, SK Hynix and Micron for allegedly conspiring to keep DRAM artificially scarce; early-stage legal/headline overhang (media report via @dnystedt, 2026-06-30).
- **🆕⚠️🔴 THE 2027 EARNINGS LINE IS FLAT ON A BULL'S OWN NUMBERS — AND THAT, NOT THE MARGIN, IS WHAT CAPS THE MULTIPLE.** BofA's Korea coverage exhibit carries SK hynix at **2027E EPS growth of +3%, the lowest of any major name in its Korea book, against +493% in 2026E**, with BofA's own caption: *"high growth continues ex-memory; Hynix's low growth should be a matter of 2026 high base"* ("Korea super-cycle continues into 2030", 2026-09-18). The paired multiples say the same thing: **5.0x 26E vs 4.9x 27E P/E**. ➤ **This is the arithmetic behind the standing "why does it only trade at 5x" question. A flat year on a record base is the classic setup for a cyclical de-rate, and it is why BofA itself has to justify its target off DURABILITY (8x 2027-28E EPS, "slightly below our fair multiples for global DRAM peers (9-11x)") rather than off growth.** ⚠️ **Logged as the house's own exhibit, not adopted — and note it is a BULL's number, which is what makes it uncomfortable.**
- **🆕 THE CHINA LEG GETS MATERIALLY BIGGER IN THE MODEL THAT JUST RAISED SUPPLY.** Deutsche Bank lifts **CXMT to 300k (2025) → 775k WSPM (2030), +475k — the largest single-vendor addition anywhere in its build, overtaking [[MU]]'s 760k by 2030** — on accelerated Shanghai expansion plus a new Beijing fab (+275k versus DB's prior forecast) ("Global Memory: Chipping away at industry supply imbalance", 2026-09-16). ➤ **Relevant because DB's headline conclusion is oversupply only "potentially" in 2030: the China build is the biggest single determinant of whether that date arrives at all, and it is the leg over which neither Korean maker has any control.**
- **Solidigm/NAND** — fragmented, structurally less profitable than DRAM, though MS sees Solidigm as a key NAND-upcycle beneficiary on high QLC eSSD exposure and fully-depreciated, low-cost fabs (MS AI NAND overview); HBM-driven DRAM mix-shift squeezing NAND wafers cuts both ways.
