Not a contradiction — capacity mix ≠ revenue coverage, and management is describing an aspiration rather than a current book — but the page should stop treating 60-70%/70%+ as SK hynix-confirmed. It is not.** ✅ **The ~60%-of-DRAM-revenue server-memory mix IS newly confirmed by management and is the cleanest structural statistic in the window.**
- 🔴 **F/X — A NEW, NON-CYCLICAL EARNINGS SENSITIVITY THE PAGE HAS ONLY SEEN THROUGH BROKERS UNTIL NOW, STATED BY MANAGEMENT:** *"Hynix mentioned that **nearly 100% of its revenue is USD denominated while less than 50% of its operating expenses is based on foreign currency**, hence near-term earnings could be impacted by F/X fluctuations."* ➤ ✅ **That is the mechanism behind Mirae's entire earnings cut this month (USD/KRW 1,553 → 1,400 taking OP down 4.8%/5.6% for 26/27F even as it RAISED ASPs) and behind the 09-14 "estimates cut on the WON, not on demand" item already on this page. Management has now quantified the exposure: roughly 100% USD revenue against >50% KRW cost — a ~50pp net USD length.**
- 🔴🔴 **SHAREHOLDER RETURN — THE DATE IS NOW FIXED AT THE 3Q26 CALL BY BOTH THE COMPANY AND TWO HOUSES.** GS (management): *"additional details on its **next round of shareholder returns would be finalized around 3Q26 earnings announcement**, with a **careful balance between buybacks and dividends**."* UBS: *"SK Hynix recently announced (1) a **Won 40tn share buyback/cancellation program (to be completed by November '26)** and (2) plans to return **at least 50% of cumulative FCF in the current policy period (2025-27E)**… SK Hynix reiterated that **50% of FCF is now a floor**."* UBS FCF: **Won 186tn / 315tn / 363tn in '26E/'27E/'28E**, average '26-'30E FCF yield **22%**. JPM: at a **67% FCF payout** SKH's TSR yield runs **7% / 14% / 20% (26E/27E/28E)** and it keeps **SKH > SEC in the near term "upon superior shareholder return policy and higher TSR yield."** ➤ **Three independent confirmations that the capital-return event is the 3Q26 call, and JPM makes it a pre-condition rather than a nice-to-have: *"We view shareholder returns as a **pre-requisite for memory stocks to re-rate**."***
- 🔴 **VALUATION MECHANICS, BECAUSE THE UBS FRAME IS AN IMPLIED-ROE ARGUMENT AND IT IS FALSIFIABLE:** *"At **2.63x NTM Book** the stock is not discounting our LT ROE forecast of **50.0%**… shares now trade at 2.65x NTM P/BV, **discounting a long-term ROE of 31.2% vs our estimate of 50.0%**."* Scenario ladder: **upside Won 3.60m (+93%, DRAM ASP +55%/+10% '27/'28E, OP W570tn/610tn) · base Won 3.00m (+61%, +37%/+2%, OP W500tn/541tn) · downside Won 1.20m (−36%, +25%/−5%, OP W440tn/490tn)**. ➤ **The whole UBS target reduces to one testable claim — that SK hynix's through-cycle ROE is 50% rather than the ~31% the market is paying for. The 2029E down-leg in its own model (revenue −11%, DRAM ASP −23.7%) is what makes 50% a THROUGH-cycle number rather than a peak one.**
- 📎 **DEMAND CONTEXT UBS ADDS, AND ONE AFFORDABILITY NUMBER TO CARRY FORWARD:** *"**DRAM bit end-consumption growing 40% YoY in '27 vs 21% in '26, and NAND flash 24%**… as almost all of the incremental wafer capacity addition for DRAM is going to HBM, and **no new capacity ex China is being added to NAND**, supply is not catching up with demand. **The main risk remains affordability as memory industry revenues are approaching US$1.64tn in '27E**."* ⚠️ **US$1.64tn is the third vintage of this number on the wiki (UBS $1,763bn on 07-03; US$1.61tn on 08-18) — the level has been broadly held, not raised, across three months. Recorded as a REVISION of UBS's own series, not as a new datapoint.**
- 🔴 **HBM CONTENT — MIRAE'S CUSTOMER-BY-CUSTOMER LADDER IS THE MOST GRANULAR ON THIS PAGE AND IT ALL POINTS UP, WHICH IS THE COUNTER TO THE DE-SPEC BEAR.** *"**AMD's MI455X features 12 HBM4 stacks totaling 432GB, 50% more than the MI355X**. **Nvidia's Rubin features 288GB of HBM4**, while reports suggest that **Rubin CPX — which was originally announced with GDDR7 — has been redesigned to feature 168GB of HBM4**, raising the possibility of 1:1 pairing with Rubin GPUs… **Meta's MTIA 300** features six HBM3E stacks providing **216GB and 6.1TB/s**, while **MTIA 400** increases this to eight stacks providing **288GB and 9.4TB/s** and is set to enter mass production in **2H26**. Meta also targets a **doubling of HBM bandwidth with MTIA 450**, followed by a **further 50% increase with MTIA 500**. Similarly, **Microsoft's MAIA 200** features six 12-high HBM3E stacks totaling **216GB and 7TB/s**, and deployment on Azure has begun. **OpenAI's Jalapeno** was the only ASIC unveiled at Hot Chips 2026 to adopt **HBM4**, featuring **216GiB and 15.4TB/s**… a **2,048-chip pod** offering **432TiB of aggregate memory and 32PB/s**."* Mirae's conclusion: *"**HBM capacity per chip among big tech ASICs is now converging in the 216–288GB range**."* ➤ 🔴 **THE ASIC CONVERGENCE BAND IS THE FINDING. 216-288GB is BELOW NVDA's Rubin 288GB and far below the 384-512GB UBS now models for VR300 — so the ASIC share gain JPM forecasts for 2027 (48% of HBM bits vs NVDA 43%) is a MIX SHIFT TOWARD LOWER-CONTENT PARTS, offset by ASIC unit growth of +102% y/y. Bits go up; content per part does not.** ⚠️ **Rubin CPX at 168GB of HBM4 is the same reversal this page logged on 08-31 (GDDR7 → HBM4); Mirae is an independent second sighting.**
- ✅ **SK HYNIX'S OWN PRODUCT POSITION, FROM THE SAME MIRAE NOTE:** *"Its **HBM4 lineup offers capacities of up to 48GB, with 12-high products in mass production and 16-high products undergoing customer qualification**. The company has also **completed development of a 48GB 16-high HBM3E product, reducing die thickness by 20% and inter-die spacing by 50% vs. its 12-high design."* ⚠️ **Note the tension with JPM in the same window: JPM pushes **16Hi adoption out to "2029E at the earliest"** on *"multiple technology validation issues and cost reasons"*, and says **TCB stays the mainstream back-end solution over hybrid bonding** as a result. SK hynix has 16-high in customer qualification TODAY. Capability ≠ adoption; both marks stand.**

**🆕🔴🔴🔴 2026-09-21 (/wiki-ingest 21h) — THE SUPPLY-SIDE ARGUMENT GETS ITS HARDEST PHYSICAL CONSTRAINT YET AND THE LTA HORIZON EXTENDS TO **2031**: *"THERE IS VIRTUALLY NO NEW CLEANROOM SPACE ARRIVING BEFORE LATE 2027 OR EARLY 2028, WHILE DEBOTTLENECKING AND PROCESS MIGRATION APPEAR LARGELY EXHAUSTED."* AND THE ADR ENTERED **SMH** ON THIS SESSION.**

(Jefferies · **Cristina Titu**, MD Equity Sales, *"SEC / HYNIX — 50% of Market Cap Coming Back?"*, **2026-09-21**, written off a Seoul trip — ⚠️ **MARKET COMMENTARY, EXPLICITLY NOT RESEARCH.** Carries third-party house estimates (JEF/KB · Jeff Kim; Eugene Investment & Securities; Fubon) rather than a Jefferies rating or PT.)

### 🔴🔴🔴 (A) THE CAPACITY ARGUMENT — AND WHY IT IS DIFFERENT FROM EVERY PRIOR VERSION ON THIS PAGE

- **Inventories remain below 10 days.** But the load-bearing claim is not inventory, it is **cleanroom**: *"There is virtually no new cleanroom space arriving before late 2027 or early 2028, while debottlenecking and process migration appear largely exhausted."* ➤ **Supply is capped. Demand is not.** ⚠️ **Note the structural rhyme with [[AMAT]]'s own message at Bernstein's bus tour the same day — customer *cleanroom availability*, not tool lead times, is the binding constraint on WFE conversion. Two different layers of the chain naming the same physical bottleneck on the same day.**
- 🔴🔴🔴 **THE LTA BOOK, EXTENDED AND PRICED: *"Contract penalties can reach 20%, and as much as 70% of memory supply could be locked into long-term agreements through 2031."*** ➤ **This page already carried the 50-70% capacity lock-up (vendor-published, 09-17). What is net-new is (i) the HORIZON — 2031, not 2027-28 — and (ii) a PENALTY NUMBER, which is what turns an LTA from a price indication into an enforceable floor. *"The result is a market where pricing power stays with the producers."*** ⚠️ **"could be" — this is Titu's read of the Seoul conversations, not a disclosed contract term.**
- ⚠️ AND THE SAME NOTE PUTS ITS OWN CEILING ON IT, WHICH IS WHY THIS IS NOT A ONE-SIDED BULL DOCUMENT: *"With Korean memory companies generating close to 80% margins, we may be approaching PEAK ECONOMICS."* Pricing growth *"will slow from today's elevated levels"* — and she carries **Fubon Securities' dated bear marker: commodity DRAM peaks out in Q2 2027**, based on checks with **ODM purchasing managers for AI racks.** ➤ **A falsifiable date on the commodity leg. Log it; do not adopt it. It sits against the 09-17 JPM ASP curve (peak 4Q26, plateau) already on this page — two houses, two different peak dates, both inside the LTA window.**

### 🔴🔴 (B) THE STORY ROTATES FROM SCARCITY TO CASH DISTRIBUTION — AND A MECHANICAL ETF FLOW LANDED TODAY

- ✅ **SK HYNIX ADR (SKHY) ADDED TO THE VANECK SEMICONDUCTOR ETF (SMH), REPLACING SKYWORKS, AT A ~5% INDEX WEIGHTING** following the semiannual MVIS US Listed Semiconductor 25 Index review. SMH had **US$68.7bn** net assets as of 09-17 → implied passive demand **~US$3.43bn (~KRW 4.7tn)**. Index change implemented at the **09-18 US close, effective 2026-09-21**. Separately Samsung Asset Management's **KODEX US Semiconductor ETF added the ADR at ~4.8% (~W300bn)**; Mirae, KB, Samsung Active, Timefolio and Asset Plus also carry it since the **2026-07-10** Nasdaq listing. ⚠️⚠️ **THE US$3.43bn IS A MECHANICAL INDEX-WEIGHT CALCULATION, NOT CONFIRMED NET BUYING, and some of it will have been front-run before the effective date. It is a TECHNICAL catalyst, separate from the HBM earnings outlook — do not read it as a fundamental datapoint.**
- ⚠️ **AND THE OFFSETTING FLOW, WHICH THE SAME NOTE FLAGS: SK Hynix and [[SAMSUNG]] have collectively repurchased KRW 34.7tn of shares, and BOTH PROGRAMMES COULD CONCLUDE AHEAD OF THEIR ORIGINAL NOVEMBER DEADLINES — a potential demand vacuum for the KOSPI (Yonhap).** ➤ **Two opposing mechanical flows inside the same fortnight: SMH inclusion in, buyback exhaustion out. Neither is fundamental.**
- ✅ **MACRO CORROBORATION AT THE COUNTRY LEVEL: Korea's exports for 2026-09-01→20 reached US$71.4bn (+78.3% y/y), beating the prior record US$61.7bn set in June. SEMICONDUCTOR exports were US$34.12bn, +259.4% y/y — an all-time high for the 1-20 period and 47.8% of total exports (up 24.1pp y/y). Exports to Taiwan +128.5%, the US +118.0%, China +113.8%. Daily-average exports +89.8% despite one fewer working day; the 1-20 trade surplus of US$23bn is the largest ever for the period. Imports +26.7% to US$48.44bn, with semiconductor imports +88.8% and semiconductor MANUFACTURING EQUIPMENT +55.6%.** ➤ **The +55.6% equipment-import line is the forward-looking one: it is the capex that the "no cleanroom before 2028" claim says cannot yet convert into wafers. Both can be true — tools ordered now, space in 2028.**

- ⚠️ **ALSO LOGGED, SOURCE QUALITY FLAGGED — A SECOND-ORDER FINANCIALISATION SIGNAL: *"A credit executive also told me recently that LENDERS HAVE BEEN EXPLORING FINANCING VEHICLES TO BUY AND RESELL MEMORY CAPACITY FOR A PROFIT"*** (The Information's AI Infrastructure newsletter, relayed by **@jukan05**, 2026-09-21). ➤ **If real, it is the memory analogue of the neocloud structure — a third party warehousing capacity risk off the producers' balance sheets. Single anonymous sourcing, relayed at second hand. NOT ADOPTED; flagged as the kind of structure that appears at cycle extremes.**

**🆕🔴🔴🔴 2026-09-20 (/wiki-ingest 21h) — CXMT PUT ITS **G5 NODE INTO MASS PRODUCTION ~TWO YEARS AHEAD OF MORGAN STANLEY'S OWN MODEL**, AND ON THE SAME MORNING BofA RAISED THE 2030 MEMORY TAM TO $2.0tn AND PUBLISHED A CONTRACT-LEVEL PRICE INVERSION: **HYPERSCALERS HAVE AGREED TO PAY A HIGHER DRAM ASP IN 1H27 THAN IN 4Q26.** THE SECOND IS THE ONE THAT MOVES THIS PAGE'S LTA DEBATE.**

- 🔴🔴🔴 **THE PRICE INVERSION, AND WHY IT IS THE MOST IMPORTANT LINE IN THE BofA NOTE FOR SK HYNIX SPECIFICALLY.** BofA · **Simon Woo, CFA** (*"Simon's tech check (20 Sept)"*, 2026-09-20): *"Another notable development is **hyperscalers' new agreements to pay higher DRAM ASP in 1H27 than in 4Q26**."* ➤➤ **This page has carried the LTA question as the central uncertainty — 50-70% of vendor capacity locked (09-17), >70% of Samsung's DRAM and NAND wafers under LTAs (09-18), and the standing bear argument that contracts SMOOTH the 2027 curve rather than compound it. A forward contract struck ABOVE the prevailing spot quarter is the opposite of smoothing: it is the customer pre-paying for tightness. ⚠️ Logged as a channel observation from BofA's checks with chipmakers, spot distributors and OEMs — no counterparty, volume or contract term is disclosed, so it is a direction, not a number.**
- 🔴🔴 **THE REST OF THE NEAR-TERM PRICE MARK:** **3Q DRAM up 20-30% q/q despite rising LTAs; NAND up 15%+**; at least a single-digit ASP increase assumed in 4Q; **2027-28 DRAM / NAND ASP assumptions raised +8-12% / +2-3%**, with no change to 2H26 estimates. PC and smartphone production cuts *"have not been severe YTD, with volumes down only 10%+/− YoY despite the memory-chip shortage"* — ➤ **a relevant demand-destruction check: the elasticity bears on this page have been arguing that $/GB at these levels forces unit cuts; a ~10% unit decline against a 3-4x ASP move is not that.**
- ⚠️⚠️ **AND THE DOWN YEAR BofA KEEPS INSIDE THE $2.0tn HEADLINE:** *"we expect **2028 ASP to decline 10%+/− (DRAM: −5%; NAND: −13%)**"*, framed as a **soft landing** before renewed 2029-30 growth. The $2.0tn 2030 TAM (raised from $1.8tn) rests on a **21% four-year CAGR from 2027's $1.4tn** and implies a **$10tn industry market cap at 13x P/E on a 50% OPM and 25% tax rate.** ➤ **Two independent methods now put the soft spot in 2028: BofA's ASP path and DB's 09-16 supply model (demand/supply 110% in 2028 → 102% in 2029 → a 96% surplus in 2030).**
- 🔴🔴 **CXMT G5 — THE COMPETITIVE LEG, AND THE TIMING IS THE FINDING.** MS · **Charlie Chan** et al. (*"CXMT Corp: G5 Enters Mass Production Ahead of Expectations; OW"*, 2026-09-20, CXMT OW / PT Rmb88.00): two **24Gb LPDDR5X** parts in mass production on G5; **quadruple patterning**; **active-area half-pitch 11.95nm** — which MS calls *"broadly in line with the active geometry of global 1b-class DRAM"*; **storage-capacitor aspect ratio 45:1**; **gross dies per wafer ≥50% above G4 on an 8Gb-equivalent basis.** MS: *"ahead of our previous expectation that G5 would begin contributing only around 2028."* @jukan05's independent read narrows the Big-3 gap to **2 generations conservatively / 1.5 optimistically**, against the KSIA mark carried here 09-19 (**CXMT 2 years behind on DRAM, 3 on HBM; YMTC 1 year behind on NAND**). ⚠️ **NOT a supply number — MS says capacity and yield are undisclosed, these are mobile LPDDR5X parts rather than server DRAM or HBM, and gross DPW is not net good die. It retires roadmap risk; it does not add bits.** ➤ **The direct read-through for SK Hynix is the CONVENTIONAL/MOBILE flank, not HBM — consistent with the 09-19 relay that Chinese smartphone makers are shifting to CXMT.**
- 🔴 **POSITIONING AND EVENT COLOUR.** BofA's **APAC Conference (21-24 Sep, HK)** has investor meetings with **TrendForce, [[SAMSUNG]], SK Hynix and Nanya *"oversubscribed, implying strong interest in memory"*** — and BofA *"expect[s] bullish memory forecasts and guidance"* there. Separately, Korean media report **Stanley Druckenmiller making his first visit to South Korea, with plans to visit Samsung, SK hynix and Doosan** (@jukan05, 2026-09-20). ⚠️ **Both are attention datapoints, not fundamentals — logged because this page's live debate is a POSITIONING debate (memory fell to 18% of preferred buy-side sector longs from 50% in mid-June), and inbound interest of this kind is the first counter-evidence in that series.**

Cross-filed [[MU]], [[SAMSUNG]], [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md).

**Sinal vs gestão** — what management said on the Q2 2026 call × what the flow is saying (✓ confirma · ⚠ nuança · ✗ contesta):

_**Síntese — update 2026-07-30 (two days post-print, after the expert call and the Korea unwind):** the mechanical explanation for the "miss" is now settled and it is **mix, not execution**. The BTG former-SK-hynix executive: SK hynix runs **~40% of wafer capacity on HBM vs Samsung's ≤25%**, and HBM pricing is set roughly a year ahead, so when conventional DRAM spiked SK hynix's blended DRAM ASP printed **+30% against Samsung's +mid-40%** — Samsung captured the spot spike, SK hynix was contracted through it. Same source: **operating margin still went 72% → 76%**, "exceeding margins seen in companies like TSMC or Apple." The offsetting fact is the moat: **HBM4 yield ~80-85% at SK hynix (already at mature-generation levels) versus ~60% at Samsung**, which is what lets SK hynix supply Nvidia's Rubin volumes and is worth ~40% of potential output to Samsung. So @firstadopter's contrarian read on print night — "SK Hynix missing numbers is **BULLISH** for SK Hynix … a Q2 miss caused by more HBM mix actually means SK Hynix is BETTER positioned in the long run" (2026-07-28) — is directionally supported by the expert work, with the caveat that HBM's 40-60% stable margin is *structurally below* the >80% conventional server DRAM can earn in a spike. **Where the Street landed: KIS (the Korean axe that called the Q2 mechanics correctly) RAISED its target 24% to KRW 4.7m** two days after the print (@jukan05, 2026-07-29), while **BNK cut to KRW 1.48m** (from 1.85m) — an extraordinary 3x spread. Jefferies is BUY at **TP KRW 4,200,000** ("Overblown Fears Present Opportunity"). Price action for the record: **-16.2% on 07-28, -7.6% on 07-29**, ~-20% on the week; **Chairman Chey Tae-won bought 3,620 shares** on 07-30 (@jukan05). Forward pricing to watch: the same expert expects **Q3 DRAM increases to moderate to +13-18% q/q** from Q2's +30%._

_**🆕 Síntese — addendum 2026-09-01 (three sources, and for the first time the pressure on this page comes from the INSTITUTIONAL STRUCTURE AROUND THE COMPANY RATHER THAN FROM THE CYCLE).** ① **The moat is re-priced from the inside.** Every bear on this page so far has been a cycle bear (peak pricing, capex into the peak, CXMT). SemiAnalysis adds a different one: **the NVDA anchor relationship that underwrites this page's supplier-differentiation thesis is, on their estimates, a subsidy — SK hynix's 2027 HBM4 to Nvidia at ~$3.0-3.3/Gb vs $3.7-4.1/Gb for other Tier 1 buyers ("only" ~+70% y/y vs Samsung +98%, Micron +89%), traded for priority Nvidia GPU allocation to SK Group's 5GW datacenter build. "SK Hynix has left money on the table."** The consequence they draw is the one that matters here: **the SK hynix-vs-Samsung HBM margin gap contracting from ~teens pp (2026) to single-digit pp (2027)**, and Samsung ending up with *"a more favorable incremental earnings and free-cash-flow trajectory than SK Hynix."* ➤ **A relative-preference reversal against the standing page view, from an independent house with no rating and no PT — logged, not adopted. Sinal moves: `Pricing / customer mix` ⚠→✗, `Margem` ✓→⚠.** ② **The supply side surprised UP again, and Morgan Stanley now disagrees with itself by 13pp on 2027.** Shawn Kim: DRAM bit growth **25% at the start of the year → 31% tracking now → ~35% possible for 2026**, then **~25% in 2027** and *"well into the 30s"* in 2028 — against **his own firm's semicap team at +32%/+38% for 2026/27 (08-25)** and against UBS/Samsung IR saying undersupply *worsens*. Pricing decelerates on his numbers: **3Q ~+15-20% (in line), 4Q ~+5% for both DRAM and NAND**, smartphone weakest — the page had no 4Q mark at all, and MS's own Greater China exhibit corroborates at **+3-8% conventional DRAM / +0-5% NAND for 4Q26E**. ③ **Two new ceilings that are not about memory at all.** **Server units cannot grow more than ~+30% next year** because of aluminium capacitors (single Japanese supplier), 47µF MLCC, ABF substrate and MOSFETs — which closes part of the 08-20 "50-70% required server bit growth vs high-teens-to-low-20s commitments" gap from the demand side rather than the supply side. And **de-spec is now asserted flatly by the covering Asia analyst and extended to SOCAMM**, with **next year's iPhone going 12GB → ~9GB** — the SOCAMM leg landing directly on SemiAnalysis's claim that Nvidia has locked a long-term SOCAMM agreement with this company. ④ **The valuation frame is now openly contested on the same date.** JPM (09-01, on this page) likes Asian memory at *"<4x P/E with demand profiles pointing to an upside risk."* Kim argues China's entry is **structurally a derating element** — forced capex/R&D, less operating leverage, lower through-cycle margin, lower sustainable ROIC — anchored on **December 2017, when the industry earned >70% operating margins and the Korean stocks still never traded above 2x book**, versus ~4x this cycle. **And the competitive clock is specific: CXMT above [[MU]] on wafer capacity by 2028 (potential #3 DRAM), YMTC probably #2 in NAND ABOVE SK HYNIX by 2028-29, China top-3 in memory within ~3 years.** ⑤ **What did NOT change:** the >50%-of-FCF return policy and the 3Q26 detail date are independently confirmed by SemiAnalysis; the Indiana backend fab, Yongin 390-420K wfpm and the W40tn buyback marks are untouched. **The unresolved question for the next two quarters is no longer only "does HBM4 catch up before pricing rolls" — it is now also "does SK hynix get paid for HBM4 at the price the rest of the market is paying."**_

_**🆕 Síntese — addendum 2026-09-02 (one source, trade press, and it introduces a lever this page had never logged: a CHINESE REGULATORY PRICE CAP on the eSSD book, with a DATE on it.)** Every argument in this window has been about HBM — pricing, yields, base dies, who gets paid. This item is about the other half of the P&L and it is not a cycle argument at all: **SAMR's 2021 conditions on the [[INTC]] NAND acquisition BAR SK hynix from raising PCIe/SATA eSSD prices in China above the trailing-24-month pre-approval average, and the five-year compliance period expires in DECEMBER 2026** (TrendForce News, 2026-09-02, citing Yonhap News / DealSite on SK hynix's semiannual report). **China is 35%–40% of group NAND capacity in 2026 (TrendForce's own estimate), so this is a lever on a large book rather than a footnote.** ⚠️ **Two things stop it being adopted as an upgrade: the restrictions do NOT lapse automatically, and NOTHING is on the record from either SK hynix or SAMR — the removal expectation is Yonhap's inference.** And the same article carries its own offset: **group eSSD share including Solidigm fell to 21.1% in 2Q26 from 23.1% in 1Q26, with [[MU]] taking the gap (17.1% from 15.4%) and [[SAMSUNG]] holding #1 at 35.1%** — TrendForce's eSSD share basis, NOT NAND bits. ➤ **Logged as a two-sided setup and left that way: a potential PRICE lever arriving on a book where the company is losing POSITION. New Sinal row `China regulatory / eSSD position` opened at ⚠ nuança; no existing number, rating or PT on this page is superseded.**_

NOTHING ADOPTED — no SK hynix estimate, rating or PT is superseded).** **THE DE-SPEC ARGUMENT THIS PAGE HAS CARRIED SINCE 08-20 FINALLY HAS A GENERATION LABEL ON EVERY RUNG, AND THE LADDER STEPS DOWN.** ① **Read in platform order the three sources do not conflict.** Jefferies' sales desk (relaying TrendForce/ET News) puts **Rubin at 12-Hi HBM4**; Morgan Stanley's SEMICON Taiwan checks put **Rubin Ultra at 8-Hi HBM4, "around one-third fewer"**; @jukan05 asks whether **Feynman** was cut to 8-High too. **Content per accelerator rises into Rubin and then falls a third at Rubin Ultra — the inverse of the premise under this page's HBM bit arithmetic.** ② 🔴 **THE MOST CONSEQUENTIAL DETAIL IS INSIDE MORGAN STANLEY.** The `HBM4` Sinal cell's principal COUNTER-EVIDENCE has been MS's own 08-24 bottom-up model carrying Rubin Ultra at HBM4E 12-Hi. **Thirteen days later the same house's channel check says 8-Hi. The model has not been restated and the 48,618mn Gb 2027 figure still embeds 12-Hi — a frozen model and a fresh check are different artefacts and are NOT netted — but the strongest counter-leg in that cell is now partly withdrawn by its own author.** ③ **The offset changed hands, and it goes to the wrong layer for this company.** Every prior rebuttal on this page is a VOLUME offset. **MS's offset is more HBM4 BASE-DIE UNITS + HBM4e + software — LOGIC content that routes to [[TSM]] or, per the 09-05 [[INTC]] evaluation, to another foundry. Value migrates one layer DOWN the stack, away from the DRAM maker** — the same mechanism Bernstein described for NVHBM, arriving through a different door. ④ **The bull inversion is the strongest this page has carried and it comes from an independent account, not a seller:** stack heights *"were lowered to make supply more elastic"*, scarcity relocates from height to **PIN SPEED**, and per-layer value RISES because *"the opportunity cost of stacking them up and failing has grown too large."* **For SK hynix that is a pro-incumbent read — pin speed is the axis it has led on — but it also makes the HBM4 base-die redesign that delayed Rubin shipments MORE costly, not less.** ⑤ ⚠️⚠️ **A NEW CONTRADICTION AT THE RUBIN RUNG, LEFT UNRESOLVED: UBS's own house view (08-25) that NVIDIA moved VR/R300 to HBM4 8-Hi, against the 09-06 desk relay's "12-Hi HBM4 for Nvidia's Vera Rubin platform." Settleable — it is a spec, not a forecast.** ⑥ **THE COMPETITIVE BEAR LEGS ARRIVE IN ONE SENTENCE OF THE SAME RELAY: *"Nvidia is broadening its HBM supplier base and product mix"* and *"Samsung's improving HBM4 yields point to intensifying competition into 2027"* — the first attacks the [[NVDA]] anchor this page treats as the moat, the second lands at exactly the generational transition where Counterpoint already printed share at 50% from 64% y/y. Plus the first per-maker HBM WAFER scale on this page: ~150-200K wpm each at SK hynix and [[SAMSUNG]] vs [[MU]] going to ~100K wpm by year-end. ⚠️ Wafers are not bits; no share conversion made.** ⑦ **NON-FUNDAMENTAL BUT DATED: a 2026-09-18 close-of-business flow event — FTSE GEIS upweight plus an ICESEMI addition (UBS Index Analytics, SALES DESK, "not a product of UBS Research"). Logged as a catalyst, not as a view.** ⑧ **NO SINAL MOVES. `HBM4` stays where it was: the evidence on the down side has thickened and acquired a ladder, MS's counter-model has weakened, and still nobody has confirmed a shipping platform spec. THE TEST IS THE END-SEPTEMBER HBM CONTRACT ROUND: does ASP HOLD while stack height falls?**_

_**🆕🔭🔴🔴 Síntese — addendum 2026-09-15 (one source, and it is the first to put ARITHMETIC under the 2028-oversupply worry this page has carried unquantified since 09-01 — plus two SK hynix-specific structural marks).** ⚠️⚠️ **INDEPENDENT SUBSTACK AUTHOR, NOT A BROKER — no rating, no price target, no house model** (**DAMNANG (Substack)** — *"Is the 2028 Memory Downcycle Real?"*, **2026-09-15**) [Source](../Damnang/2026-09-15%20-%20Is%20the%202028%20Memory%20Downcycle%20Real-.md). ⚠️ His own disclaimers, carried because they bound the use: *"for informational purposes and is not an investment recommendation… scope is DRAM (HBM and general purpose DRAM), and it does not cover NAND… checked as of September 14, 2026. The author may hold the stocks mentioned."* The configurations and price asks are sourced by him to **industry conversations, not to company disclosure**, and the scenario outputs are **sensitivities under his assumptions, not forecasts**. ① **THE DECOMPOSITION.** Combined 2028 profit for the three memory makers, on his LTA-adjusted price path: **+7%** if 8-Hi stays dominant · **+4%** with 4-Hi *and* sufficient accelerator expansion · **0%** with 4-Hi *and* insufficient expansion · **−12%** only if LTA renegotiation lands on top of both. His one-line conclusion: *"fewer layers alone do not bring a downcycle in 2028, but fewer layers, multiple semiconductor manufacturing bottlenecks, and LTA renegotiation together can."* ② 🔭 **THE PIVOT TEST, AND IT IS THE MOST DIRECTLY RELEVANT THING HERE FOR A HOUSE RUNNING ~40% OF WAFERS ON HBM.** Reducing layers yields **1.5x the stacks at 8-Hi and 3x at 4-Hi** off the same wafers, enabling **~26%** and **~60%** more accelerators respectively — against a CoWoS/substrate/power ceiling he assumes at **20-40%, and uses at 35%**. So at 8-Hi **HBM remains the binding constraint and no wafers spill into commodity DRAM**; only the 4-Hi branch frees wafers. ⚠️ That is the cleanest rebuttal on this page to the 09-09 Communacopia *"the trade ratio starts to shrink"* line — but it is falsifiable from the other side: **if the next-bottleneck headroom is materially above 40%, his 8-Hi "no spill" result breaks. The 35% is an assumption, not a measurement.** ③ 🔴🔴 **SK-HYNIX-SPECIFIC #1 — the mix decision this page logged name-by-name now has a mechanical rule behind it.** Allocation is decided on **revenue per wafer**, and **HBM consumes ~3-4x the wafers of commodity DRAM per GB** (Micron's own HBM3E-vs-DDR5 disclosure). He cites the **June-2026 report that SK hynix would DELAY converting HBM3E lines to HBM4 and run them for DDR5 instead** as exactly this arithmetic in action. And his explanation for why HBM ships at all: *"The reason is contracts"* — today's HBM is the **2H-2025 annual contract volume signed before the price reversal, with volume commitments and prepayments attached**, so suppliers *"fill the contracted volume but do not add beyond it."* ④ 🔴🔴 **SK-HYNIX-SPECIFIC #2 — the LTA perimeter, which is the only leg of his model that produces a negative.** He reports SK hynix is pursuing a **5-year commodity-DRAM contract with [[GOOG]]**, alongside [[SAMSUNG]] tying **60-70% of total DRAM+NAND capacity into 3-5yr LTAs** and [[MU]] at **~20% of volume with a price floor**. ⚠️ **Renegotiation of those LTAs is the single switch that turns his 0% branch into −12%.** ⑤ **SUPPLY DATES: Yongin Y1 first cleanroom Feb-2027 · [[MU]] Idaho first wafer 2H-2027 · [[SAMSUNG]] P5 operation 2028.** And the timing mechanism matters more than the capacity — he expects **DRAM contract prices to peak and turn around 3Q-2027**, *"because buyers who built inventory in 2026 will slow their purchases when they see equipment arriving at new fabs in the second half of 2027"*. **The turn is triggered by observing the equipment, not by the wafers arriving** — watchable and dated. ⑥ **PRICE: suppliers asking +70 to 140% in the 2027 negotiations**; his base is **2027 HBM ~2x 2026** and **2028 flat per GB** (range **+10-20% to −15%**), because HBM4E stack prices are *"almost proportional to the number of layers"* with a **~10% premium for lower layer counts** — cutting layers does not cut price per GB. ⚠️ His named downside lever is **[[SAMSUNG]]'s qualification** bringing three-way competition forward. ⑦ His own falsification thresholds, stated unprompted: a downcycle needs **demand growth below 10% AND supply growth above 30%** (CXMT commodity expansion or early fab ramps). Two monitorable tripwires. **NOTHING ADOPTED — no SK hynix estimate, rating or PT is set by this source, which has none.**_

_**🆕🔴🔴 Síntese — addendum 2026-09-15 (four sources: one Bernstein research note off Bernstein's London SDC, two SALES-DESK relays, one independent research house plus an X counter-view. NOTHING ADOPTED — no SK hynix estimate, rating or price target is superseded; none of these sources carries an SK hynix rating or PT).** ① **The clearest item is a competitive divergence, and it CONFIRMS rather than surprises.** BESI's management tells Bernstein that Samsung is *“the most vocal memory player on HB adoption for HBM”* while **SK Hynix “remains more cautious and is still testing HB, extending use of mass reflow”** (2026-09-15). That is the third independent channel — after the company's own packaging material (hybrid bonding **not** for HBM4E) and DAMNANG (2026-03-28) — to place SK hynix as the conservative and Samsung as the aggressor. **The question this page should now carry is not WHETHER SK hynix is conservative but WHETHER CONSERVATISM PAYS:** extending MR-MUF protects the yield moat and defers tool capex, but concedes queue position if HBM migrates. Logged as a new Sinal row at **✓ confirma**, with the ambiguity left open. ② **The supply side of the book is restated as CLOSED by a desk with no position in it.** Rothschild (Rowan Adley, TMT specialist sales, 2026-09-15) has the three makers **“largely locked in 2027 DRAM/HBM capacity”** with NAND buyers at **60-70% of requested quota** — the SAME fill rate this page has carried since early August, unchanged six weeks later. **`Demanda` and `Pricing / customer mix` Signals HELD; the ration has not loosened.** ③ **The de-spec debate moved in two ways, neither of them settled.** It spread PRODUCT CATEGORY — JPM's desk reports client concern *“across HBM and now even NAND”* on DeepSeek V4.1's KV-cache reduction, with **bandwidth and interconnect, not capacity**, named as the binding bottleneck (Duncan Wagner relaying Mixo Das and Gokul Hariharan, 2026-09-15). And it acquired a SECOND NAMED HOUSE ON THE OTHER SIDE — SemiAnalysis argues 4-Hi wins on supply (**“4-Hi more than doubles the cubes you can harvest per wafer”**, and *“the loudest calls for less memory are coming from the labs themselves”*), while **TrendForce argues 4-Hi isn't enough** (@jukan05, 2026-09-14). For this name the wafer-harvest point matters more than the bit count: **more cubes per wafer is a SUPPLY RELEASE from the same capacity, not a demand cut** — and it is the mechanism, not the conclusion, that is new. ④ **Housekeeping, but worth the row:** SK hynix's own **$26.5bn July ADR listing** is now the template a third party uses for the next memory capital raise ([[KIOXIA]], $10bn+, Spring 2027) — our resolved 07-10 mark confirmed from outside. ⚠️ Three of the four sources are desk commentary or independent research; none is an SK hynix disclosure._

_**🆕🔴🔴🔴 Síntese — addendum 2026-09-18 (three sources: one Deutsche Bank industry supply model, one 66-page BofA Korea industry overview, and one GS-hosted [[SAMSUNG]] MANAGEMENT meeting carried here as a read-through. NOTHING ADOPTED — no SK hynix rating, price target or house estimate is superseded; BofA REITERATES marks already on this page and the other two carry no SK hynix rating at all).** ① **THE 2028-DOWNCYCLE WORRY GETS ITS FIRST PROPER SUPPLY-SIDE ANSWER, AND IT COMES FROM A HOUSE THAT JUST RAISED SUPPLY.** Deutsche Bank lifted wafer-start growth to **+9% / +17% / +20% / +17% / +10%** for 2026-30 (from +7% / +15% / +17% / +14% / +8%) and STILL models DRAM demand/supply at **108% / 112% / 110% / 102% / 96%** — worse in 2027-28, equilibrium in 2029, oversupply only "potentially" in 2030. ➤ **The page has carried the 2028 worry unquantified since 09-01 and part-quantified since the 09-15 DAMNANG piece; this is the first named house to put a full wafer model behind a two-year deferral of the date.** ② **AND SK HYNIX IS THE BIGGEST ADDER INSIDE IT: 545k → 1,060k WSPM, +515k over 2025-30, more than [[SAMSUNG]] (+420k) or [[MU]] (+420k).** Fab by fab: **M15X +80k** (ramp 2H26 at 40k, 80k in 2027), **Yongin Fab 1 +360k by 1H30** with the **first of six cleanrooms in HVM in 1Q27**, **Yongin Fab 2 ~200k** (construction July 2027, cleanroom mid-2029), **Yongin 3-4 beyond 2033.** ✅ **The 1Q27 Yongin date is now triple-sourced (DB, DAMNANG 09-15, the semicap tool-in read).** 🔴 **And the line that should be on every 2028 bear's slide: "all 4 Yongin fabs expected to be completed by 2033, 12 years ahead of original 2045 target."** ⚠️ **That is genuinely two-sided and is logged as such — it is the supply ammunition AND the reason DB still has the market short, because even at that pace the wafers arrive behind the bits.** ③ **DE-SPEC: THE STACK-HEIGHT STEP-DOWN IS NOW CORROBORATED BY A THIRD HOUSE WHILE ITS EARNINGS CONSEQUENCE IS CONTESTED BY TWO — AND THE PAGE NOW SEPARATES THOSE TWO QUESTIONS.** DB names the SKU and the cause (*"[[NVDA]] has pivoted its mainstream Rubin Ultra SKU from 12-Hi HBM4e configurations to HBM4 / HBM4e 8-Hi stacks"*, to secure **late-2027** delivery, on **12-Hi yield/validation issues**), which is the independent second source for JPM's 09-09 *"8Hi sales mix 55-60% higher vs 12Hi"* — yet DB cuts its HBM bit CAGR only **+40% → +39%**, calling de-spec *"primarily a tactical workaround… rather than a true contraction in long-term memory demand."* BofA, two days later, models **HBM revenue from US$35bn (2025) to high-US$200bn / ~US$300bn (2030) with "no de-spec assumed"** printed on the exhibit. ➤ **Two named houses, two days apart, opposite treatments of the same risk. Neither adopted.** ④ **BofA REITERATES BUY / W3,000,000 / ADR US$268 AND FINALLY SHOWS THE METHOD — WHICH TURNS OUT TO BE AN EXPLICIT PEER DE-RATING.** The PO is **8x 2027-28E EPS**, *"slightly below our fair multiples for global DRAM peers (9-11x) given elevated share-price volatility in 1H26 and July"*, with 2026 excluded from the window because *"investors may be concerned about a potential downturn (2027 or 2028)"*; the **ADR US$268 is a 20% premium at FX W1.34k/USD**, justified purely by the persistent post-IPO premium and US comparables' higher multiples. ⚠️🔴 **AND THE HOUSE'S OWN EXHIBIT SUPPLIES THE BEAR: 2027E EPS growth of +3%, the lowest of any major name in BofA's Korea coverage, against +493% in 2026E — captioned *"Hynix's low growth should be a matter of 2026 high base."* The paired 5.0x 26E vs 4.9x 27E P/E is the same fact.** ➤ **This is the most concrete answer yet to the standing "why only 5x" question on this page: on BofA's own numbers there is essentially no 2027 earnings growth to re-rate into — which is precisely why BofA has to argue the re-rating comes from durability rather than from growth.** ⑤ **TWO NET-NEW HARD NUMBERS AND ONE DATED CATALYST: 711mn shares ex-treasury (from BofA's SK Square build, fair mcap W2,133tn = 711mn × W3.0mn); SK Square's 20% stake at a 40% holdco discount with management targeting ~30%; and BofA naming as an upside risk "Hynix's earlier/stronger-than-expected SPECIAL DIVIDEND announcements in 2H26 vs normally late-Jan" — a possible pull-forward of the January cadence into this calendar year.** ⑥ **THE COMPETITOR READ-THROUGH, AND ONE UNCOMFORTABLE COMPARISON.** [[SAMSUNG]] management (GS, 09-18) guides **2027 tighter than 2026** on a **very low fulfilment rate**, says new capacity is *"mainly allocated for HBM"* in both DRAM and NAND, and puts the **unbound share of its own capacity at 30-40%** — implying **60-70% under LTA versus SK hynix's own ">50% of bits sold under LTA."** ⚠️ **On the face of it Samsung is the MORE locked-up of the two, which inverts how the LTA-optionality bear is usually framed here — but the bases differ (forward capacity vs bits sold) and the two are deliberately NOT netted.** 🔴 **The genuinely competitive item: Samsung's 4nm is "very tight due to demand from HBM4 base die", it is considering adding 4nm in Korea, and 2nm lines for HBM5 base dies are under review — the in-house base-die gap against SK hynix's TSMC-routed (and reportedly [[INTC]]-evaluated) supply is now node-dated by the side that has it.**_

_**🆕🔴🔴🔴 Síntese — addendum 2026-09-22 (six sources: UBS research 09-22, a GS-hosted MANAGEMENT meeting 09-22, Mirae research 09-07, two J.P. Morgan industry notes 09-22, and Bernstein's CXMT initiation 09-21 as a competitive read-through. ONE RATING/TARGET MOVED — Mirae W2,800,000 → W3,100,000 — and ONE HOUSE IS NET-NEW TO THIS PAGE: Goldman Sachs, Buy, TP W3,500,000).** **THE WINDOW'S REAL CONTRIBUTION IS THAT THIS PAGE CAN FINALLY STACK THE WHOLE STREET ON ONE BASIS, AND THE STACK SAYS THE DISAGREEMENT IS NOT ABOUT 2027 — IT IS ABOUT WHAT MULTIPLE TO PAY FOR IT.** On 2027 earnings the houses are within a wide but explicable band (UBS W610,284 · Mirae W529,561 · GS W491,918 · consensus W474,958), and every one of them is ABOVE consensus. On the target they use four completely different instruments — **GS a 9.0x average P/E, Mirae a 4.1x P/B struck at a 10% discount to [[MU]] and [[KIOXIA]], UBS a 4.25x NTM P/BV justified by a 50% long-run ROE, JPM a 17.4% WACC DCF** — and land W2.75m to W3.5m, a 27% spread on the same company in the same week. ➤ 🔴 **THE SINGLE MOST INFORMATIVE ARTEFACT IS THAT JPM'S OWN BASE-CASE DCF (W3,040,000) SITS ~11% ABOVE ITS OWN PUBLISHED TARGET (W2,750,000). A house does not usually publish that unless it thinks the target is the conservative number. Paired with its stated view that *"the market is pricing-in an extremely bearish sentiment between a bear and extreme bear scenario"*, the read is that even the Street's own bull-side targets are not the bull case.** ✓ **WHAT GOT CONFIRMED, AND IT IS THE SUPPLY LEG THIS PAGE'S WHOLE THESIS RESTS ON:** management told GS its fabs are *"nearly fully utilized"*, M15X's only meaningful capacity goes to **HBM**, and **Yongin opens February 2027 but output does not start until END-2027** — independently corroborated by UBS (*"equipment into one clean room every six months at… Y1, starting February 2027"*, M17/NAND tooling **2H28**). **Two houses and the company now agree that the 2027 wafer adds do not produce 2027 bits.** That is why capex going up (UBS W63tn/W71tn '27/'28E; Mirae W71.7tn '27F) is not, on these books, a 2027 supply event. ⚠️ **WHAT GOT WORSE IS THE SHARE LINE, AND IT IS THE THIRD INDEPENDENT VERSION OF THE SAME CALL: UBS has SK hynix at 48% of industry HBM bits in '26E falling BELOW Samsung in '27E (37% vs 41%, MU 22%), and JPM has SEC+MU combined going 54% → 59%. The company is not losing the cycle; it is losing the crown inside it.** And UBS names a mechanism that should worry the moat argument more than a discount would: **NVDA cut the HBM4 pin-to-pin requirement from 11.7Gbps to 10.6**, so *"the gap to Samsung's pricing is likely limited."* **The differentiation was relaxed by the customer, not competed away by the rival.** 🔴 **TWO NEW, DATED TESTS ARE NOW ON THE BOARD AND BOTH LAND INSIDE SIX WEEKS: (1) THE OCTOBER HBM PRICING ROUND — FundaAI reports market chatter of *"around 50%"* for SK hynix's 2027 HBM price increase against 70-80% for Micron, and says negotiations continue in October; UBS models +62%. A 50% print would be the first hard evidence that the NVDA anchor is costing SK hynix price, not just share. (2) THE 3Q26 CALL — management has committed to finalising the next shareholder-return round there, and JPM has made returns *"a pre-requisite for memory stocks to re-rate."*** ⚠️ **AND ONE QUIET DOWNGRADE TO THE PAGE'S OWN CONFIDENCE: management's first LTA number is *"at least 50% eventually would not be impossible"* — SOFTER than the 60-70%-of-volume and 70%-of-capacity figures this page has been carrying from UBS and JPM. Those are industry/structural estimates, not SK hynix disclosures, and should stop being read as company-confirmed.** ✓ price · ✓ supply/cleanroom · ⚠ **share (losing #1 in HBM bits on UBS's numbers)** · ⚠ **HBM price (October round unresolved, 50%-vs-62%-vs-70-80% three ways)** · ✗ **LTA coverage is less established than this page assumed** · ⏳ 3Q26 call = capital return._

Both are on the page; NEITHER is adopted.** ➕🔴 **09-16/09-18 — A THIRD SUPPLY MODEL ARRIVES AND IT LANDS ON THE DEMAND SIDE'S SIDE, WHICH IS NEW.** Deutsche Bank ("Global Memory: Chipping away at industry supply imbalance", 2026-09-16) RAISES its wafer-start growth forecast to **+9% / +17% / +20% / +17% / +10%** for 2026-30 (from +7% / +15% / +17% / +14% / +8%) — and still models DRAM demand/supply at **108% / 112% / 110% / 102% / 96%**, i.e. **the imbalance WORSENS in 2027-28, reaches equilibrium only in 2029 and turns to oversupply at most "potentially" in 2030.** DB has DRAM bit demand at **+21% CAGR** 2025-30 with servers at **+43%**. The MS bit-supply objection above is not withdrawn, but the page now has a supply model that raised its own supply numbers and still concludes shortage through 2029, plus a duopoly competitor's management saying the same thing from the demand side. The 2028-downcycle worry carried since 09-01 now has a named house pushing the date to 2030.** ⚠️ **The offsetting item is in the same model: CXMT raised to 300k → 775k WSPM (+475k), the largest single-vendor add, overtaking [[MU]] by 2030 — the China leg is the biggest swing factor in whether even the 2030 date arrives.** |

So the delay is a share/mix negative on the [[NVDA]] franchise, a near-term bit cushion, **and** incremental industry supply feeding the cycle-durability bear — all three at once) · **⚠→✓ UPGRADED 2026-08-03** (the ramp was late and it cost the quarter; the supply chain now DATES the volume arrival to late-Aug/Sep and confirms SK hynix — not the peers — as the volume HBM4 vendor the Rubin ramp is built around) · ⚠️ **NEW 2026-08-21, LOGGED AS AN UNCONFIRMED RUMOUR AND DELIBERATELY *NOT* SCORED: a claim that runs the OTHER way on HBM CONTENT PER GPU.** DAMNANG (Substack — **independent author, not a broker**) reports Rubin Ultra's HBM configuration **"is being reviewed downward… toward HBM4 8-Hi,"** supply constraint the direct cause, citing TrendForce (Aug-4: multiple configurations under evaluation, final spec NOT set), The Information (192GB and 256GB prototypes) and SemiAnalysis (main SKU to HBM4 8-Hi 192GB, compute held, bandwidth slightly higher). ⚠️⚠️ **THE AUTHOR'S OWN DISCLAIMER, CARRIED VERBATIM: "The 8-Hi configuration for Rubin Ultra is UNCONFIRMED information based on industry checks, and the final specification may change." THE SINAL IS NOT MOVED ON THIS — a downshift in Rubin Ultra stack height would be a mix-DOWN in HBM content per GPU against this page's core franchise, which is precisely why an unconfirmed spec rumour must not be scored as evidence.** ➡ **His own offset: under supply constraint the per-GPU bit decline does NOT pass one-for-one into HBM revenue — lower stack height raises the number of stacks buildable from the same DRAM and stacking capacity, and [[NVDA]] can absorb it into extra GPU shipments (his sensitivity: 12-Hi→8-Hi ≈ 1.084x stack-yield improvement on a 98% per-core-die yield assumption, ~1.63x effective output ceiling, total HBM revenue ~81.5% of plan at 8-Hi priced 50% of planned HBM4E / ~98% at 60% — ⚠️ HIS WORDS: "sensitivities to stack count, not shipment forecasts," NOT adopted as estimates). The durable half is competitive: if 8-Hi's life extends, differentiation moves OFF high-stack qualification — where this page's HBM4 yield moat sits (~80-85% vs [[SAMSUNG]] ~60%) — and ONTO yield, capacity, supply stability and BASE-DIE functionality, i.e. the same direction as the custom-base-die + CXL-tier expansion in Current state.** (DAMNANG (Substack) "HBM Density Peak: Why AI Value Is Moving From Memory to Connectivity", 2026-08-20.) | **➕ 08-25 — THE AXIS OF THE DEBATE HAS MOVED FROM ‘IS THE RAMP LATE’ TO ‘HOW MANY LAYERS DOES THE RAMP SHIP’, AND THE PAGE NOW HAS A BROKER ON EACH SIDE.** UBS, at its Korea Summit: *“de-specing is viewed as POSSIBLE considering supply constraints. We believe NVIDIA HAS MOVED TO HBM4 8-Hi FROM HBM4E 12-Hi FOR VR/R300. This is NOT affecting overall demand (MORE SiPs, LESS CONTENT PER SiP)”*, with HBM demand “very strong in 2027” and possibly undersupplied “into 2030 or over” (UBS “First Read: SK Hynix — Key Takeaways from UBS Korea Summit 2026”, 2026-08-25). **Against it, Morgan Stanley's 08-24 bottom-up HBM model STILL CARRIES Rubin R200 at HBM4 12-Hi and Rubin Ultra at HBM4E 12-Hi** (Morgan Stanley “AI Supply Chain: Google ASIC…”, 2026-08-24), and **Mizuho/SemiAnalysis go further the other way** (“OEMs could cap future HBM content at 4-8Hi”; TPUv8 HBM3e cut to 8-Hi; Mizuho “SemiAnalysis: Memory De-Spec…”, 2026-08-24). ➤ **The 08-20 DAMNANG entry above logged the 8-Hi switch as an EXPLICITLY UNCONFIRMED rumour; five days later a Buy-rated house states it as its own view and [[SAMSUNG]]'s DS spokesperson confirms customers are considering exactly this for SUPPLY reasons. The rumour has been upgraded to a CONTESTED BROKER VIEW — it is still NOT a confirmed platform spec, and one house's model disagrees outright. THE SINAL IS NOT MOVED ON THIS; the September pricing-negotiation finalisation (UBS's own named catalyst, ~Sep 18) is the first real test.** ⚠️ **Note the direction of the P&L risk is genuinely two-sided and this page already holds the arithmetic: DAMNANG's 12-Hi→8-Hi sensitivity puts the effective output ceiling at ~1.63x and total HBM revenue at ~81.5-98% of plan depending on 8-Hi pricing — sensitivities, not forecasts.** **➕ 09-06 — THE LADDER GETS A GENERATION LABEL ON EVERY RUNG, THE COUNTER-LEG OF THIS CELL IS PARTLY WITHDRAWN BY ITS OWN AUTHOR, AND THE SINAL IS STILL NOT MOVED.** **Morgan Stanley · Charlie Chan, Daniel Yen, Daisy Dai, Tiffany Yeh, Lucas Wang, Henry Zhao, Ethan Jia ("Greater China Semiconductors: SEMICON Taiwan 2026 key takeaways — we need more", 2026-09-06): *"our recent supply chain checks suggest that **Rubin Ultra CPU is highly likely to use 8-Hi HBM4, around one-third fewer than 12-Hi in Rubin**. But by **increasing HBM4 base-die units and migrating to HBM4e, plus some software optimization**, total memory bandwidth should be sufficient."*** ⚠️⚠️ **MS's own wording is "Rubin Ultra ***CPU***" — stack height is a GPU/package attribute and Rubin Ultra's CPU is Vera. QUOTED VERBATIM, NOT CORRECTED, AMBIGUITY UNRESOLVED.** 🔴 **THIS MATTERS FOR THE CELL BECAUSE THE MS 12-Hi MODEL WAS THIS CELL'S PRINCIPAL COUNTER-EVIDENCE:** the sentence above reads *"Morgan Stanley's 08-24 bottom-up HBM model STILL CARRIES Rubin R200 at HBM4 12-Hi and Rubin Ultra at HBM4E 12-Hi."* **Thirteen days later the same house's channel check says 8-Hi at Rubin Ultra. The model has not been restated and the 48,618mn Gb 2027 figure still embeds 12-Hi — a frozen model and a fresh check are different artefacts and are NOT netted here — but the counter-leg is materially weakened.** **AND THE OFFSET IS NOT THE ONE THIS CELL HAS BEEN DEBATING: not "more units", but more HBM4 BASE-DIE UNITS + HBM4e + software — LOGIC content routing to [[TSM]] or another foundry, not DRAM bits at SK hynix.** ⚠️⚠️ **A NEW CONTRADICTION AT THE RUBIN RUNG, LEFT OPEN: UBS's own view above (VR/R300 moved to HBM4 8-Hi, 08-25) against **Jefferies Asia TMT specialist SALES · Conor O'Mara (09-06, SALES DESK COMMENTARY NOT RESEARCH, relaying TrendForce / ET News): *"rapid adoption of 12-Hi HBM4 for Nvidia's Vera Rubin platform."*** Same rung, incompatible marks.** ✅ **THE BULL INVERSION, FROM AN INDEPENDENT ACCOUNT (@jukan05, 2026-09-06 — NOT A BROKER): stack heights *"were lowered to make supply more elastic"*, scarcity relocates from height to **PIN SPEED**, and per-layer value RISES because *"the opportunity cost of stacking them up and failing has grown too large."* He also asks — UNCONFIRMED, and contradicting Mizuho's 768GB HBM4e 16-Hi mark — whether **Feynman** was cut to 8-High.** ➤ **FALSIFIABLE TEST RECORDED, THREE SEPARABLE LEGS: (a) does HBM bit demand per accelerator actually DECLINE at the Rubin Ultra transition; (b) does HBM ASP HOLD while stack height falls — the direct test of the pin-speed inversion; (c) do base-die units and HBM4e offset the step, and does that offset land on DRAM revenue or on the logic layer? **THE SINAL IS NOT MOVED: still no confirmed shipping platform spec.** The end-September HBM contract round remains the first real test.** ➕🔴🔴 **09-16 — THE SKU IS NAMED, THE CAUSE IS GIVEN, AND IT CORROBORATES JPM'S 09-09 MIX WARNING FROM A DIFFERENT HOUSE.** Deutsche Bank: *"several sources have noted that [[NVDA]] has pivoted its mainstream Rubin Ultra SKU from 12-Hi HBM4e configurations to HBM4 / HBM4e 8-Hi stacks"* — to secure delivery volumes for **late 2027**, amid severe HBM shortages and **yield/validation issues on advanced 12-Hi layers**. ✅ **This is the independent second source for JPM's 09-09 line on this page that *"overall 8Hi sales mix (55-60%) is likely to be HIGHER vs 12Hi with strategic customers opting to use more 8Hi for HBM4 and next-gen HBM4E"* — and it supplies the REASON (12-Hi yield) that JPM did not give.** ⚠️ **But DB refuses to treat it as demand destruction: de-spec is *"primarily a tactical workaround to manage severe DRAM supply bottlenecks rather than a true contraction in long-term memory demand"*, and DB cuts its HBM bit CAGR only from +40% to +39% through 2030. BofA the same week models HBM revenue to high-US$200bn / ~US$300bn by 2030 with "no de-spec assumed" printed on the exhibit.** ➤ **SINAL STILL NOT MOVED — no confirmed shipping platform spec — but the shape of the debate has changed: the STACK-HEIGHT step-down is now corroborated by three houses while the EARNINGS consequence of it is contested by two. Those are different questions and the page now separates them.** 🆕🔴🔴 **⚠→⚠ 2026-09-22 — THE PRODUCT IS ON TRACK; THE *SHARE* IS THE ROW THAT IS NOW MOVING AGAINST MANAGEMENT.** On the product, management's Q2 line holds: GS reports **HBM ASP and margins up 2H vs 1H as HBM4 takes a greater revenue share into year-end**, and Mirae confirms **HBM4 up to 48GB with 12-high in mass production and 16-high in customer qualification**, plus a completed **48GB 16-high HBM3E** (die thickness −20%, inter-die spacing −50% vs 12-high). **But UBS now models SK hynix at 48% of industry HBM bit shipments in '26E and FALLING BELOW SAMSUNG IN '27E at 37% (Samsung 41%, Micron 22%)** — the most explicit numerical version yet of the crossover this page has carried since 08-18, corroborated the same week by JPM (*"SEC and MU's combined share could rise close to **54% this year, followed by 59% next year**"*). ⚠️ **And JPM characterises SK hynix's own strategy in a way that is consistent with ceding bit share deliberately: *"SKH (strategic customer being NVDA and balanced bit production across different tiering of HBM layer products with a **potential focus on 8Hi**)"* — versus SEC prioritising *"the largest HBM share in the market."* Whether that is a choice or a constraint is the open question; the bit-share number is the same either way.** ⚠️ **One live contradiction to carry: JPM pushes **16Hi adoption to "2029E at the earliest"** (and keeps **TCB over hybrid bonding** as a consequence) while SK hynix has 16-high in customer qualification today. Capability ≠ adoption; both stand.** |

That is a structurally different thing from a discount: it narrows the product differentiation itself, and it explains the pricing convergence without needing a concession story.** UBS still models **SK hynix HBM ASP +62% y/y in '27E** ($1.79 → $2.89 → $3.21/Gb). ⚠️⚠️ **AND THERE IS NOW A DATED FALSIFICATION POINT PLUS A CONTRADICTORY THIRD MARK: FundaAI (independent, 2026-09-22) reports *"recent market reports have indicated that **SK Hynix's HBM prices next year might only increase by around 50%**"* against its own 70-80% for [[MU]], and says **"SK Hynix will continue negotiations in October."** UBS's +62% sits between them. His historical parallel: US invention (IBM 1966) → Intel in the 70s → Japan in the 80s → US embargo/agreement → Korea out-invests through the 90s and is #1 by 1998; the paradigm-shift trigger this time is AI rather than geopolitics. **④ 🔴 CXMT SPECIFICS (Charlie Chan, Greater China semis): 2Q gross margin 87.6%** — which he reads as evidence that CXMT's pricing follows the global commodity price and that yields must be very high (**mature products already above 90%**); **fab expansion is largely OFF-BALANCE-SHEET**, funded by government and sovereign PE, with process nodes licensed to affiliates that can still buy foreign equipment — so *"if you just look at the financial report [it] is very very hard to see the prediction of the capex and capacity expansion"*, but equipment-vendor checks show **~100k wafers/month added per year**. **HBM: the current module runs at 5,600MHz against a roadmap target of 8,000MHz, and 12-Hi is reached by WAFER-ON-WAFER — two layers stacked six times, 12-Hi = 24GB — needing only DUV plus a hybrid bonder, NO EUV.** **Bear case to the whole thesis = a DUV export restriction; bull case = a 3D DRAM breakthrough; base case = the transitional 2.5D VCT (vertical channel transistor) / 4F² structure entering *small* production by END-2028**, on which equipment vendors have *"no visibility"* and which Chan calls *"a wild card… too early to say it's impossible and too early to say it's going to be successful."* **CXMT has also disclosed memory LTAs with Chinese CSPs — which Chan notes is exactly the argument memory investors use to move the valuation from cyclical P/B to secular P/E.** On pricing: **3Q saw a 5-10% discount to global prices at the Chinese government's request** (*"show some support to the country"*), described as temporary. **⑤ ON EUV AND WHEN IT ACTUALLY BINDS (Kim): you do not need EUV until 1b** — *"that's what Micron did not use EUV even 1-alpha"* — 1b is roughly 12nm on DRAM half-pitch and CXMT is thought to be at **16nm**, so EUV is a **beyond-2028/29** worry for CXMT. They can **lower memory capacity per accelerator** or reallocate system budget."* So *"**HBM price and GB per accelerator have to be read together**"*: ASP up with content de-spec partially offsets dollar content growth. From HBM4 it switched to working with [[TSM]], and the current mass-production product uses TSMC's 12nm-class process. NOT a company disclosure, no timeline, no volume split. Do not treat the Intel award as made. Track it via SKH's own HBM4E qualification commentary and INTC's foundry customer disclosures.** |

**THE CREDIT LOGIC, which is genuinely informative about how the AI buildout is being financed: (1) *"Credit fundamentals of SK hynix are **solid**… we have been OW as we anticipate rating upgrades from BBB+ to single-A"* — but *"this story has **largely played out**, as Moody's and S&P have already upgraded to **A3/A−** (Fitch likely to follow) and the curve has compressed meaningfully (the '30s have tightened 20bps to SOT+60)."* (2) **The relative-value problem: SK hynix is *"rated up to SIX NOTCHES BELOW the Big Four US hyperscalers (S&P: AA− to AAA), but the curve is trading SIMILAR to the latter"*, and *"**US participation in SK hynix is heavy, with ~50% of its bond issue in September 25 allocated to the US**"* — so as US hyperscalers widen (**over 40bps YTD vs JULI +6bps, due to heavy AI bond supply**), SKH gets dragged.** (3) **The macro overhang: APAC (ex-China) DC capacity ~3x to **53GW by 2030E** generating **US$95bn of DC bond supply over 2027-30E**, priced *"~200bp wider than Asian TMT names."***
➜ **The read-across for equity holders is not the rating, it is the funding cost: the marginal AI datacentre bond is repricing the entire Asia TMT curve, and SK hynix is the most US-owned, most tightly-traded name in it. **Contrast [[TSM]], which JPM initiates at Neutral partly because **83% of its Jun-26 issue went to APAC investors** — ownership structure, not fundamentals, is what separates them.** |

🔴🔴 **THE SECOND-ORDER READ INVERTS THE NAIVE BEAR CASE, AND IT IS ARITHMETIC RATHER THAN OPINION: *"Now that CXMT has successfully made HBM3E, **a significant portion of its DRAM wafer capacity will likely be DIVERTED toward HBM3E production. Doesn't that mean COMMODITY DRAM SUPPLY is about to get even tighter?**"* (@jukan05) — corroborated by the wafer-intensity point from the same corpus day: *"The memory trio is selling HBM at an extremely low price **when you take into account the WAFERS CONSUMED to produce DDR-equivalent bits**"* (@zephyr_z9). **HBM burns far more wafers per bit, so every Chinese wafer converted to HBM3E removes a multiple of that from the commodity pool. CXMT is ~9% of the DRAM TAM. Working conclusion for this page: near-term BULLISH commodity DRAM, only medium-term bearish HBM — the two effects do not arrive together.**
⚠️ **AND THE MARGIN TRAP, because both numbers circulated the same day: TrendForce reported *"**CXMT's Q2 gross margin hit 87.6% — rivaling Micron and SK hynix**"* and JPM reported CXMT 1H26 sales **Rmb150.3bn (+874% y/y)**, profit **Rmb117.1bn**, **OPM 78% vs −27% in 1H25**, US$22bn = **9% of the US$245.5bn 1H26 DRAM TAM**. **The corpus supplies the deflator: CXMT's margin is high BECAUSE it barely sells HBM, not despite it. A like-for-like GM comparison against Micron / SK hynix is a MIX ARTIFACT.** (Same failure mode this wiki logged on 08-19: find the decomposition before judging a number.) **Also: CXMT 16GB LPDDR6 in qualification and now in mass production for a Xiaomi handset, on a timeline the Chinese press puts roughly level with the incumbents — against JPM's read of a persistent *"2-3 year technology gap"* on the prior node. 🔴🔴 **NVHBM — JPM DIRECTLY CONTRADICTS THE KOREAN-PRESS CLAIM THAT [[SAMSUNG]] HAS THE SOCKET: *"**Contrary to the news speculations, we believe the NVHBM project is NOT EXCLUSIVE TO A SINGLE MEMORY MAKER with the product spec NOT BEING FINALIZED YET.** It remains unclear which supply-chain vendor would take a higher value share in the customized logic die, but as we see more HBM customization projects emerging from GPU/ASIC vendors, we expect **memory makers' HBM manufacturing capabilities to be FURTHER TESTED with the market starting to view HBM as a TRUE CUSTOMIZED PRODUCT more so than a commodity** (i.e. compatibility between the core die and the logic die, logic die performance/sourcing method, yield, etc.)."*** **The spec [[NVDA]] claims for NVHBM vs HBM4E: *"up to **30%+ more memory bandwidth**, frees up **25%+ packaging area** for additional XPU capabilities, and lowers **up to 15% of power usage**"*, on a **customized base die**, with JPM dating customisable HBM to **late-27 onwards**.** ⚠️⚠️ **HELD AGAINST THE COMPETING MARK, NOT NETTED: Korean media (via @jukan05, corpus, 2026-08-28) reported *"Samsung Takes Early Lead in Nvidia's 'NVHBM' Following HBM4"*, with Samsung developing an **8-high HBM4E** for NVDA's preference for *"FEWER stacked DRAM layers and HIGHER speeds rather than continuing to increase stack height."* ➜ **If NVDA is genuinely moving to lower stack heights at higher pin speeds, SK hynix's stacking/TSV lead matters less and speed/logic-die execution matters more. That is a threat to this page's core moat argument and it is the reason to watch NVHBM sourcing closely rather than dismissing the Samsung report.**
🔴 **THE VALUATION AND FLOW ARGUMENT, WHICH IS THE ACTIONABLE PART: *"Memory stocks are **down 6% in the past 3M (vs. SOX: −11%)** on brewing concerns of **memory content cuts, LEVERAGED ETF UNWINDS (especially for Korean memory makers), and lack of catalysts after shareholder return policies were released**… we sense investors are particularly concerned about the **easing S-D imbalance in 2028** and the subsequent decelerating growth (after high base in 2026)… That said, we believe **investors are DISCOUNTING CY27E memory earnings strength with Asian memory makers (SEC, SKH, Kioxia) trading at <4x P/E** with demand profiles pointing to an upside risk, reinforced by NVDA's FY28 guidance upward revision… **We advise investors to ACCUMULATE memory shares as the market begins to price-in 27E earnings.**"*** **The four named catalysts: *"a) **shareholder return policy update at the 3Q26 earnings conference (Oct-end)**, b) **CSP capex update**, c) **LTA PRICING DISCLOSURES**, and d) **China competition update**."*** ⚠️ **<4x P/E is a CY27E multiple on a peak-cycle earnings estimate — the bear case is precisely that CY27E is the peak. JPM's answer is NVDA's FY28 +70% framework, not a rebuttal of the 2028 S-D concern.** (J.P. Morgan Asia · Jay Kwon, Sangsik Lee, 2026-08-31) |

We believe NVIDIA HAS MOVED TO HBM4 8-Hi FROM HBM4E 12-Hi FOR VR/R300. The focus for the first phase will remain on DRAM.” Overall capex up y/y in 2027, UBSe Won61tn (+30% y/y), and “stay ELEVATED FOR SOME TIME.”** ⑦ **PROCESS ROADMAP — net-new and specific: “SK Hynix MAY CONTINUE 6F2 TIL 0a NM (we believe still TBC vs. 4F2), then move to 4F2 for several nodes TIL 3D DRAM.” NAND: capacity “could be added in DALIAN on FLOATING GATE process tech, which over time may move to 240L FROM 144/192L”; M17 (NAND) “expected to start MP in 1H29.”** ➤ The Dalian line corroborates the 08-24 @jukan05 relay and the 08-25 DIGITIMES report below with the process detail neither had. ⑧ **VALUATION AND THE ESTIMATE GAP, which is the real reason to hold the note: UBS values SK Hynix at 4.32x NTM P/BV on a LONG-TERM ROE of 49.2% and CoE of 11.4%. Named POSITIVE CATALYST, approximate date SEPTEMBER 18, 2026: “monthly smartphone sell-through numbers, PRICING NEGOTIATION FINALIZATION, NT supply chain color on GPU demand, further updates on shareholder returns.”** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/UBS_000660KS_First_Read_SK_Hynix_Key_Takeaways_from_UBS_Korea_Summi.html) |

Both logged; neither adopted.** ➤ **Why this is the sharpest competitive item of the week for SK hynix: an explicitly profit-insensitive share push by the #1 DRAM maker is the single mechanism that could break the pricing discipline this page's whole ‘memory is no longer cyclical’ leg rests on. It also cuts against the 08-25 Jefferies/Sherman claim that Hynix is LATE at NVIDIA — if Samsung is buying share with capacity rather than winning it on product, the Sherman read and this one are describing the same event from opposite ends.** **INDUSTRY-BULLISH OFFSETS FROM THE SAME CALL, and they corroborate UBS almost line-for-line:** demand booking/fulfilment rate **“about 60 to 70 percent”** across products including HBM; unmet 2026 demand **pushed into 2027**; S/D **“tighter in twenty seven, EVEN TWENTY EIGHT… expected to continue AFTER twenty eight”**; and on de-spec, that customers are considering lower stack heights **because of supply constraint, not weak demand** (*“we haven't seen any kind of slowdown signal so far”*). **Two further items that matter architecturally:** Samsung floated **3D HBM — stacking HBM directly on the GPU/CPU logic die, eliminating the interposer link** — to customers, *“and some leading GPU and AI players are quite interested”*; and it framed customised HBM as a foundry-like lock-in (*“if they start a design and place an order, definitely they cannot change the vendor”*). ➤ **Same destination as SK hynix's CPO/CXL/CMM-Ax work already on this page, approached from the opposite direction: in-package 3D integration vs out-of-package optical disaggregation.** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Samsung_IR_call_BTG_raw_notes_25_ago_26.html) |

scenario skew 2.4:1 (Rmb166.10 upside / **Rmb4.00 downside, −92%**, on industry ASP −15% + 1x P/BV — the tail if the cycle breaks). ⚠️ **The allocation mechanism that cuts AGAINST Hynix's mix: "the top three DRAM suppliers have REALLOCATED RESOURCES TO SUPPORT HBM, which has resulted in STRONGER PRICE HIKE MOMENTUM FOR DDR THAN HBM" — UBS DDR ASP $0.40/Gb ('25) → $1.61/$2.24 ('26/27E), a 137% CAGR, vs HBM $1.52 → $1.74/$3.13, 43% — i.e. the commodity leg Hynix is LEAST weighted to is the fastest-repricing leg** (consistent with mgmt's own "2027 HBM pricing reset seeks to bring profitability IN LINE with DDR," 08-07 NDR row above). **The leg that cuts FOR the top-3: CXMT's ASP path is modelled BELOW industry ($1.33/2.14/2.17 vs $1.61/2.24) yet smartphone-maker checks find "LIMITED PRICE DISCOUNTS for CXMT's DRAM vs global peers" — CXMT prices under the incumbents' umbrella, it does not undercut it.** HBM leg: CXMT ramping **HBM3 MP (Feb-26 news) + first domestic HBM3e sampling**, HBM = 0.4% ('26E) → 2% ('28E) → 7% ('30E) of its bits, with WAIC checks showing China AI-accelerator cos "increasingly willing" to co-develop HBM with CXMT — a China-HBM localisation marker, not a near-term share event. Server bits 4.9 → 29.5bn Gb '25→28E (China server share 12% → c.20%); customers ByteDance/Alibaba/Tencent + one domestic-CSP LTA; Q126 OM already 70%. Undersupply until ≥Q2'28 reaffirmed. (UBS · Jimmy Yu / Nicolas Gaudois / Xinlei Li, "CXMT — Capitalising on the memory supercycle", initiation, 2026-08-07) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/20260807_UBS_688825-CH_CXMT_Corporation_-Capitalising_on_the_memory_supercycle.html) |
| 08-07 | 🔴 **UBS 2Q26 NDR KEY TAKEAWAYS (Gaudois) — THE BUYBACK QUESTION THE PRINT LEFT OPEN IS NOW DATED BY THE COMPANY: an announcement "WITHIN 3Q26"** | capital / supply / pricing | **bull** | **★ THE CAPITAL-RETURN ITEM, AND IT DIRECTLY RESOLVES THE "no buyback" MISS THIS PAGE LOGGED ON THE 07-28 PRINT: "SK Hynix indicat[ed] that considering ELEVATED FCF GENERATION, IT WOULD NOT WAIT FOR THE END OF THE PERIOD FOR THE CURRENT PROGRAMME (END '27) TO ANNOUNCE INCREMENTAL MEASURES… SK Hynix having disclosed AFTER THE CLOSE ON AUGUST 7TH that they will be MAKING AN ANNOUNCEMENT WITHIN 3Q26. Management is considering BOTH SHARE BUYBACKS AND SPECIAL DIVIDEND (we expect MORE OF A BUYBACK BIAS), and will look at making this SUSTAINABLE LONGER TERM."** **UBS sizes the fuel: FCF of Won186tn/Won306tn/Won357tn in '26E/'27E/'28E, a 2H26 buyback at Won12tn, and over time a move toward returning 50% OF FCF. "For us, AN ANNOUNCEMENT ON SHAREHOLDER RETURN IS THE NO. 1 POSITIVE CATALYST AHEAD."** ⚠️ **Note the disclosure sequencing: the company's own after-close 08-07 statement is the hard fact; the buyback bias, the Won12tn size and the 50%-of-FCF path are all UBS estimates. Consistent with — and independently sourced from — BofA's "50% of FCF, W40tn+ buyback vs W20tn+ dividend" split already logged below, but this is the first time the TIMING comes from the company.** 🔴 **LTAs GET A SCOPE AND A TENOR, which is what the LTA debate on [hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md) has been missing: "A MEANINGFUL PORTION OF REVENUES WILL BE TIED UP TO LTAs, and >50% OF BITS SOON is a reasonable assumption. LTAs are OVER 5 YEARS, and cover DDR BUT ALSO IN SOME CASES HBM AND NAND."** ⚠️ **">50% of bits under 5-year contracts" is the most consequential structural fact in the note and it cuts both ways — it is exactly the mechanism UBS credits for higher through-cycle ROE and exactly the mechanism Jefferies calls "extenuating the cycle" at softer ASPs. HBM and NAND sitting inside some LTAs is NET-NEW; this wiki had been treating LTAs as a DDR/NAND phenomenon.** **SUPPLY/DEMAND & PRICING: "the market is expected to get EVEN TIGHTER IN 2027, with perhaps DRAM MORE SO THAN NAND… Demand upside continues to materialise for AI/DC across SERVER DDR AND SSD." SK Hynix "may grow DRAM bit shipments OVER 25% IN 2026 (UBSe +26%) but LIKELY AT A LOWER RATE IN 2027 (UBSe: 20%)," and "expects PRICING UP QoQ IN 3Q26/4Q26."** 🔴 **HBM DE-SPEC — MANAGEMENT'S OWN FRAMING, and it is the cleanest company-side answer to the bear this wiki has carried since 08-06: "Whilst NOT COMMENTING on possible de-specing, IF IT WAS TO OCCUR IT WOULD REFLECT SUPPLY SHORTAGE, NOT A CHANGE IN DEMAND." And on the reset: "2027 PRICING RESET IS STILL SEEKING TO BRING [HBM] PROFITABILITY IN LINE WITH DDR."** ⚠️ **That second clause matters for the "HBM-vs-EPS paradox" on this page: management is targeting HBM economics UP toward conventional DRAM, not defending a premium.** **CAPACITY/CAPEX: "Capex will LIKELY GO UP IN 2027 (UBSe: +30% YoY). The pace of YONGIN INFRASTRUCTURE BUILD IS BEING ACCELERATED WITH AN INITIAL FOCUS ON DRAM. M17 CONSTRUCTION STARTED FOR NAND WITH PRODUCTION BY 2029. DALIAN still has some floor space to fill (UBSe +15K WPM CAPACITY IN '27)."** ⚠️ **"Yongin accelerated, DRAM first" is a modest hawkish revision against the ~$38-39bn / June-2029-first-cleanroom timeline logged below — Korean supply is trending faster, not slower, even if none of it lands inside the 2026-28 window. Dalian +15K wpm in '27 IS inside the window and is the only near-term supply item in the note.** **ESTIMATES CUT, RATING AND PT HELD: Buy, PT Won3,000,000 (spot Won1,495,000 at 06-Aug); ADR PT US$210 (was US$204 — FX only). '26-29 OP forecasts lowered ~5% on average on slightly reduced NAND ASPs ("contract pricing negotiations A TOUCH SOFTER in the near term") and '27 HBM bit shipments cut to 21bn Gb (was 23bn), only partly offset by higher HBM ASPs (+64% in 2027, was +61%). FY27 EPS Won547,875 (was Won576,936, −5.0%); FY26 Won390,164 (was Won396,943, −1.7%). UBS is 12% ABOVE '27E CONSENSUS, "which we believe HAS NOW FACTORED IN LTA ADJUSTMENTS" (UBSe '27 OP +11.7% / EPS +14.4% vs cons).** ⚠️ **Valuation marks for the drawdown record: shares DOWN 49% FROM THEIR 22 JUNE PEAK, trading 1.80x NTM P/BV "discounting a LONG-TERM ROE OF 20.5% VS OUR ESTIMATE OF 41.1%"; UBS values the shares at 3.61x NTM P/BV on a 41.1% LT ROE and 11.4% CoE. Scenario band: Won3.30m upside (+121%) / Won3.00m base (+101%) / Won800k downside (−46%).** (UBS Global Research · Nicolas Gaudois / Jimmy Yoon / Luke Yoo, "SK Hynix — 2Q26 NDR Key Takeaways", 2026-08-07) |
| 08-07 | 🔴 **A ~US$38-39BN KOREAN FAB COMMITMENT — and the dates are the point: FIRST CLEANROOM JUNE 2029, WFE INSTALLATION 2031** | supply / capital | **mixed** | **The disclosure: SK Hynix plans a ~$38BN expansion of its Korean chipmaking facilities. NONE OF THIS IS SUPPLY RELIEF FOR THE 2026-28 WINDOW UNDER DEBATE. It is the reason UBS can forecast DRAM undersupply "THROUGH AT LEAST 2Q28" without contradiction, and it corroborates [[LRCX]] CFO Bettinger's constraint framing logged on 08-06 ("greenfield clean room construction takes ~3 YEARS REGARDLESS OF GEOGRAPHY").** ⚠️ **The counterweight, from Jefferies the same day and pointing the other way on NAND: "[[LRCX]]'s strong performance and RECORD NAND REVENUE indicate that 'SOMEONE'S ADDING CAPACITY' in NAND, LIKELY THE KOREANS, EARLIER THAN PREVIOUSLY ANTICIPATED. This raises concerns about potential future OVERSUPPLY." A long-dated greenfield commitment and a near-term equipment-led NAND capacity add are not the same thing — both logged, neither netted.** **Capital returns, per BofA: SK Hynix expected to return 50% of FCF "WITH A GREATER EMPHASIS ON BUYBACKS VS CASH DIVIDENDS, SUCH AS W40TN+ VS W20TN+."** **And the 2Q ASP gap this page logged on 08-06 now has its recovery number in BofA's model: DRAM ASP "UP ONLY 30% QoQ VS 45-70% AMONG PEERS" in 2Q, with "A RAPID 3Q ASP RECOVERY OF 25% AND MORE MEANINGFUL HBM4 SHIPMENTS."** (Bloomberg via JPM "Chips for Breakfast" · Joshua Meyers / Jay Kwon, 2026-08-07; DIGITIMES 2026-08-07; BofA Global Memory Tech · Simon Woo, 2026-08-06/07; Jefferies Semis EPS Debrief · Blayne Curtis, 2026-08-07) |
| 08-04 | **JPM · Jay Kwon — "Major AI Capex Durability Trade beneficiary available at cheap valuations" · OW, ▼PT CUT TO W2,750,000 (from W3,000,000), timeframe extended to Jun-27 (from Dec-26)** | valuation | **mixed** | **★ A PT CUT WITH RAISED EARNINGS — read the mechanism, not the headline, because it is entirely a de-rating of the multiple: "we reset our target multiple to 7x F12M P/E (FROM 8x) and extend our timeframe to Jun-27." Spot W1,564,000. EPS was RAISED 3-16% through FY26E-28E vs the May-26 marks (higher FY26E NP mainly on Kioxia stake-sale impact; FY27E-28E +3-7%), on (i) higher 2H26 DRAM ASP (prev. +10%/+5% for 3Q/4Q), (ii) higher NAND ASP off a stronger 2Q base, (iii) HBM ASP up on the ongoing S-D mismatch, (iv) higher bit production on capacity pull-in + faster migration. FY26E-28E capex raised to W187trn (from W175trn). FY26E-28E EPS CAGR +26%.** ⚠️ **Kwon's own explanation for cutting the multiple while raising numbers is the most useful sentence for this page's durability debate: "the road to a multiple normalization (FTM P/E from <4x to 6x or above) WILL TAKE TIME in the current market environment. Reflecting HIGHER OPPORTUNITY COST vs. the past (i.e. crystal clear capital allocation plan delivery and stronger memory consumption in AI computation thesis required to cast away the negative market narrative IN THE THIRD YEAR OF THE AI CAPEX RACE)."** — i.e. the earnings are not in dispute; the market's willingness to pay for them is, and JPM is now pricing that patience explicitly. **Recommendation stays accumulate: "risk-reward remains favorable on a 12 month horizon and we would take the recent correction as an opportunity to add positions."** 🔴 **THE ANSWER TO THE OPEN LTA QUESTION THIS PAGE FLAGGED ON 08-02, and it is partial: "SKH reported successful LTA with TEN KEY CUSTOMERS (JPMe: OVER HALF OF TOTAL VOLUME largely composed of CSP and AI memory customers) and there appears to be VARIOUS PRICING TERMS (floor price, take-or-pay, prepayment, ceiling price WITH/WITHOUT price hike terms)."** ⚠️ **So SK hynix's book is heterogeneous where [[SAMSUNG]] disclosed a uniform asymmetric collar (sub-5% down vs 10-20%+ up on 60-70% of sales). "Ceiling price with/without price hike terms" means SOME of Hynix's contracts cap the upside — which is the thing Samsung's terms explicitly do not. Contract details are NDA-bound, so this remains the most important undisclosed contractual fact in the complex; it is now better characterised, not resolved.** **On the despec debate, Kwon takes the constructive side and quantifies it: "Key customers' recent decision for content adjustment seems to be OPTIMIZATION INITIATIVES OVER DEMAND CORRECTION (i.e. total HBM procurement demand is UNCHANGED, similar to the SOCAMM content noise). Migration back to 8Hi will ENABLE GREATER OUTPUT to help reduce the S-D gap from 30-40% to a 10-20% gap (SHORTAGE MARKET NATURE INTACT)" — with HBM bit shipments +30%/+55% y/y in FY26E-27E.** ⚠️ **He has been deliberately conservative on HBM pricing and stays there: "we have been advocates of MORE REALISTIC HBM ASP JUMPS next year (<40%) vs bullish expectations (50-100% increase) and pencil in +35% blended HBM ASP increase for FY27E"** — against **HBM OPM above 70% next year, ahead of FY24-25 levels**. **On the 2Q ASP miss: "we take it as a ONE-OFF QUARTER with multiple mix changes and price hike recognition timing issues coming together," expecting server DRAM ASP to move up more than peers in 3Q and blended DRAM ASP above the industry average on HBM4 contribution. 3Q26E/4Q26E OP W78trn/W93trn — "largely in-line with recent consensus."** **NAND: industry-leading mix with eSSD at 70-80% of NAND sales and QLC traction ahead of peers.** **Next catalyst named: "a BUYBACK could demonstrate clear evidence on how management perceives the duration of the up-cycle… JPMe: within the next couple of months"** — consistent with the 08-03 ADR-disclosure-constraint explanation on this page. (JPM · Jay Kwon / Sangsik Lee / Neelay Kamath, 2026-08-04) |
| 08-04 | **BofA · Simon Woo — "SK Hynix appears undervalued vs super-cycle earnings — COVERAGE RESUMED" · Buy, ▼PO W3,000,000 (from W3,590,000); ADR INITIATED at US$250** | valuation | **bull** | **★ Coverage resumed with a LOWER PO but an unchanged bull case — and, like JPM the same day, the cut is a multiple decision: PO W3.0mn on 8x 2027-28E P/E (vs the prior W3.59mn on 9x). Woo's framing of the 8x: "the mid-point between the previous peak cycle (4-5x in the 2017-18 cloud boom) and the long-term historical average (low teens)… slightly below our fair multiples for global DRAM peers of 9-11x."** 🆕 **NEW INSTRUMENT ON THE PAGE: BofA initiates the ADR at PO US$250, "at a premium of 20% to the local shares, reflecting both the post-IPO observed premium and consistent with [[MU]]'s 2027-28E P/E premium above Hynix's local-share multiple."** **Five stated reasons for Buy: (1) long-term strong chip orders from US Big Tech; (2) STABLE ASP in 2027-28 ("no hard landing"); (3) super-cycle-level OP (W300tn+ annual run-rate from 3Q26); (4) dominant position in high-end memory (HBM, LPDDR5, eSSD); (5) P/E of only ~4x on 2027-28E EPS.** **The 2Q26 actuals as BofA states them: sales W79.3tn (+51% q/q, +257% y/y), OP W60.5tn, OPM 76% (DRAM 81% / NAND 65%), NP W93.9tn incl.
