OP slightly below consensus on a limited DRAM ASP increase (+30% q/q vs its own and consensus 40-50%) "due to some PRE-AGREED QUARTERLY CONTRACT PRICES."** 🔴 **The most consequential claim, and it directly contradicts the standard bear reading of LTAs: "we see a quick resumption of normal contract-price negotiations and more favorable new ASP agreements with US Big Tech customers, EVEN UNDER LTAs (ASP IS NOT REALLY FIXED AND IS MOSTLY NEGOTIABLE EVERY QUARTER, EVEN FOR FIVE-YEAR CONTRACTS)."** ⚠️ **Hold that against JPM's same-day disclosure that some Hynix LTAs carry CEILING PRICES — both cannot be fully true across the whole book. The news is THE 2027 ALLOCATION ROUND ALREADY CLOSED, QUIETLY, IN JULY AND AUGUST… Deposits are now universal. Even small buyers who'll never get a 3-5 year LTA are WIRING CASH UPFRONT to hold a slot. VOLUME IS LOCKED, PRICE IS NOT — 2027 DRAM and NAND ASPs get set NEAR SHIPMENT. Anyone assuming LTAs cap the upside HAS IT BACKWARDS: the suppliers kept the optionality. Fill rates running 60-70% of what buyers asked for" (@The_AI_Investor, 2026-08-03).** Corroborated by @jukan05/@aleabitoreddit citing DigiTimes: **2027 DRAM/HBM capacity fully booked ahead of schedule; NAND not as tight but bookings expected filled by end-August; customers allocated only 60-70% of requested volumes; buyers are not publicising their allocations for fear of shrinking them.** ⚠️ **"Volume locked, price not" is the single most important structural claim in this cluster and it sits ON BofA's side of the LTA argument (quarterly negotiability) and AGAINST JPM's ceiling-price disclosure. Third-party/FinTwit sourced — treat as channel colour, not company-confirmed, but note three independent accounts converge on the 60-70% fill rate.** ⚠️ **The one negative datapoint in the cluster, and it is Hynix-specific: Counterpoint's Q2 DRAM share figures show "SK Hynix's revenue-based market share is only about 1 PERCENTAGE POINT AHEAD OF MICRON'S… attributed to HBM3E PRICES ACTUALLY FALLING THROUGH RENEGOTIATIONS rather than rising, along with the impact of DELAYS IN THE LAUNCH OF HBM4. Another factor is that SK Hynix signed its LTAs EARLIER than its competitors, which means Hynix's fixed LTA prices are reportedly LOWER than those of its rivals" (@jukan05, 2026-08-04). That is the first-mover-disadvantage version of the LTA question and it is the cleanest bear datapoint on the page this week.** |

| 08-04 | **Invest Like the Best podcast · Gavin Baker (Atreides) — "what would you do if you were the CEO of Hynix?"** | supply | **bull** | ⚠️ *Informal podcast commentary (~2026-08-04), not verified financials.* **The LTA game theory argued from the buyer's seat, and the conclusion is that the floors hold: four buyers matter at scale ([[AMZN]]/Trainium, [[GOOG]]/TPU, [[AMD]], [[NVDA]] "much bigger than everybody else combined"), memory-per-flop is "the single most important thing you could do to increase token output per unit of compute" ("the axis that is dominating all others" — no demand elasticity so far), and accelerator market shares "for the next several years are going to be determined by supply-chain allocations and what you have pre-purchased." Therefore: "if you break your LTA and in the next two or three years FOR ANY REASON leverage shifts back to the memory guys, YOU'RE OUT OF BUSINESS… they break the volume agreement and give the volume to your competitor" — and "the game theory almost even holds in a severe oversupply situation." The old-cycle contrast is [[AAPL]]-as-monopsonist: "even if they super screw Hynix, Micron will of course take them… This is just different."** [The direct buy-side rebuttal to the "buyers describe the option to stop paying" thread on [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md).] **Asked directly what he'd do as CEO of SK hynix: "I'd do the exact same thing Nvidia is doing right now… I would be going to the buyers of GPUs, Trainiums, and whoever, and saying I'll PARTICIPATE IN THE NVIDIA CREDIT WRAPPER" — put up some cash from today's cash flow into the financing structure and take "a cut of the ongoing revenues," i.e. a ROYALTY ON RECURRING COMPUTE REVENUE, "a logical extension of the LTAs where they're trading upside for durability… I'm sure our friends at Blackstone and Apollo are suggesting some variant of this to the memory companies."** A structural-capital-allocation idea to watch against the SCA/prepayment structures already on this page — the direction of travel he describes (memory suppliers converting spot upside into contracted, financeable, royalty-like streams) is the same one the 16-agreement SCA disclosure started. (Invest Like the Best · Gavin Baker, Atreides, ~2026-08-04) [Source](../_equity_calls/Overall/2026-08-04_GavinBaker_ILTB-AI-repricing.md) |
| 08-03 | **Fubon · Sherman Shang / Arthur (hosted by Jefferies), "industry highlights & checks update" — THE PRIMARY CALL behind the relay row below, and read directly it is a WIN on the item that matters** | supply | **bull** | ★ **THE VOLUME SENTENCE, VERBATIM: "from the Rubin HBM side, in the supply chain, we currently only see a SMALL VOLUME OF MICRON AND SAMSUNG'S HBM. And the RUBIN WITH HYNIX HBM4 WILL START TO RAMP UP AT LATE AUGUST OR SEPTEMBER WITH A BIGGER VOLUME, WHICH IS WHY IN Q4 WE WILL SEE A HIGHER VOLUME OF RUBIN."** ⚠️ **The causal direction is what upgrades this from sequencing trivia to a franchise datapoint: Fubon is not saying Hynix is late *within* the Rubin ramp — he is saying THE RUBIN RAMP IS BACK-HALF WEIGHTED BECAUSE HYNIX'S HBM4 ARRIVES THEN. Rubin production of 200-300K units in Q3 steps to 700-800K in Q4 on that arrival. So the "Hynix behind Samsung and Micron" headline (relay row below) and this are the same fact seen from two ends: the peers are qualified first at token volume; SK hynix is the vendor the platform's volume curve is built around. On this page's own terms that is the moat argument intact, not impaired — and it re-dates the Q2 "HBM4 ramp delay" revenue miss as a LATE-Q3/Q4 revenue event rather than a lost socket.** _(machine transcript; ASR garbles names — `Fubon`←"Foobon", `Blackwell`←"Black Wheel"/"Blackwall", `CoWoS`←"CoOS".)_ **The rest of the memory slice, all Fubon: HBM4 cost to NVDA at $30-32/GB from $17-18/GB HBM3-class — ~1.8x — ⚠ dated "in 2026" in the presentation and "in 2027" in Q&A, ambiguity left standing; a Rubin GPU at $78-80K on an assumed 75-80% NVDA chip GM; Rubin rack BOM $7-8m vs $3-3.5m GB200-class. Content: the four-compute-die Rubin Ultra heard at ">1,024 GB/chip" is CANCELLED and the surviving two-die part is "around 384 GB" (B200 ~192GB, B300 ~288GB) — spec unchanged, shipping SKU lighter. And the ODM rebuttal of the memory-downsizing rumour, in the buyers' words: a module cut "was discussion" but is "unfeasible for AI server," with NO 2027 spec change seen "including the Google, AWS," and purchasing teams "rising the capex for buy and sell for the GPU."** ⚠️ **One item on the debit side that belongs to the whole complex, not to Hynix alone: "the thermal design change AND THE HBM BASE DIE ISSUE" are the two named causes of the ~two-month Rubin push-out — an HBM execution problem is now inside the industry's most consequential 2H26 slip. Hynix is the volume HBM4 vendor into that platform, so it owns a share of that risk as well as the upside.** See [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md) · [MU.md](MU.md) · [SAMSUNG.md](SAMSUNG.md). [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_08_03_sherman_update_03_08_26.html) |
| 08-03 | **Jefferies/Fubon group call (Sherman Shang) via Conor O'Mara — HYNIX IS BEHIND SAMSUNG AND MICRON IN THE RUBIN CHAIN, BUT OWNS THE VOLUME** | produto | **mixed** | ⚠️ **THE SEQUENCING MATTERS AND IT IS UNFLATTERING NEAR TERM. Shang, verbatim to the Jefferies desk: "he has seen SAMSUNG AND MICRON HBM RELATED PRODUCTS FOR NVIDIA BUT NOT HYNIX, THAT WILL COME LATER IN THE YEAR." O'Mara headlines it "HYNIX BEHIND SAMSUNG IN HBM."** **On the group call the same day the fuller version is more balanced: "only see SMALL VOLUME of [[MU]] and [[SAMSUNG]] HBM in supply chain" while "RUBIN WITH HYNIX'S HBM4 WILL ONLY START RAMPING IN LATE-AUG/SEP WITH BIGGER VOLUME" — i.e. the competitors are qualified first but at small volume, and Hynix is still the volume vendor, arriving ~a month out.** **The pricing context is strongly favourable regardless of order: NVDA's HBM4 cost goes to US$31-32/GB from US$17-18/GB for HBM3e, and "for other GPU and custom-ASIC projects… US$35-36 per GB, meaning the HBM cost will DOUBLE in 2027."** ⚠️ **Two things to hold open until the ramp is visible: whether the late start costs Hynix allocation share on the first Rubin builds, and whether [[NVDA]]'s ~10-15% price advantage over the ASIC field is being funded out of Hynix's margin or out of volume commitments. Neither is answered.** Supporting: **[[NVDA]] Rubin FY26 volumes cut to 1mn from 1.5mn (pushing HBM4 demand right), and Rubin Ultra HBM content is UNCHANGED despite the 4-compute-die cancellation.** Offsetting, and it is the bigger picture for memory: **Jefferies' ODM checks find "NO DESPEC IN MEMORY FROM NVIDIA," with procurement managers budgeting at $7-8m per rack** (Arthur Lai via O'Mara, 2026-08-03). (Jefferies/Fubon · Sherman Shang via Conor O'Mara, 2026-08-03) |

| 08-01 | **Susquehanna · Mehdi Hosseini (Tech Hardware weekly) + UBS positioning — node migration over QLC, the CXMT defence, and the month's drawdown quantified** | produto | **mixed** | **① NAND STRATEGY: SK hynix "is similarly FOCUSED ON NODE MIGRATION, targeting its 321-LAYER TECHNOLOGY TO REACH 50% OF DOMESTIC PRODUCTION BY YEAR-END to support surging eSSD demand"** — i.e. it is playing the middle path between [[SAMSUNG]]'s aggressive 2Tb QLC ramp (bits to more than double in 2H26; V10 V-NAND mass production August 2026) and [[KIOXIA]]'s explicit refusal to chase QLC as "too costly for broad deployment." ⚠️ **Layer count is a cost-per-bit lever that works across TLC and QLC alike, so this is the lowest-regret of the three positions — but it also means SK hynix is not making the density bet that captures the AI-storage share if Samsung is right about the requirement.** **② THE CXMT DEFENCE IS BEING FOUGHT ON PRODUCT, NOT POLICY: "SK hynix plans to DIMINISH CXMT'S GOLDEN PERIOD with the only way it knows — TECHNOLOGICAL SUPERIORITY, as it WINS OVER CHINESE CUSTOMERS WITH LPDDR6."** ⚠️ **Read alongside the counter-reports in the same link flow: "CXMT is betting on CUSTOMIZED DRAM to circumvent U.S. sanctions on HBM; KOREAN SUPPLIERS PLAYING SAFE COULD MISS OUT ON A MAJOR OPPORTUNITY," and "Innosilicon's LPDDR6 memory IP lands at a major Korean DRAM maker." The first frames SK hynix's caution on custom DRAM as a strategic risk rather than prudence; the second suggests external IP is being licensed into the LPDDR6 effort. Both are press relays with no company confirmation — logged as the shape of the China-competition narrative this week, not adopted. Also: "CXMT EVICTED HUAWEI-LINKED ENGINEERS from its R&D facility," and "[[SAMSUNG]], SK hynix chase DIVERGING AI DEALS" (BusinessKorea).** **③ THE DRAWDOWN, QUANTIFIED — and it is the largest in the memory complex: SK hynix −34% m/m, against [[MU]] −28% and [[SAMSUNG]] −22%, with the KOSPI then +17% ON FRIDAY ALONE** (UBS · Robert Ruple, "Tech Musings", 2026-08-01). ⚠️ **A −34% month on a company whose operating margin printed in the 72-76% range is not a fundamentals move, and the mechanism is now documented: SK hynix was one of the two positions "the entire hedge fund community knew" Situational Awareness was massively long, and the name traded through the liquidation ahead of the news (see [themes/macro-cycle](themes/macro-cycle.md) and [[ANTHROPIC]]). The +17% KOSPI Friday is the other side of the same flow. | 07-30 | Bernstein · David Dai / Carmine Milano / Juho Hwang ("ASML: Best Idea 3Q26") | produto | **bull** | **Read-through only — an [[ASML]] Best-Idea note with no SK hynix rating or PT. The relevance is that Bernstein names SK hynix as the industry's LEAD High-NA adopter, ahead of every logic player except Intel, and argues DRAM — not logic — is where High-NA economics work best.** Verbatim: **"SK hynix has the clearest early signal: the company announced in September 2025 that it had assembled ASML's TWINSCAN EXE:5200B, the first production-oriented HNA system, at its M16 fab for next-generation DRAM development."** The economic argument behind that lead is specific and structural: **"the strongest economic case for HNA is in DRAM, as DRAM dies are smaller than logic dies and therefore do not require stitching. This translates into higher throughput and a lower cost per exposure, making HNA adoption easier to justify economically."** Bernstein's adoption roadmap (Exhibit 10) has **DRAM HNA beginning with SK hynix and [[SAMSUNG]] at the 1d node**, while [[TSM]] waits until ~A10 at the end of the decade. **The node-migration arithmetic underneath is the part to hold, because it is a capex-intensity claim, not a tool-vendor claim: DRAM EUV exposures per node run ~2 at 1a → 3 at 1b → 5 at 1c → 8 at 1d, rising into the low teens at 0b/0c; DRAM litho intensity moves from <20% at 1Y/1Z → 26% at 1c → ~30% at 1d; and total DRAM EUV exposures compound 4.5x from 3.4 MWPM (2025) to 26.3 MWPM (2030).** ⚠️ **Read honestly, that is a rising cost-per-bit-of-capacity headwind for SK hynix as a litho BUYER, at the same time as it is a demand tailwind for ASML** — the note is written from the vendor's side and does not cost it from the memory maker's P&L. The offset Bernstein offers is process simplification: HNA single-exposure replaces LNA multi-patterning, eliminating masks, litho-etch loops and cut steps, with **litho capex/opex ~1.2x but non-litho cost declining**, so total process cost can fall even as the tool bill rises. Also relevant to the 4F² debate: Bernstein frames the 6F²→4F² transition around 0a as **"a temporary easing of scaling requirements rather than a departure from the long-term trend"** of shrinking features — i.e. it does not read 4F² as a break in the EUV-layer trajectory. Demand context: ASML guided FY26 memory system sales **+75% y/y vs +25% for advanced logic**, with memory now ~50% of system sales (from ~30% historically) — management's word for the DRAM setup was a **"perfect storm"** of node migration and volume capacity adds at once. (Bernstein · David Dai, 2026-07-30) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/BERN_258544.html) |
| 07-31 | **BofA · Simon Woo / Dai Shen / Vivek Arya — Global Memory Tech weekly** | supply | **bull** | **★ The LTA mechanics, and they invert the common bear reading of high contract coverage. "LTA does not fix memory chip price… lot of room to raise contract price every quarter but there is LIMIT FOR PRICE CUT." Specifically: Samsung said 60-70% OF ITS MEMORY SALES WILL BE UNDER LTA, but with ASYMMETRIC terms — a price-cut FLOOR of sub-5% down q/q against price INCREASES broadly open at 10-20%+. And on tenor: Samsung's LTAs (mainly for US Big Tech) are mostly 5-YEAR ROLLING, so a 6th year can be added right after or before the 1st year completes.** ⚠️ **So LTAs here are not price locks — they are DOWNSIDE COLLARS WITH UNCAPPED UPSIDE on a rolling tenor. High LTA coverage is therefore bullish for realised ASP stability rather than a cap on it, which is the opposite of how rising contract coverage is usually read at this point in a cycle.** **Spot: sentiment positive into August on three drivers — (1) chip order increases for inventory restocking; (2) many Tier-2 OEMs resuming smartphone/PC launches for Sept/4Q DESPITE high memory cost; (3) China domestic smartphone shipments bottoming out — only 17MN UNITS IN JUNE vs a normal 25-30mn, with lean channel inventories. ⚠️ Note the DRAM/NAND divergence in the confirmed July contract prints: TrendForce July DRAM contract price UP LOW-TEENS % M/M = 30-50% Q/Q, but FLAT FOR NAND.** **On China supply, the reason it is not showing up in spot: "minimal impact from China local chipmakers' capacity expansion — China-branded memory NOT COMMONLY TRADED IN THE SPOT MARKET; even Korea/US/Japan chips not much available for white-box OEMs."** Adjacent confirmation that the AI-server pull is broad: **SEMCO and LG Innotek both guided bullishly on AI MLCC and FC-BGA, with 2Q OP more than doubling y/y, stronger LTA orders, LARGE PREPAYMENTS funding new fab construction, and contract price increases through 2H and 2027 — and all three top MLCC makers (SEMCO, Murata, Taiyo Yuden) have raised prices.** |

BofA hosted a Korea ETF expert call the same week: Korean ETF AUM ~W500tn across 1,000+ funds = **30%+ of KOSPI trading value**, with 2x leveraged ETFs acknowledged as an ongoing volatility contributor, partly offset by new leveraged-ETF regulation and pension inflows into active ETFs. |
| 07-27 | **Morgan Stanley · Shawn Kim (via "Playing the AI Infrastructure Dip", Byrd et al)** | supply | bull | **SK Hynix (000660.KS) Overweight, PT KRW2,600,000 vs KRW1,919,000 close** on MS's Korea AI-linked thematic list (prices as of 2026-07-24; the stock is shown up 194.8% YTD, so this is a momentum name being reiterated, not a contrarian call). The supporting memory framework (Kim / Cindy Huang, "Chipflation — Navigating A Memory Crisis"): a **multi-year bottleneck across DRAM, HBM, NAND and HDD**; **server share of DRAM demand 37% (2023) → 59% (2028e)** and **enterprise SSD 18% → 65% of NAND**; **DRAM prices up more than six-fold over the last year**, breaking the multi-decade decline; **LTAs "now absolute necessities to secure capacity,"** with producers locking in long-term orders and prices to "establish a stable high-margin model and mitigate the industry's cyclical volatility." The allocation dynamic that favours the HBM leaders specifically: "**suppliers are prioritizing HBM, server DRAM and enterprise SSD demand tied to AI infrastructure, while traditional consumer and industrial memory users are increasingly coping with significantly higher prices, longer lead times and weaker bargaining power**" — and MS's 2027 sufficiency math has **PC and smartphone memory falling ~15%/~12% short of demand** (−58mn PCs, ~134mn smartphones; a 19bn Gb residual shortfall, 13% of demand). Korea policy tailwind: the government "intends to expand manufacturing capacity before global supply constraints become binding," inside ~KRW1,500tn of cumulative domestic commitments from Samsung, SK Group/SK hynix and others, with **"Samsung and SK Hynix together account[ing] for the majority of global HBM supply."** See [hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md). | 07-28 | Morgan Stanley webcast "2Q26 memory, Intel implications and ARM preview" · Sean (recorded PRE-print) | guidance | **mixed** | 🔴 **The pre-print bogey, the explicit "watch the reaction, not the number" call, and the supply-side sting in the HBM4 delay.** ⏱ *Recorded before the print — "we do have earnings for Hynix tomorrow. And we have Samsung on Thursday" — so read every figure here as an expectation, not a result.* **The bogey: "for Hynix the numbers were anywhere between 60 to 65 trillion. Our number is currently at 65 trillion. We've seen some adjustment towards the 60 handle."** Against the actual **₩60.54tn OP**, the print landed **at the bottom of the buyside range and ~7% below MS's own ₩65tn** — the drift Sean flagged was directionally right, and the houses that had not cut were the ones marked. **His framing is the more valuable part: "the number itself will not be that meaningful. The reaction to any number would actually give us a better sense of whether this selloff is actually at the tail end. If tomorrow we do have numbers and stocks bouncing, I think that would be good news for the sector."** What the tape then delivered — SK Hynix **opening -6% and recovering to -2.3%** while [[MU]] fell ~8.9% intraday plus ~4% after hours — reads as an **ambiguous-to-mildly-constructive** result on Sean's own test for the name and a clearly negative one for the complex. **On the buyback: "For Hynix, my view is that it could happen, given the current volatility, but again, we have no visibility on that"** — against a stated buyside view that "valuations are clearly an attractive entry point for memory **with the potential buyback as near-term catalyst**." It did not come; that catalyst is now a miss, not a pending item. **The net-new analytical point is on supply: Sean names "Hynix probably HBM3E, where no HBM4 will actually reduce the amount of total HBM and more DRAM will be produced as a result" as one driver of TrendForce industry DRAM bit growth moving from "a 25% type of grow to now reaching 31%" this year and ~24% next** (alongside [[SAMSUNG]]'s P4 second-floor cleanroom) — i.e. the same HBM4 slip that caused the revenue miss is *adding commodity DRAM bits to the industry*. **On CXMT he puts the re-rating in transfer terms: the debut closed at a US$492bn market cap, "the 25th most valuable company in the world upon the IPO. You can probably say that that's quite proportionate to the amount of market cap that was taken away from stocks like Hynix"** — while bounding the fundamentals: "probably doesn't impact this cycle, maybe in the outer years," with CXMT "**currently at about 30,000 wafers capacity**" running DDR5 at "slightly lower on the clock speed versus mainstream DRAM, but nevertheless good enough for some of the mid to low end products in China," and the real concern being "**the expansion into 2030 could be more aggressive**." ⚠️ **Data-integrity flag: the "about 30,000 wafers capacity" figure is irreconcilable with the ~290-350k wspm CXMT capacity this page and [[MU]] carry from Wells Fargo (07-14) and BofA (07-12) — treat it as a transcription or sub-segment artifact and use the WF/BofA numbers.** Sector frame: his three-prong debate (AI spend robust; LTA re-rating still an *upside* story; the cycle) resolves by elimination onto the cycle — **y/y DRAM pricing peaks in 3Q, 3Q hikes ~15-20%, and 4Q would need +60-70% sequential to sustain the second derivative, "a bit tough to achieve."** Also: TrendForce inventory up since 2Q, **first time since 4Q last year, and at module makers not producers**, concentrated in non-AI NAND. (Morgan Stanley webcast "2Q26 memory, Intel implications and ARM preview" · Sean, 2026-07-28) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_28_2q26_ms_memory_intel_implications_and_arm_preview.html) |

What earns the upgrade in weight is that this wiki's standing rule is to look for the DECOMPOSITION before rejecting an implausible number — and the decomposition (30% x 70%, 3,000 TSVs, named customers, named failure modes) is internally consistent and independently reported.** ➤ **FOR THIS PAGE THE READ IS DIRECTLY BULLISH AND IT IS THE STRONGEST COMPETITIVE DATAPOINT OF THE WINDOW: the >90% versus ~25% comparison is SK hynix's own yield being used as the industry benchmark, and the 3,000-TSV bandwidth deficit means even a CXMT yield recovery does not produce a competitive part. | **09-15** | 🔭 **DAMNANG (Substack)** — *"Is the 2028 Memory Downcycle Real?"* ⚠️ **INDEPENDENT SUBSTACK AUTHOR, NOT A BROKER — no rating, no PT, no model** | **ciclo / supply** | **bull** | 🔭🔴🔴🔴 **THE 2028 DOWNCYCLE, DECOMPOSED — AND THREE OF THE FOUR BRANCHES ARE FLAT-TO-POSITIVE.** Combined 2028 profit for the three: **+7%** (8-Hi dominant) · **+4%** (4-Hi + sufficient accelerator expansion) · **0%** (4-Hi + insufficient) · **−12%** only if **LTA renegotiation** lands on top. *"Fewer layers alone do not bring a downcycle in 2028, but fewer layers, multiple semiconductor manufacturing bottlenecks, and LTA renegotiation together can."* **THE PIVOT: fewer layers give 1.5x stacks at 8-Hi / 3x at 4-Hi, enabling ~26% / ~60% more accelerators vs a CoWoS-substrate-power ceiling he assumes at 20-40% (uses 35%) — so at 8-Hi HBM IS STILL THE BOTTLENECK and no wafers spill into commodity DRAM.** SK-hynix-specific: the **Jun-2026 delay of HBM3E→HBM4 conversion to run DDR5** is the revenue-per-wafer rule in action (**HBM = ~3-4x the wafers of commodity DRAM per GB**, per MU's HBM3E-vs-DDR5 disclosure); **a 5-year commodity-DRAM contract being pursued with [[GOOG]]**; **Yongin Y1 first cleanroom Feb-2027**; contract prices expected to **peak ~3Q-2027** when buyers see equipment arriving. ⚠️ **Industry-conversation sourcing, sensitivities not forecasts, DRAM-only scope; the 35% ceiling is an assumption, not a measurement.** [Source](../Damnang/2026-09-15%20-%20Is%20the%202028%20Memory%20Downcycle%20Real-.md) |

**Síntese do quarter:** the first headline miss of the cycle — **OP ₩60.54tn vs ₩64.2tn, revenue ₩79.3tn vs ₩83.9tn** — and the most important qualifier is that it is a miss against a **stale screen consensus**: local brokers were already at ~₩60tn and Jefferies' analyst at ₩62tn. Margins did not break (**GM 83%, OM 76%, EBITDA 81%**) and the sequential level still rose off Q1. **The genuine negative is mechanical and specific: the revenue shortfall is attributed to the HBM4 ramp being late**, even though HBM4 shipments did begin in Q2 and 1cnm HBM4E samples are already with a major customer. Two things make this print more consequential than its numbers. **(1) The moat is being re-read as a constraint.** Jefferies expects the miss to feed the view that **SK Hynix has been less aggressive than [[SAMSUNG]] on pricing precisely because of its HBM commitments to [[NVDA]]** — the anchor relationship that underwrites this page's supplier-differentiation thesis is now the reason it may be leaving price on the table. **(2) An ~80% capex increase to the high-₩40tn range, with no buyback despite large net cash**, lands in the same week Jefferies research calls **memory prices to peak ~1H27** with hikes already tracking **15-20% vs 25-30% expected** and **2027 consensus requiring ~US$2tn of US AI capex**. That is the bear case in one sentence: peak-margin cash being committed to a capacity step-up into a price peak. Set against it, management's demand framing got *broader*, not narrower — **agentic AI expanding demand across HBM/server DRAM/eSSD**, PC/mobile softness explicitly a **volume-allocation** problem rather than end-demand — and DRAM ASP was still **+30% q/q**. The unresolved question for the next two quarters is whether **HBM4 catches up before pricing rolls**, with **CXMT's +466% IPO debut** now supplying a permanent sentiment overhang on the China supply question. **Two additions from MS's pre-print webcast (Sean, 07-28) that change how the print should be scored.** First, the **bogey**: MS was at **₩65tn** with the buyside range **₩60-65tn** and "adjustment towards the 60 handle" already underway — so the **₩60.54tn** print landed at the *bottom* of the range and ~7% below MS, which makes the "stale screen consensus" defence above accurate but narrower than it reads: the sell-side had cut, MS had not. Sean's own test was explicit and better than the number — **"the number itself will not be that meaningful. The reaction to any number would actually give us a better sense of whether this selloff is actually at the tail end"** — and the reaction (**Hynix opening -6% and recovering to -2.3%**, while [[MU]] fell ~8.9% intraday plus ~4% after hours) scores as **ambiguous-to-mildly-constructive for the name and negative for the complex**, not the clean bounce he was looking for. Second, and analytically the more useful: **the HBM4 delay is not only a revenue event, it is a supply event.** Sean names **"Hynix probably HBM3E, where no HBM4 will actually reduce the amount of total HBM and more DRAM will be produced as a result"** as a driver of TrendForce industry **DRAM bit growth moving 25% → ~31% this year (~24% next)** — so the freed wafer lands in commodity DRAM, where returns are currently competitive with HBM per this page's own HBM-vs-EPS paradox. Hold all three legs: **share/mix negative on the [[NVDA]] franchise, near-term bit/EPS cushion, and incremental industry supply feeding exactly the cycle-durability bear the Jefferies note is pressing.** Finally, the **buyback that did not come was an expected catalyst, not an absent question** — Sean, pre-print: "it could happen, given the current volatility, but again, we have no visibility," against a buyside "of the view that valuations are clearly an attractive entry point for memory with the potential buyback as near-term catalyst." Contrast [[SAMSUNG]], where the same webcast argues the buyback window is structurally *year-end* rather than simply absent. See [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md), [themes/china-export](themes/china-export.md). **Update 2026-08-03 (Fubon · Sherman Shang / Arthur, hosted by Jefferies — the primary supply-chain call): the first leg of the unresolved question now has an answer, and it is the favourable one. The question this Síntese posed was whether "HBM4 catches up before pricing rolls." Fubon dates the catch-up: "Rubin with Hynix HBM4 will start to ramp up at LATE AUGUST OR SEPTEMBER WITH A BIGGER VOLUME, which is why in Q4 we will see a higher volume of Rubin" — with only "a small volume of Micron and Samsung's HBM" in the chain so far. Rubin production of 200-300K units in Q3 stepping to 700-800K in Q4 is therefore PACED BY SK HYNIX, which reframes the "moat as a constraint" reading: the NVDA anchor relationship is what the platform's volume curve is built on, and the Q2 miss reads as timing inside a socket the company still owns rather than share loss to the peers. Against it, three offsets to keep live: (i) the HBM4 $/GB step-up to $30-32/GB from $17-18/GB HBM3-class is being captured across all three suppliers, so it is a cycle fact rather than a Hynix edge (⚠ the transcript dates it "in 2026" in one place and "in 2027" in another; ambiguity carried); (ii) the surviving Rubin Ultra SKU is LIGHTER on HBM — the four-compute-die ">1,024 GB/chip" part is cancelled and the two-die part is "around 384 GB" (B200 ~192GB / B300 ~288GB) — so content per package is a genuine headwind even though ODMs reject any 2027 memory de-spec "including the Google, AWS" and are raising purchasing capex against a $7-8m Rubin rack; (iii) "the HBM base die issue" is one of the two named causes of the ~two-month Rubin push-out, so the volume vendor also carries the execution risk. Net: the ramp-timing leg of the bear case weakens materially from late August; the pricing-peak and content-per-package legs are untouched.** **Update 2026-08-17 (/run-inbox) — the pricing-peak leg now has its most explicit dated bear, and it comes from the same house this page already quotes on the other side.** TrendForce, presented at FMS and relayed by FUNDA (2026-08-11): **the DRAM supply/demand gap bottoms and begins to NARROW in 2027, with the sufficiency ratio turning POSITIVE at +4.0% in 2028; NAND turns positive at +0.4% in 2027 and reaches 2% oversupply in 2028.** ⚠️ **Log the source's own inconsistency rather than adopting either version: TrendForce on 07-30 (already on [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md)) had the DRAM sufficiency gap WIDENING in 2027. Twelve days later, at FMS, it has the gap narrowing from 2027 and positive by 2028. Same house, opposite direction on the 2027 DRAM inflection — and this is a relay, not the TrendForce publication.** Against it, FUNDA explicitly disagrees: the ASP-growth flattening is *"a process of structural normalization,"* not a signal of 2027-28 oversupply, and it expects **memory prices to hold at elevated levels through 2027** on a rising LTA share and a mix shifting further to data centers, *"rather than peaking and collapsing."* ➜ **For this page the relevant asymmetry is that a 2028 DRAM oversupply call lands squarely on the ~80% capex step-up into the high-₩40tn range that this Síntese already flags as the bear case in one sentence: peak-margin cash committed to capacity arriving into a possible sufficiency flip. Unresolved and deliberately not netted.** Separately, the company's own FMS slide (**3D stacked DRAM, HBF, CXL Pooled Memory as three new tiers**) broadens the product answer to exactly that risk — but only HBF has a date, and [[NVDA]] was absent from every HBF panel. **Update 2026-08-20 (/run-inbox — three houses on the W40trn event, plus two company primaries): the "no buyback despite large net cash" clause in the bear sentence above is now DEAD, and it died above the bar rather than at it.** The buyback landed on 08-19 at **W40trn with full cancellation**, and the only house that published its own bar shows how far above expectations that is: **UBS "had modelled Won12tn" and put buy-side expectations at "c.Won20-50tn"** (UBS, 2026-08-19) — against JPM's **W29.3tn of 2026E buyback** already on this page (08-09), so the print **cleared every estimate the desk had**. The policy ceiling moved with it, from **"within the range of 50%"** to **">50%"/"50% or higher"** of cumulative 2025-27 FCF (Barclays 2026-08-19; JPM 2026-08-20), which UBS models as taking **2026E FCF returned to shareholders from 15% to 30%** off this one disclosure. **Three houses, one event, tightly clustered upside: JPM +84% (W2.75m vs W1.491m), Barclays +92.8% (US$300 vs US$155.62), UBS +100% (W3.0m vs W1.5m)** — ⚠️ **and note the trap: Barclays quotes the name in USD on the GDR line from its European Technology Hardware desk, so its US$300 is NOT ladderable against the KRW marks; only the upside is.** ➜ **BUT THE BEAR SENTENCE ONLY LOSES HALF ITS SUBJECT, AND THE SURVIVING HALF IS THE ONE THAT MATTERS. "Peak-margin cash committed to a capacity step-up into a price peak" was never really about the absent buyback — it was about the ~80% capex step-up landing into the possible 2028 sufficiency flip. A >50%-of-FCF payout commitment ON TOP of that capex path does not relieve the tension, it TIGHTENS it: both claims on the same cash are now contractual-ish, and they are only jointly affordable if the FCF base is as large as the bulls model (Barclays c.51% of 25-27 FCF returned by YE27 ≈ c.15% of market cap; JPM a 16.7% 2027E cash yield). ⚠️ The clearest tell is that Barclays — bullish enough to write that it sees "tightness holding through 2027" — STILL prefers buybacks to dividends "given industry cyclicality," i.e. it wants the reversible instrument. And the two houses disagree wildly on the run-rate: Barclays models c.KRW200trn of 2027 buyback against JPM's W73.3tn, a ~2.7x spread on the same policy. Neither is a forecast to trade; the disclosed facts stop at "additional returns within the same program period, detailed at 3Q26."** **On the demand side, two company primaries added this run and both cut the same way — broader, not cheaper.** Solidigm's co-CEO gives the agentic claim its mechanism and its buyer-side evidence: agentic workflows need **"active storage"** for multi-agent hand-offs on a common dataset, and **NVIDIA's own "STX" server exists because "memory alone is not enough to store all the context and key-values (KV cache)"**, with Solidigm supplying into that stack via the **BlueField DPU** (Bloomberg Intelligence "Tech Disruptors" · Xin Guo, 2026-07-27) — ⚠️ **"STX" is NVIDIA's product name here, not [[STX]]'s ticker; the router mis-routed the file on that collision.** That reconciles a loose end this Síntese left open: SK hynix's **HBF anchor is the [[GOOG]]/ASIC camp with [[NVDA]] absent from every panel, but its eSSD franchise sits INSIDE NVIDIA's preferred DPU-mediated storage path** — the group is on both branches of the storage-tier debate via different products. Alongside it, **SK Telecom + NVIDIA plan up to 2GW of AI DC capacity on Vera Rubin systems AND SK hynix HBM via a new entity "SK Hyper"** (MS "AI Sovereignty", 2026-08-19) — intra-group demand that need not be won against [[SAMSUNG]] or [[MU]], though ⚠️ **with no timeline and only 100MW dated (AWS, late 2027) against a 1GW site cap, treat 2GW as ambition** — and the company published a **co-packaged-optics roadmap in *Nature Electronics*** under the framing that **"AI competition is shifting from chips to systems"** (SK hynix Newsroom, 2026-08-20), ⚠️ **a peer-reviewed roadmap with no date, partner or spec — a fourth system-level vector alongside the FMS three tiers, not a product.** ➜ **Net for the quarter: the capital-return leg of the bear case is closed and closed favourably; the demand-breadth leg keeps strengthening from primary sources; and the two legs that actually decide the name — the price the breadth clears at, and whether an ~80%-higher capex path plus a >50% payout both survive a 2028 sufficiency flip — are exactly where they were. Nothing netted.** 🆕 **UPDATE 2026-08-21 (/run-inbox) — one addition and one deliberate NON-addition, and the non-addition is the more important discipline.** **The addition:** an independent read-through (DAMNANG (Substack), "HBM Density Peak: Why AI Value Is Moving From Memory to Connectivity", 2026-08-20 — ⚠️ **not a broker**) supplies the *mechanism* under the CPO primary already logged on this page — **compute throughput 3x per ~2 years vs interconnect bandwidth ~1.4x** — and names the one *shipping* commercial artefact of the memory→connectivity shift: **CMM-Ax with [[MRVL]] (Structera A + SK hynix DRAM and software stack, disclosed Aug-2026)**. Read together with the FMS three-tier slide (08-11) and the *Nature Electronics* roadmap (08-20), the pattern is that the company is **monetising the ELECTRICAL disaggregation tier now while the OPTICAL tier stays a research direction** — which is a scope-widening story (die → controller, base die, packaging, memory placement), not a near-term revenue line. **The non-addition:** the same piece claims Rubin Ultra is being de-spec'd to **HBM4 8-Hi**, and the author himself calls it **UNCONFIRMED** — so it is logged in the HBM4 Sinal cell and **NOT scored**. If it were ever confirmed it would be the first genuine **mix-DOWN in HBM content per GPU** this page has had to price, against a franchise whose moat is high-stack qualification. That is exactly why it is held at arm's length rather than folded into the thesis — and why the CXMT work archived from 07-27 matters more for the near term: the China price-war leg of the bear case weakens on inspection (see Risks), while the leg that actually moves this stock is unchanged — **"what moves SK hynix shares is [the capex plans of a handful of AI-accelerator customers], not CXMT."**

**🆕 08-09/10 — the bear-headline week, and what actually changed:** three of the four drags (HBM discount, Solidigm IPO, Chongqing stake) are **headline risk that management has now put on a ≤1-month disclosure clock**, not confirmed facts; the fourth (W54tn capex) is confirmed but back-end loaded to 2031. The genuinely new bear datapoint is competitive rather than corporate: **Samsung HBM4 yield "nears 80%"** (DIGITIMES, 2026-08-10) — i.e. the second source is qualifying, into a 2027 HBM price negotiation SKH is already being reported as discounting. Against that, **JPM prefers SEC > SKH in the near term** inside an OW-on-both memory call (JPM / Kwon, 2026-08-09), and **SIG prefers Micron specifically on "HBM share gains against SK Hynix"** (SIG / Hosseini, 2026-08-10). Two of the three houses that touched Korean memory this week are routing incremental money *away* from SKH within a bullish memory view — that is the read, not the tape. `demanda/valuation · mixed`
**🆕 Síntese — addendum 2026-08-25 (the UBS Korea Summit note, the competitor's own IR call, and three supply-side models — eight sources, and the quarter's question has changed shape).** When this window opened the debate was **“is the HBM4 ramp late, and does the capex step-up land into a price peak.”** It is now **“how many HBM layers ship, who pays for share, and does the 2027 bit-supply number break the tightness story.”** Four things moved. ① **THE CAPITAL-RETURN STORY IS BETTER SPECIFIED AND SLIGHTLY WORSE: the Won40tn buyback is INSIDE the >50%-of-FCF policy rather than additive, but 50% is now an explicit FLOOR for 2025-2027 and the company has effectively acknowledged DISCRETIONARY PRICE-SUPPORT BUYING — “SK Hynix may be supporting the stock price with additional buybacks, within this policy, when needed”** (UBS, 2026-08-25). With the Jefferies desk sizing daily buyback at ~15% of KRX-only volume the same day, **the tape and the policy are now the same object, and that is a reason to discount price action as evidence, not to celebrate it.** ② **THE LTA BOOK LOCKS VOLUME, NOT PRICE — AND TWO OF THE THREE MAJORS SAID SO IN THE SAME 48 HOURS.** UBS: >50% of bits LTA-covered by year-end, *“the main focus is on VOLUME VISIBILITY WITH NO PRICING FLOOR/CEILING.”* Samsung IR, relayed by BofA: ASP *“remains negotiable on a quarterly basis even under LTAs.”* ➤ **This is symmetric and the page should stop treating it as one-sided: it removes the downside floor the LTA bulls implied AND the upside cap the Jefferies bear implied.** ③ **THE DE-SPEC RUMOUR HAS BEEN UPGRADED TO A CONTESTED BROKER VIEW, NOT TO A FACT.** UBS now states as its own view that **NVIDIA moved to HBM4 8-Hi from HBM4E 12-Hi for VR/R300**, Mizuho/SemiAnalysis go further (**cap at 4-8Hi**; TPUv8 cut to 8-Hi), and Samsung's DS spokesperson confirms customers are evaluating lower stack heights **because of SUPPLY CONSTRAINT, not weak demand**. **Against all three, Morgan Stanley's 08-24 HBM demand model still carries Rubin R200 at HBM4 12-Hi.** ⚠️ **Left OPEN. The DAMNANG sensitivity already on this page — ~1.63x effective output ceiling, HBM revenue ~81.5-98% of plan — remains the right frame and remains a sensitivity, not a forecast.** ④ **THE GENUINELY NEW BEAR IS COMPETITIVE, AND IT IS THE COMPETITOR'S OWN WORDS: Samsung says it will hold mid-to-high-30s% HBM share in 2027 and will buy it with capacity “REGARDLESS OF THE PROFITABILITY.”** That is the one mechanism capable of breaking the pricing discipline underpinning the “memory is no longer cyclical” leg of this page — and it arrives the same day Jefferies' Sherman claims Hynix is **late at NVIDIA**. ⚠️ **An internal inconsistency inside Samsung's own call (mid-to-high-30s% for all-HBM vs “mid- to high-twenty percent” in the HBM4E answer) is logged, not reconciled.** **Net:** UBS's estimate gap to consensus widens every year (**+11.9% / +29.0% / +49.0% in 12/26E-28E EPS**) on **78-82% EBIT margins** and net cash building to **Won462tn by 2028E** — i.e. the bull case is now almost entirely a *durability* call, and every one of the four items above is a durability test with a date on it. **The nearest is UBS's own named catalyst: ~18 September 2026, pricing-negotiation finalisation plus a further shareholder-return update.**


## Management commentary — evolution (last 4 quarters)

| Theme | Q3 2025 (2025-10-29) | Q4 2025 / FY25 (2026-01-29) | Q1 2026 (2026-04-23) | **Q2 2026 (2026-07-28)** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Revenue / OP growth | Rev 24.4tn (+10% QoQ); OP 11.4tn, >10tn first time | Rev 32.8tn (+34% QoQ); FY25 OP 47.2tn (+101%) | Rev 52.6tn (+60% QoQ, +198% YoY); OP 37.6tn | **Rev ₩79.3tn vs ₩83.9tn exp; OP ₩60.54tn vs ₩64.2tn** — first headline miss, but in line with local brokers' cut to ~₩60tn |
| OP margin | 47% | 58% (+11pp QoQ); FY25 49% | 72% (+13pp QoQ) | **76%** (GM 83%, EBITDA margin 81%) |
| HBM cycle | HBM4 mass production in Sept; ships Q4; "sold out since 2023" | HBM rev >2x YoY; HBM3E + HBM4 supplied simultaneously | HBM4 ramp prep; demand "far exceeding supply capacity" | **HBM4 shipments BEGAN in Q2** at required speeds, ramping 2H; **1cnm HBM4E samples to a major customer in 1H**; HBM4 ramp delay cited as the revenue shortfall |
| DRAM | 128GB+ DDR5 shipments >2x QoQ; "sold out 2026" | 1cnm full mass production; 256GB DDR5 RDIMM | "Significant price increases"; customers prioritize volume | Q2 bits +HSD, **ASP +30% q/q**; 2026 bit growth **mid-20s%**; Q3 bits **+10%**, server-focused; SOCAMM2 fully commercialised on 1C |
| NAND / eSSD | AI server eSSD at price premiums; 321-layer ramp | 321-layer QLC done; highest annual NAND rev on record | Shipments ~-10% QoQ on high base; group eSSD ~$4.64bn | eSSD named part of the structural AI-server demand expansion (not separately quantified in the first-take flow) |
| Capex / footprint | 2026 investment to increase vs 2025 | Capex up "significantly YoY"; M15X, Yongin, Indiana | — | **FY capex up to +80% to the high-₩40tn range**, being pulled in; **no buyback despite large net cash** |
| Demand / supply balance | 2026 contracts finalized; tightness into 2027 | Could not fully meet HBM demand; 2026 locked | Tight supply; flags PC/mobile softening on high prices | Demand **broadening as AI turns agentic**; Big Tech expanding procurement on a **compute-capacity shortage**; **PC/mobile softness = difficulty securing volumes**, recovery as tightness eases |

_Source: SKHYNIX earnings calls (dates above); management commentary, paraphrased._

The DESK RELAY of it (Bernstein · Tyler Seidman) reached the page on 07-27 and is the row directly below the new one. The relay's content was verified line-by-line against the PDF and NOT re-added.** **WHAT THE RELAY LEFT OUT, all net-new: ① the rating — Outperform, PT KRW 3,300,000 (px KRW1,781,000, 24-Jul close), on 6.2x forward 5Q-8Q EPS of KRW536,158, with EPS KRW60,341A / 395,677E / 568,862E = 29.5x / 4.5x / 3.1x. Note the multiple is the SAME 6.2x Bernstein applies to [[SAMSUNG]], so the relative Korea call is entirely EPS. ② The deal detail behind "$500B+": the LOIs cover memory AND "a 2GW Vera Rubin DSX AI Factory planned to be online for SK TELECOM IN 2027" — a dated, sized, named-offtaker facility that was nowhere on this page, and which is simultaneously an [[NVDA]] revenue line and a captive HBM sink for the affiliate. The memory leg covers "long-term technical developments & stable supply of next-gen AI memory (HBM4 & later?)" (the question mark is Bernstein's).** 🔴 **③ ENTIRELY NEW TO THE WIKI: "SK Group reportedly will ADDITIONALLY collaborate with other global tech companies for memory supply worth US$250B over the next 5 years." Sourcing is "reportedly," on two press links, NOT disclosure — carried as a press report, not booked. If it stands, the group's contracted memory book is $500B+ (NVDA) plus $250B (unnamed), and the second leg has no identified counterparty.** **④ The cross-complex rating logic, which sharpens the CXMT debate on this page: Outperform on Samsung, SK hynix and [[MU]], Underperform on [[KIOXIA]], discriminated explicitly by product — "we worry about the long-term threat from China in NAND… The threat is much lower in DRAM as China eventually will find it difficult to compete in DRAM market WITHOUT EUV." That is a litho-access ceiling argument, not a capacity argument, and it cuts against measuring CXMT by wpm at all (the UBS 240K→466K and JPM 16%/11%-by-28E marks on this page measure the input Bernstein says does not decide it). Logged as a competing frame, not a resolution.** **⑤ Industry frame extended: consensus memory revenue ~US$0.9T (CY26) → US$1.3T (CY27) → US$1.3T (CY28) — the relay had the $1.3T; the CY26 base and the FLATNESS between '27 and '28 are new, and the flatness is the part worth attacking.** Cross-filed [[SAMSUNG]], [[MU]], [[KIOXIA]], [[NVDA]], [[AVGO]], [[TSM]], [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md).

No rating, PT or estimate superseded.** **Three things this adds to the Q2 print already on the page.** **(1) The bogey, which sharpens the "miss against a stale consensus" defence into something more precise: MS's Sean had the buyside range at ₩60-65tn with "our number… currently at 65 trillion" and "some adjustment towards the 60 handle."** So the **₩60.54tn** actual landed at the *bottom* of the range and **~7% below MS's own number** — the sell-side had cut, MS had not, and the screen consensus of ₩64.2tn was the lagging figure. **(2) His stated test, which is the better scoring rule and worth keeping as a template: "the number itself will not be that meaningful. The reaction to any number would actually give us a better sense of whether this selloff is actually at the tail end. If tomorrow we do have numbers and stocks bouncing, I think that would be good news for the sector."** Scored against the tape — **Hynix opened -6% and recovered to -2.3% while [[MU]] fell ~8.9% intraday plus ~4% after hours** — the result is **ambiguous-to-mildly-constructive for the name, negative for the complex**; not the clean bounce, not the rout. **(3) The genuinely net-new analytical point, and it inverts the sign on the page's central negative: Sean names "Hynix probably HBM3E, where no HBM4 will actually reduce the amount of total HBM and more DRAM will be produced as a result" as one driver of TrendForce industry DRAM bit growth moving from "a 25% type of grow to now reaching 31%" this year (~24% next).** The same HBM4 slip that caused the revenue miss is **adding commodity DRAM bits to the industry** at a moment when commodity-DRAM returns are competitive with HBM (this page's own HBM-vs-EPS paradox, MS/Sean Kim 04-14). Folded into the HBM4 Sinal row as a three-legged read — share/mix negative on the [[NVDA]] franchise, near-term bit/EPS cushion, and incremental supply feeding the cycle-durability bear — rather than logged as a separate positive. Also added: **the buyback was an expected catalyst that did not arrive** ("it could happen, given the current volatility, but again, we have no visibility on that," against a buyside pricing "the potential buyback as near-term catalyst"), now marked as a resolved miss in the Capex/capital-return Sinal row; and Sean's **CXMT-as-transfer framing** — a **US$492bn** debut close, 25th most valuable company globally, "quite proportionate to the amount of market cap that was taken away from stocks like Hynix," with fundamental impact bounded to the outer years and **the 2030 expansion pace as the real question**. ⚠️ **Data-integrity flag raised, not adopted: the webcast's "CXMT currently at about 30,000 wafers capacity" cannot be reconciled with the ~290-350k wspm figures this page and [[MU]] carry from Wells Fargo (07-14) and BofA (07-12); treated as a transcription/sub-segment artifact.** ⚠️ **Date tension noted for the record: the webcast is filed 2026-07-28 but is internally pre-print ("earnings for Hynix tomorrow… Samsung on Thursday" = 07-30), while this page dates the Q2 print 2026-07-28.** The commentary is unambiguously pre-print and has been labelled as such throughout; the one-day filing discrepancy does not affect any figure. Cross-filed [[MU]], [[SAMSUNG]], [[SNDK]].
- **2026-07-28** — **/wiki-ingest · Q2 2026 PRINT (2026-07-28) — new-print reset.** New `## Current state` for the June quarter (the Q1 2026 block plus all the standing capacity/HBM4/MR-MUF/packaging deep-dive material is retained inline below it); archived the Q1 2026 intra-quarter window (Apr 23 → Jul 28, **130 lines** — the largest window on the wiki) into this Changelog; opened a fresh Q2 2026 window; rolled the management-evolution table forward (Q2 2025 drops off, **Q2 2026 added**). **Superseded per the thesis-drift rule** — prior values preserved: *Current state* previously led with **Q1 2026 revenue ₩52.6tn (+60% q/q, +198% y/y), operating profit ₩37.6tn, OP margin 72%, net profit ₩40.3tn, EBITDA ₩41.3tn, net cash ₩35tn.** **What printed: revenue ₩79.3tn vs ~₩83.9tn expected and operating profit ₩60.54tn vs ₩64.2tn consensus — the first headline miss of the cycle.** ⚠️ **The qualifier matters as much as the miss: local Korean brokers had already cut OP to ~₩60tn and Jefferies' Jeff Kim was at ₩62tn**, so the print is in line with the most recent numbers and only misses the stale screen consensus. Margin structure intact — **GM 83%, OM 76%, EBITDA margin 81%** — and the *level* still rose sequentially off Q1. **The margin trajectory this page has tracked (41% → 47% → 58% → 72%) therefore breaks its ascent at 76%.** **Cause of the shortfall, per Jefferies: the HBM4 ramp is late** — HBM4 shipments did begin in Q2 at customer-required speeds and **1cnm HBM4E samples went to a major customer in 1H**, but the ramp slipped relative to plan. Other management detail logged: **DRAM Q2 bits +HSD with ASP +30% q/q, 2026 bit growth guided mid-20s%, Q3 bits +10% server-focused**; **SOCAMM2 fully commercialised on 1C**; demand **broadening as AI evolves into agentic form** across HBM/server DRAM/eSSD; **PC and smartphone softness explicitly attributed to difficulty securing memory volumes, not weak end-demand.** **Two developments change the shape of the debate rather than the numbers.** **(1) The [[NVDA]] anchor is being re-read as a pricing constraint.** Jefferies expects the miss to fuel the view that **"Hynix has been less aggressive than [[SAMSUNG]] on pricing given its commitments to NVDA on HBM"** — i.e. the supplier-differentiation moat this page underwrites may also be a price ceiling. **(2) Capex is stepping up ~80% to the high-₩40tn range and is being pulled in, with no buyback despite large net cash** (Culpium; Jefferies) — landing in the same week Jefferies research published **"HAS MEMORY PEAKED?": price hikes tracking 15-20% vs 25-30% expected, memory prices peaking ~1H27, and 2027 consensus requiring ~US$2tn of US AI capex to be achievable**, with **HBM4 costs jumping to US$31-32/GB**. That is the bear case in one line — peak-margin cash committed to capacity into a forecast price peak. It is also consistent with management's own long-standing argument that ~70% OP margins are "not high enough" to fund the greenfield/hybrid-bonding step-up. **Tape context (not a company event, but it dominated the reaction):** on 2026-07-27 the **KOSPI fell 10.7% with a sell sidecar and the year's eighth circuit breaker, Samsung -12%, SK Hynix -13%**, driven by **CXMT's IPO debut at +466% — China's second-largest IPO ever — "making the memory threat feel suddenly very real"** plus a live-Fed-hike scare (~40% odds priced). On the print the stock opened **-6% pre-market and recovered to -2.3%**, while **[[MU]] fell a further ~4% after hours on top of -8.9% intraday**. Also logged: an unverified @zerohedge claim that SK Hynix slumped below a US IPO price — **flagged, not adopted**, as it is uncorroborated elsewhere in this ingest; and a Korean-press exclusive (@jukan05) that **LPDDR6 mass production starts in 2H with Xiaomi as first customer**, which sits in tension with the same-day comment that PC/mobile volumes are being rationed. Cross-filed [[MU]], [[SAMSUNG]], [[NVDA]], [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md), [themes/china-export](themes/china-export.md).

| 07-27 | 🆕 **DAMNANG (Substack) — "After CXMT's Listing, Do the Memory Three Have Further to Fall?"** · ⚠️ **INDEPENDENT AUTHOR, NOT A BROKER; ingested 2026-08-21, ~25 days late** | competicao | **bull** | **THE MOST SYSTEMATIC TEST OF THE CXMT BEAR CASE THIS PAGE CARRIES — it takes each circulating claim and checks it against the disclosed data rather than asserting a view. ⚠️ The price marks are the 07-27 listing day and are HISTORICAL; the value is the framework.** ① **THE "CXMT SELLS 30% BELOW" CLAIM IS AN APPLES-TO-ORANGES ARTEFACT:** it compares company-wide average ASPs, and roughly **two thirds of CXMT's revenue is low-priced MOBILE memory**, which drags the company average "for reasons that have nothing to do with competitiveness." Like-for-like the gap is **5-10% per CXMT's own IPO prospectus — and in SERVER products the SIGN FLIPS**: in July 2026 a **64GB DDR5 server module built on CXMT chips sold for RMB18,999 in Chinese online retail vs RMB18,595 for the same specification on Samsung/SK hynix chips — CXMT 2.2% HIGHER** — and in the contract market CXMT was reported to have **quoted above Samsung's supply price of US$1,240 per module**. ② **IT HAS NO ROOM TO UNDERCUT:** at its current node CXMT spends **>30% more to produce the same amount of memory**, its **chips are ~40% larger in area**, and its yield falls **short of the 85-90% the industry treats as standard**. ③ **THE "8% SHARE" IS A REVENUE NUMBER, AND REVENUE RISES WITH PRICE:** CXMT booked **US$7.31bn of DRAM revenue in 1Q26 = 7.7% of the world market**, but it puts **13% of global DRAM WAFER STARTS into production** and converts them into only **6% of memory CAPACITY** — "half of what goes in disappears on the way from wafers to capacity." ④ **ITS GROWTH SO FAR IS PRICE, NOT VOLUME:** 1Q26 revenue **+71% y/y**, but measured q/q **memory capacity shipped grew only +11% while ASP rose +57%**. ⑤ **THE NODE GAP HAS STOPPED CLOSING:** CXMT took **18-24 months** per generation from its third to its fourth, **its fifth has already run past 24 months** and slipped toward 2H26, while the incumbents also turn a generation in ~24 months and are ramping 1c and moving to HBM4 → **the ~three-generation gap holds**. It did bring **16nm-class DDR5 to volume on DUV multi-patterning with no EUV option**, so the constraint is development PACE, not capability. ⑥ **HBM IS A PRICE-POSITIVE FOR THE INCUMBENTS, NOT A PRICE-NEGATIVE:** CXMT allocates **<2% of wafer starts to HBM**, its HBM3 schedule slipped on thermal problems and **8-high combined yield is estimated ~25% vs 70-90% at the incumbents**; because stacking consumes **~3x the wafer area per GB of ordinary DDR5**, if CXMT converts 10% of its DRAM lines to HBM the ordinary DRAM capacity off those lines **falls to one third**. The same mechanic on the incumbents' side is this page's own tightness explanation, now quantified: **in 2026 HBM consumes 22% of global DRAM wafer capacity while producing only 9% of the memory capacity — "that gap is the underlying reason ordinary DRAM is short today."** ⑦ **IPO-FUNDED SUPPLY IS A 2028 EVENT AT THE EARLIEST** (proceeds → equipment orders → production = 18-24 months). On the circulating "CXMT doubles DRAM capacity by 2030" story (said to have come out of a pre-listing broker conference call — the author states he **cannot verify it**, and warns that pieces "written by press outlets and institutions without the technical background" are circulating as established fact and reaching share prices): **what doubles is WAFERS, not capacity — 13% of wafer starts / 6% of capacity doubled still lands near 12% on a capacity basis, while DRAM demand over those four years grows far faster** → he reads the story as **good news for Chinese equipment makers (Naura, AMEC) rather than bad news for the memory three.** ⑧ 🔴 **THE SK HYNIX-SPECIFIC CONCLUSION, WHICH IS THE ITEM FOR THIS PAGE:** *"SK hynix has its earnings concentrated in HBM. CXMT cannot enter that segment, so of the three SK hynix is the most insulated from it."* The offsetting half is the exposure this page already owns: *"those earnings ride the capital spending plans of the handful of customers buying AI accelerators. **What moves SK hynix shares is that, not CXMT.**"* (For contrast, in the same piece [[SAMSUNG]] "is hit first and hardest" because it sells the largest share of its output to Chinese smartphone and PC makers and meets CXMT's commodity mobile/PC DRAM competition first, with a 4.32x forward multiple already discounting it; [[MU]] "has the least left to lose in China" — mainland revenue fell from ~11% of total to the 7% range after the 2023 purchase ban and it exited the Chinese server-memory business in Oct-2025.) ⑨ **The listing-day marks, HISTORICAL:** CXMT opened **RMB49.50 against an IPO price of RMB8.66 (+472%)**, a market cap of **RMB3.31tn (~US$489bn) — the most valuable company listed on the A-share market** — reaching **RMB54.65 by midday (+531%, ~RMB3.7tn)**; **Samsung Electronics and SK hynix were both weak the same day, with foreign investors net sellers**. Forward multiples that day: **CXMT ~30x this year's expected earnings, Samsung 4.32x, SK hynix 4.69x, Micron 6.42x.** ⚠️ **Nomura's CNY116 initiation is already logged on the 07-26 row above — the net-new here is the reverse-engineering of it: RMB5.80 of 2028 EPS at 20x → RMB7.76tn market cap, which backs out 2028 net profit of RMB388bn, "3.9 times what the market expects CXMT to earn this year, and more than Micron earns at the peak of a cycle."** **His path call, for the record:** the SMIC precedent (opened RMB95 in Jul-2020 +246%, never traded above that price again, −48% ten weeks later and −32% a year later, but never broke its IPO price and was +136% above it a year on); he expects **day one to mark the high**, a possible correction around the **first lockup release in January 2027**, and no break of the IPO price. **On the complex: "the recent memory correction is MORE LIKELY TO END after the CXMT listing than to continue"** — further downside needs memory earnings to turn down or AI demand to reverse outright, and neither price nor volume shows it. [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/13_After_CXMTs_Listing_Do_the_Memory_Three_Have_Further_to_Fall_.html) |

| 07-24 | SemiAnalysis "Can AMD break the CUDA Moat? AMD Advancing AI 2026" | produto | **mixed** | **The HBM4 pin-speed bake-off, the 2027 HBM-price call, and the de-spec that offsets it — all read off the buyer's BOM.** **(1) Pin speed / binning:** AMD's MI455X carries **12 HBM4 stacks / 432GB / 23.3 TB/s** vs Nvidia Rubin and Google at **8 stacks / 288GB**, Rubin **22 TB/s** — a near-tie despite AMD's **50% wider bus**, because AMD runs **7.6 Gbps** pin speed vs Nvidia's **10.7 Gbps (40% faster)**. "Nvidia aggressively raised its targeted HBM4 pin speeds last year, well above the original JEDEC HBM4 specification… precisely driven by the need to make up for the memory bandwidth deficit to AMD MI455X." That "was challenging for memory suppliers to keep up with. Memory suppliers had to rework their HBM4 to deliver the spec Nvidia was targeting. This also pushed out upstream output for Rubin **but as of today these issues have been finally resolved**" — and **Nvidia must use a much higher-quality bin than AMD**. AMD + Nvidia are the only two shipping HBM4 this year. Net for the #1 HBM supplier: HBM4 is **two markets** (easy 7.6 Gbps AMD bin vs premium 10.7 Gbps Nvidia bin), so the differentiator shifts toward **yield on the top bin** and the quality/price spread between suppliers — a share/mix question, not a volume one. Cross-check, held carefully: this page's own SemiAnalysis datapoint has SK hynix's **HBM4 engineering samples at ~10 Gbps with lower jitter / stronger package execution than Samsung's** ("Memory Mania," 02-06), just under the 10.7 Gbps Rubin target — **SemiAnalysis names no bin winner and neither do we.** Also the first explicit statement on the page that the Rubin HBM4 upstream-output delay is *behind* the chain. **(2) Bull — 2027 pricing:** expects "a significant increase in HBM pricing in 2027 as memory suppliers move to match the margins they now earn on commodity DRAM," strong enough that AMD "can't pass the full memory increase on to its customers" → **gross-margin drag on the MI455X package**; "HBM, DRAM, and NAND prices alike have increased dramatically since our baseline in mid-2025." Direct external support for management's refusal to commit to HBM-DDR margin parity (roadshow 07-06). **(3) Bear — de-spec:** original 2025 MI455X BOM carried **138TB of DRAM (LPDDR5X + DDR5 MRDIMM)**; current estimate has **LPDDR5X removed entirely** (the up-to-**1TB** per-accelerator direct-attached LPDDR tier is gone on tight supply) plus a significant DDR5 RDIMM de-spec — "**much of the DRAM price increases are offset by aggressive de-specs.**" [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_24_ENC_Can_AMD_break_the_CUDA_Moat_AMD_Advancing_AI_2026_email.html) |

| 07-25 | Susquehanna · Mehdi Hosseini (Technology Hardware weekly, Jul 19-25) | supply | mixed | **"US Negotiating Leverage Bears Fruit" — the sharpest articulation yet of the political mechanism behind Korean memory supply, and it has a bearish tail.** SIG (Neutral, ₩1,781,000) argues the US administration's negotiating strategy is working: **Korean memory suppliers are now expected to unveil major US memory-fab investment plans** (Korean president in Silicon Valley for meetings and an AI summit; Tom's Hardware reporting Samsung and SK Hynix set to announce large deals with leading US tech firms). Hosseini says the leverage rests on two points: **(1) the majority of Korean suppliers' memory demand comes from US-based customers, >50% of revenue mix**; and **(2) the rise of CXMT and YMTC as a viable supply alternative**. Both Chinese makers are **currently barred from supplying US-based OEMs** and both sit on the **Pentagon's Section 1260H list**, with **YMTC also on the Commerce Entity List** — so the implicit threat is that Washington can "unlock" that supply. **SIG's sizing, net-new: CXMT and YMTC reach 17% and 25% of DRAM and NAND supply respectively by YE28.** The consequence he draws is the part that matters for the cycle debate on this page: **new US greenfield memory fabs cannot come online before 2029** given construction and tooling lead times, so **in the interim CXMT/YMTC "could still offer a valuable short-term remedy to a tight memory supply-demand balance"** — the US "essentially killing two birds with one stone" by easing the imbalance and reducing memory-price inflation. Read as a **policy-driven downside scenario to the pricing curve** that is independent of demand: the tightness that underwrites current DRAM/NAND ASPs is partly a function of an export-control regime that the same administration has an incentive to relax once the fab commitments are banked.
