**Quarter synthesis:** with HBM demand and ASP already confirmed by the flow, the debate has shifted entirely to the multiple — cycle durability (GS bull, Jefferies "out-earn the hyperscalers") vs HBM commoditization once 2-3 suppliers qualify (ex-Micron VP). New from 06-27: (1) **Samsung/SK Hynix "huge AI spending push"** (JPM) — the upcoming CAPEX disclosures are framed as the near-term catalyst for the sector, following MU's template of $100B LTA RPO + 16 SCAs; (2) **BofA ASML Flow & Tell** confirms ASML as the DRAM-exposure proxy, with US LO inflows already building ahead of the Korean memory capex announcements. **06-29: Korea $880B AI mega-plan confirmed** (Bloomberg/JPM) — government-mandated Yongyin pull-forward (7–12 yrs) is the most explicit government backstop on memory capacity in the cycle; Samsung + SK Hynix combined $1.3T over 10 years. **06-30: figure revised sharply higher within 48 hours — Korea AI mega-plan now $3.1tn (JPM · Jay Kwon via Joshua Meyers)**, ~3.5x the 06-29 framing: Samsung + SK Group jointly announced KRW4,755tn (~US$3.1tn) through 2040 for semis/AI datacenters/robotics, doubling installed Korean DRAM capacity within 5 years, with the same 7-12yr Yongin pull-forward now sized against a much larger capex base; JPM sees a $12-20tn cumulative revenue opportunity for Korean memory makers, and estimates 60-70% of the $3.1tn flows to front-end wafer spend (bullish SPE/power-infra/EPC read-through). Hynix +3% on the news (Rothschild, 06-30). CXMT capacity confirmed insufficient for domestic demand (IPO prospectus), neutralizing the near-term China oversupply concern. **07-01: the stock has not followed the capex-plan news** — SK Hynix -2% (Jefferies) to -3.4% (Rothschild) even as US semi-cap beneficiaries (AMD/AMAT/LRCX/KLAC) hit 12-month highs on the same narrative; JPM's "hyperscaler curse" framing attributes the disconnect to NPS rebalancing and a June DRAM-export-data dip, not a change in fundamentals. Read-through support from Jefferies/KB's Samsung note (Jeff Kim, 07-01): memory shortage 2+ years, DRAM +308%/NAND +256% in 2026, memory's AI-capex share 14%→50% by 2027. **06-29/30:** KIS/@jukan05 details that **SK Hynix's LTA carries a floor with no ceiling** (vs Samsung/Micron), keeping full upside while locking a floor — and that customers, not suppliers, first proposed the 2028+ LTAs (shortage-runs-long signal). Offsetting: a 17-plaintiff DRAM price-fixing class action (N.D. Cal., 06-30) is a new legal overhang, and SemiAnalysis flags CXMT as clearly the No. 4 DRAM player expanding on strong cash flow (low-end competitive creep, not yet a server/HBM threat). **Folded in from two SemiAnalysis deep-dives** (Memory Mania 02-06; Scaling the Memory Wall 08-11, backfilled 2026-06-30): the Rubin HBM supply split (SK hynix ~60% vs Samsung ~30%/Micron ~0%) is the most concrete quantification yet of SK hynix's HBM4/Rubin lead, reinforced by the historical precedent of SK hynix having taken HBM3 share from Samsung outright and its lower-risk 1b-node choice for HBM4. The Hanmi/Hanwha TC-bonding tool dispute (fall-2024 diversification attempt, Apr-2025 Hanmi field-service pullback, incompatible ASMPT tools, placating re-order) is a concrete instance of the page's generic "HBM tool dependency" risk and shows the fragility is real, not hypothetical — though it evidently didn't dent SK hynix's 2025-26 output or share. **07-07:** UBS's SpaceX/SPCX-initiation "Terafab" note adds a long-dated competitive read-through — Terafab explicitly targets memory, and while UBS sees limited incentive for incumbents to license IP, a new at-scale memory supplier (if successful) could pressure Korean incumbents and prompt Korean US front-end (DRAM) investment; a monitor item, not a near-term thesis-mover. **07-08:** Morgan Stanley (S&T) — the ADR listing catalyst previously flagged with a date now has full mechanics: ~**US$28.2bn / ~2.5% TSO**, 1 ADR = 0.1 common share, **trades 07-10**, with MS expecting a **~5-10% ADR premium** to the Seoul line (below TSMC's ~18%), one-way fungibility (the ~2.5% headroom + SK Square's ≥20% floor cap further primary issuance), and index inflows led by **SMH ~$3.8bn** / MSCI ~$1.9bn (Nasdaq-100 near-term inclusion unlikely on the ~$30bn ADR mcap). The ~$28.2bn size is consistent with Jefferies' ~$29bn (07-07) — a rounding/pricing-window difference, not a supersession; net-net the S&T note quantifies the flow/premium mechanics behind the "buy-ADR/sell-Seoul" trade the desks had been recommending, but is technical listing color rather than a fundamental thesis-mover. **07-08/07-09:** into the ADR debut, demand signals stayed firm — @illyquid reports the **$28bn ADR bookbuild closing Wednesday US time with the book already covered multiple times** (MS flagged its "SKHY ADR FAQ" as helpful), corroborating Reuters' >7x oversubscription, and JEF (Madeleine Falkiner) included Hynix in its EU/Asia semis bus tours. Industry pricing/demand corroboration held: Bernstein's Memory tracker (Newman & Li, 07-08 — mild MoM contract-price rises, major 2QCY26 increase still indicated, server demand absorbing supply vs some consumer demand destruction), Goldman's ~$1T 2027 hyperscaler capex (~40-50% to memory, @ParadisLabs), @QQ_Timmy's ~50%-over-two-quarters move (AI broadening HBM→general DRAM/NAND) and BofA's HBM $/rack ~quintupling Blackwell→Rubin (via @firstadopter); Newman also published a Memory-LTA deep dive + structure call (07-09). Offsetting: CXMT's ~CNY29.5bn (~US$4.1bn) STAR Market IPO (China's biggest 2026 IPO) funds 17nm/DDR5/HBM with HBM3 mass production targeted end-2026 (still in qual — @zephyr_z9) — low-end competitive creep now reaching into HBM3. **07-10: the ADR listing catalyst RESOLVED — SKHY priced at $149, raising ~US$26.5bn**, the largest-ever first-time share sale by a foreign issuer on US markets (past Alibaba), 7x oversubscribed with anchor allocations cut on demand and a ~3% ADR premium (Reuters/Bloomberg/FT). The realized ~$26.5bn came slightly below the ~$28-29bn marketing size, and the ~3% premium landed below the ~5-10% MS range / 15-20% some desks flagged — but the record-setting foreign-IPO demand validates the US-investor bid thesis; Cramer expects sell-side number-raises Monday. Offsetting on the same day: Bloomberg profiled CXMT building a top-to-bottom China memory supply chain to challenge the big three (structural low-end threat, long-dated). **Two primary-source management datapoints now anchor the durability leg:** the **SK Hynix ADS roadshow (07-06)** put management's own words on the LTA re-rating thesis — LTAs are margin-focused (explicitly *not* Micron's floor-price approach), 3-5yr with penalty provisions (vs old unenforceable 1yr gentleman's agreements) → lower volatility → higher P/E; >50% of 2028-30 capacity to be LTA-covered (signing before year-end 2026); proceeds fund Cheongju (₩19tn/PNT7) + Yongin (₩21tn) + EUV, with a ₩100tn cash target and a Q3-call shareholder-return measure; total wafer cap ~2x by 2030 but customer demand bigger; HBM-DDR margin parity explicitly NOT committed; CXMT ~20% wafer / only ~6-7% bits. The **Chairman Chey interview (06-10)** framed the AI cycle as a structural break ("not cyclical anymore" because customers proposed the LTAs), with the TSMC-base-die partnership and "doesn't compete with customers" as durable moats, a 5-year capacity double (customers want 4-6x), and SK Group's ~$1T/10yr AI-DC plan (~15GW Korea + ~5GW ex-Korea, ~$35B already in US). Against these, Bernstein's "Memory LTA effectiveness" note (07-09) is the skeptic's bound — the ~$3.33B industry deposit secured so far is <1% (~0.6%) of what an LTA book would need to protect, and coverage tops out at ~50-67% of the market — expecting SK Hynix/Samsung to announce *more* deposits but doubting they reach a level that "de-risks" for investors. **07-14:** Mirae Asset (Young-gun Kim, "Take a broader view") maintains Buy and **the street-high TP W4,200,000** (tying Jefferies) even while cutting 2026/27 earnings ~12% — 2026F OP W266,817bn lands just *below* consensus W275,793bn — framing the pullback as an attractive entry with the 2Q26/ADR-optimism unwind already priced in; the note reinforces the durability/re-rating leg (LTAs ~50% of revenue, DRAM spot up 40+ straight days) while widening the page's PT dispersion to ~W2m (W4.2m Mirae/Jefferies high vs JPM W2.221m low). _(Also corrected this run: the Bernstein 07-06 SK Hynix PT is KRW 3,300,000, not the stale body-figure 1,150,000 previously logged — see Changelog.)_ **07-13→15: a sharp ASP-miss selloff, quickly rebutted and reversed.** The KIS/local 2Q26 OP-preview (W60.4tn, ~8% below the W65tn consensus — the miss a *mix* effect, higher HBM revenue mix → lower blended ASP hike, not weakness) triggered SK Hynix's **biggest one-day drop on record in Seoul** (Bloomberg, "deepening Korea selloff"; @zerohedge -4.5%; @citrini's W1,850 technical line) — but the fundamental case held: **BofA (Simon Woo, 07-13, "DRAM ASPs look better than concerns")** called the correction overdone (conventional DRAM ASP +50-60% QoQ, Korea exports +193% YoY first-10-days-July, DRAM spot rallying since June), framing Korea's soft 2Q ASP as a 2Q-specific base effect rather than a 3Q trend, and the stock **rebounded ~+27% the next session to near all-time highs as the US ADR premium reasserted** (Barron's/@firstadopter/The_AI_Investor; @JulianKlymochko "$1tn company trading like a meme stonk," 3.8x fwd; @jimcramer "how did all of tech become hostage to SK Hynix"). Net-net the whipsaw was flow/ETF/sentiment-driven, not a thesis change. On the durability/supply leg, **Ray Wang (SemiAnalysis, on CNBC via @QQ_Timmy, 07-14)** sharpened the share-loss rebuttal — in a fully-sold-out HBM market, Samsung gaining share need not come at Hynix's expense because the bottleneck is **wafer capacity, not demand** — and SK hynix put concrete execution behind the capacity pull-forward: **Yongin Y1 equipment orders begun with the Phase-1 pilot line pulled forward to Feb-2027** and price-negotiations moved up a quarter (@jukan05, 07-14; @trendforce: supply gap could last beyond 2030). The residual bear item is long-dated China capacity (@EugeneNg/@zephyr_z9, 07-15: 2030E DRAM capacity — big-3 ~2x, CXMT ~3x, all-China ~4x). On the ADR, arbitrage between the Korean line and Nasdaq SKHY is "malfunctioning" — **two-way conversion only opens after the July-29 domestic listing** of the ADR-backing shares (@jukan05/KSD, 07-14), a technical explanation for the premium dislocation. Jefferies (Cristina Titu, 07-15) reiterates BUY on both Korean memory names. _(KIS 07-13 and Mirae 07-14 were already ingested on the 2026-07-14 run — not re-added.)_ **Added this run:** the positioning mechanics behind the 07-13 washout now carry named sources — **Citi "Dr Mo" (Apabhai, 07-13)** sized ~$3bn of leveraged-ETF Hynix selling into the close on a report of a 23-Jul earnings miss, and the **Goldman-panel PSI (07-13)** logged the 13-day foreign KOSPI sell streak / 46.6% SEC foreign-ownership (2009 low) / $29bn SKHY ADR overhang — both flow/positioning, not a fundamental change. **Didier's ASML post-2Q call (07-15)** adds an EUV read-through (SK Hynix a rising EUV buyer, Korean memory capex in ASML's backlog). And the **Wells Fargo CXMT deep-dive (07-14)** puts hard numbers (~8% DRAM rev share, HBM 2-4yr behind) on the low-end China threat, consistent with the roadshow's "CXMT ~20% wafer / ~6-7% bits." **07-24 (SemiAnalysis, AMD Advancing AI 2026) — the run's one genuinely new axis, and it is a *content* and *binning* axis, not a price axis.** On price the note is confirmatory but from an unusually valuable seat — the *buyer's* bill of materials: SemiAnalysis expects "a significant increase in HBM pricing in 2027 as memory suppliers move to match the margins they now earn on commodity DRAM," and models it as strong enough that AMD cannot pass it all through and takes a gross-margin hit on the MI455X package. That is a third-party estimate of HBM pricing power sufficient to move a customer's GM — the most direct external support yet for management's refusal (roadshow 07-06) to commit to HBM-to-DDR margin parity, and consistent with the commodity-DRAM-is-more-profitable inversion already on the page (Redburn's 60→50bn Gb-eq HBM-2027 cut). What is new is the offset: the same BOM shows MI455X DRAM content collapsing from an original 2025 estimate of **138TB across LPDDR5X and DDR5 MRDIMM** to a far lower DDR5 RDIMM figure with **LPDDR5X removed entirely** (the up-to-1TB per-accelerator direct-attached LPDDR tier simply gone on tight supply), summarised as "much of the DRAM price increases are offset by aggressive de-specs"; the custom parts are worse — **Meta's MI450/MI455 cuts HBM from 12 stacks to 6 and 12-Hi to 8-Hi**, at one of AMD's two largest GPU customers, on a program the same house says will "nuke AMD's volume at Meta" (SemiAnalysis 07-21/07-24). The marginal risk to 2027 memory revenue has therefore moved from *price* to *bits per accelerator*, and it bites hardest on the ~33-50% of the market the LTA book does not cover (Bernstein's coverage ceiling, 07-09). On differentiation, the **HBM4 pin-speed bake-off** (AMD 7.6 Gbps / 12 stacks / 432GB / 23.3 TB/s vs Rubin 10.7 Gbps / 8 stacks / 288GB / 22 TB/s, Nvidia having pushed above the JEDEC spec deliberately to close the bandwidth gap to AMD, forcing suppliers to rework HBM4 and requiring "a much higher-quality bin") makes HBM4 **two markets rather than one** — shifting the competitive question from wafer capacity toward **yield on the premium bin**, which is a genuinely different lens on this page's core HBM4-allocation swing factor. Two smaller but real reads: the note states the Rubin HBM4 upstream-output delay is now **resolved**, and it juxtaposes usefully with the page's existing "SK hynix HBM4 ES at ~10 Gbps, lower jitter than Samsung" datapoint — though SemiAnalysis names no bin winner and no such claim is made here. Net: price signal ✓ confirms, content and binning signals ⚠ new; no house rating, PT or estimate superseded.

- **2026-07-27** — **/wiki-ingest (Outlook, into the 7/28 AMC print; MS group callback with Shawn Kim 7/30). 4 intra-quarter rows. Nothing superseded.** **The weekend's headline is the NVDA / SK Group $500B+ initiative, and this page carries it with its verification status attached rather than as a booked number.** **What was announced: an [[SKHYNIX]] partnership to SECURE NEXT-GEN MEMORY SUPPLY FOR NVDA AND JOINTLY DEVELOP HBM; SK Telecom building a 2 GW AI datacentre on NVDA Vera Rubin with the FIRST FACILITY ONLINE IN 2027; SK ramping to 15 GW BY 2035; and NVDA investing $1B in Naver.** UBS sizes the SKT leg at **~$70-80B of NVDA revenue** on Vera Rubin plus SK hynix HBM4. **The verification, and it materially changes how much weight the headline carries: The Information reports that NVDA AND SK HAVE ONLY SIGNED LETTERS OF INTENT — 'letters of intent are not worth the paper they're written on' — with NVDA's own precedent being an [[OPENAI]] LOI last September for a 'landmark strategic partnership' that was ABANDONED A FEW MONTHS LATER. Further, the Friday release was 'MOSTLY A REPRISE OF TWO OTHER ANNOUNCEMENTS FROM EARLY JUNE' (the SKT gigawatt-scale AI Cloud on DSX, and the NVDA/SK hynix memory partnership) — and 'the latest update DOUBLED THE SCALE OF THE AI CLOUD IN KOREA TO 2 GIGAWATTS', which is the only genuinely new element. On the $500B itself, 'as to details… such as WHICH COMPANY IS PUTTING UP HOW MUCH, Nvidia isn't saying.'** Bernstein independently confirms the LOI status and that the $500B was announced earlier in the month. **NVDA fell 5% Monday on the headline.** **The strategic nuance worth carrying, from UBS and it cuts against reading this purely as a win: 'we expect NVDA to work even more closely with the memory supply chain to secure sufficient volumes and, TO SOME EXTENT, MAINTAIN ITS ABILITY TO LOCK IN PRICING ON MORE FAVORABLE TERMS — a key factor for NVDA to maintain ~75% gross margin over the long term.'** In other words the same agreement that guarantees SK hynix volume is described by the sell-side as a **pricing-leverage mechanism for the buyer** — which is the standing tension on this page between LTA volume security and the ASP melt-up. Context: memory revenue consensus of **$1.3T in '27/'28**, and this is the third $100B+ Korea-linked announcement of the weekend alongside [[AVGO]]/[[SAMSUNG]] $200B+. Cross-filed [[NVDA]], [[SAMSUNG]], [[AVGO]], [[OPENAI]], [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md).
- **2026-07-26 (/wiki-ingest — Outlook, weekend batch into the 7/28 AMC print). 3 intra-quarter rows. Nothing superseded.** **(1) The China-supply question got its first proper sell-side anchor: Nomura initiated CXMT (688825 CH) at Buy, TP CNY116, forecasting its global DRAM share going ~10% → ~18% by end-2028F on 40-45% bit-CAGR expansion — which independently corroborates the 17%-of-DRAM-supply-by-YE28 figure Susquehanna put on this page on 07-25, from a house that is long the stock.** Two bears agreeing is weak evidence; a bull and a bear agreeing on the supply number is much stronger, and that is the reason to carry it. CXMT **listed in Shanghai 7/27** at a debut valuation BofA's desk framed as **15-20x FY27 P/E → ~RMB5T (bull 30x → ~RMB9T)** — a multiple of SK hynix's own market cap on roughly 10% share, i.e. **the threat is cost of capital, not technology.** The gate is export control (**MATCH Act**; CXMT equipment localisation ~40%; worst case its **Shanghai capacity — 30-40% of total by 2028F — pushed out**). **(2) The same note contains the strongest structural demand statement now on this page, and it also comes from the competitor's analyst: global memory usage up more than SEVENFOLD over 2026-30F (60%+ bit CAGR) "even after accounting for potential adoption of memory-efficiency technologies (discounting memory usage by 4x)," against industry bit expansion of only 30-40% CAGR — "that could still be insufficient."** A 4x efficiency haircut and demand still outruns supply. **(3) Positioning is genuinely contradictory across desks and is left that way, because the disagreement is the information: MS Prime has HFs recovering ~50% of the capital unwound through June with the Global Memory (MSXXGMEM) L/S ratio at ~7x vs peaks >20x — i.e. re-engaged but nowhere near crowded — and MS's EU client poll has 61% saying now is the time to add to memory longs (Semis the preferred 2H26 TMT sector at 57%). JPM/Schlegel, on a long-term lookback, has global semis at the 98th %ile on both net exposure and L/S ratios (12m easing to 81st %ile). And Jefferies' own desk flow says "memory our most sold sector" on Friday.** The likely reconciliation is the lookback window (MS is comparing to 2026 peaks, JPM to history) but it is not reconciled here. Against all of it, memory was the best-performing sub-sector on the tape last week (**MU +8%, WDC +9%, STX +8%, SNDK +6%**) while the **SOX sat -19% off its June high** — the pricing checks (**+15-20% m/m July PC DRAM contract, implying +30-40% q/q vs TrendForce's +13-18%**) are being paid for even as the sector derates. Cross-filed [[MU]], [[SAMSUNG]], [[SNDK]], [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md), [themes/china-export](themes/china-export.md).
- **2026-07-25 (/wiki-ingest — Outlook). 2 intra-quarter rows; additive, nothing superseded. First Susquehanna mark carried on this page (Mehdi Hosseini, Neutral, ₩1,781,000).** The material addition is **"US Negotiating Leverage Bears Fruit"** (Technology Hardware weekly, 2026-07-25), which supplies a **policy-driven bear case on memory pricing that is independent of demand** — worth carrying precisely because the page's whole debate has been demand/ASP durability vs multiple. Hosseini's chain: Korean suppliers derive **>50% of revenue from US-based customers**; **CXMT and YMTC are barred from US OEMs** and sit on the **Pentagon's Section 1260H list** (YMTC also on the Commerce **Entity List**); Washington can therefore trade access for US fab commitments, and Korean makers are **expected to announce major US memory-fab investments** (Korean president in Silicon Valley; Tom's Hardware, 07-23). **Net-new sizing: CXMT and YMTC at 17% and 25% of DRAM and NAND supply respectively by YE28.** The kicker: **US greenfield memory fabs cannot come online before 2029**, so in the interim unlocking Chinese supply is the only fast remedy to tightness — "killing two birds with one stone." Second row logs the **conflicting Intel-Ohio reporting** (TrendForce: SK hynix eyed as a potential *operating partner*; BusinessKorea: SK Hynix denies a deal), which matches the 07-23 INTC read that the acquisition rumour is dead but collaboration paths are live — recorded as unresolved, and cross-referenced to [INTC.md](INTC.md). Also logged **Nanya's ~US$800bn 2026 memory-market call (≈half of all semiconductor revenue)** as an upper-bound marker on how far the industry is extrapolating this cycle. - **2026-07-24 (/run-inbox — SemiAnalysis Advancing AI 2026 + 1 dedup pass; all additive, nothing superseded).**
  - **NET-NEW — SemiAnalysis "Can AMD break the CUDA Moat? AMD Advancing AI 2026" (2026-07-24).** The genuine route to this page. Added in four places: **(1) Current state** — the **HBM4 pin-speed bake-off** bullet: AMD MI455X **12 stacks / 432GB / 23.3 TB/s at 7.6 Gbps** vs Nvidia Rubin and Google at **8 stacks / 288GB**, Rubin **22 TB/s at 10.7 Gbps (40% faster)** — a near-tie despite AMD's 50% wider bus, because "Nvidia aggressively raised its targeted HBM4 pin speeds last year, well above the original JEDEC HBM4 specification… precisely driven by the need to make up for the memory bandwidth deficit to AMD MI455X," a change that "was challenging for memory suppliers to keep up with. Memory suppliers had to rework their HBM4 to deliver the spec Nvidia was targeting. This also pushed out upstream output for Rubin but **as of today these issues have been finally resolved**," and which means **Nvidia must use a much higher-quality bin than AMD**. Framed as a **two-tier HBM4 market** (easy 7.6 Gbps AMD bin vs premium 10.7 Gbps Nvidia bin) → the competitive question shifts from wafer capacity toward **yield on the top bin**, widening the quality/price spread between the three suppliers. Juxtaposed (explicitly flagged as a juxtaposition, not a claim) against the page's existing "SK hynix HBM4 ES ~10 Gbps, lower jitter / stronger package execution than Samsung's" datapoint from "Memory Mania" (02-06). **No bin winner asserted — the source names none.** **(2) Debate / thesis** — a **price-up/content-down** bullet: expects "a significant increase in HBM pricing in 2027 as memory suppliers move to match the margins they now earn on commodity DRAM," modelled as strong enough that AMD cannot pass it all through ("at a lower incremental margin than their corporate margins, dragging down gross margins on the MI455X package") — direct external support for management's refusal (roadshow 07-06) to commit to HBM-DDR margin parity — set against the offsetting de-spec: original 2025 MI455X BOM of **138TB DRAM across LPDDR5X + DDR5 MRDIMM**, now with **LPDDR5X entirely removed** (the roadmapped **up-to-1TB per-accelerator direct-attached LPDDR** tier gone on tight memory supply) plus a significant DDR5 RDIMM de-spec: "much of the DRAM price increases are offset by aggressive de-specs." Baseline noted: "HBM, DRAM, and NAND prices alike have increased dramatically since our baseline in mid-2025." **(3) Risks** — two new bullets: **de-spec / content destruction** (bits per accelerator, not price, is the exposed variable; bites hardest on the ~33-50% of the market the LTA book does not cover per Bernstein 07-09) and **HBM4 top-bin qualification** (risk concentrated in premium-bin yield rather than wafer output). **(4)** 1 Intra-quarter Full-log row (07-24, produto/mixed), an augmented **HBM demand** Signal cell (now **✓ confirms demand / ⚠ new binning nuance**), a synthesis addendum, and a Sources entry.
